#+TITLE: Architecture Decision Records
#+AUTHOR: James Cook
#+STARTUP: overview
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       docs-decisions
:END:

One heading per decision. Every decision carries a revisit condition, not just a
date — a date alone doesn't tell you *why* to reopen something.

* ADR-001: Upgrade Doom core independently of the AI subsystem
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-doom-core-upgrade
:STATUS:   Accepted
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Accepted"    from "Proposed"    [2026-07-20 Mon]
:END:

** Context
Doom core (=~/.config/emacs=) was 6.5 months behind =doomemacs/doomemacs= master
(=3e15fb36d=, 2026-01-07) at the start of this project. The user asked to upgrade
before any AI package work, matching the project's own constraint against mixing
upgrade risk with feature work.

** Decision drivers
- Constraint: "do not mix upgrade failures with AI package changes"
- Constraint: separate Doom upgrade from any Emacs upgrade
- gptel and copilot.el both had substantial upstream progress worth having before
  reconfiguring them (Claude Sonnet 5 support, BYOK, tagged releases)

** Candidates
Only one real candidate: run =doom upgrade= (=git pull --rebase= + =doom sync -u=)
without touching the installed Emacs binary (30.1, already within Doom's supported
27.1–30.2 range).

** Evidence
=doom info= (2026-07-20) confirmed the stale core revision and a clean =doom
doctor= baseline before touching anything.

** Decision
Ran =doom upgrade -!=, hit one transient upstream problem (see Consequences), fixed
it, then =doom build -!= to force full recompilation, verified with =doom doctor=.

** Consequences
- Doom core: =3e15fb36d= → =04b2956ba=. Doom's own reported version string went
  =3.0.0-pre= → =2.2.0=; this reads like a regression but isn't — Doom's own
  version file changed between those revisions upstream, unrelated to anything
  local. Not investigated further since =doom doctor= is clean either way.
- Doom's module tree was split into a separate =doomemacs/modules= source
  (fetched to =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/=), tracked independently.
  =:config default= (used by this config) still exists there intact — no action
  needed.
- gptel =fd876e6= → =8701e2b= (0.9.9.3 → 0.9.9.5): now includes Claude Sonnet 5
  support in =gptel-anthropic=, directly relevant to [[id:ai-providers][the Phase 4 provider rework]].
- copilot.el =59a4a29= → =277ca35= (unreleased → tagged 0.9.0): gained BYOK model
  support and a chat/compose interface — worth a second look during Phase 5,
  since it changes copilot.el's role from pure completion toward also-a-chat-tool.
- Upstream incident: =purcell/package-lint= (a transitive dev-dependency pulled in
  by =use-package='s lint feature, not anything this config declares) renamed its
  default branch =master= → =main=. straight's local mirror was still pinned to
  the now-nonexistent =refs/heads/master=, failing the sync. Fixed by deleting the
  clean, uncommitted local mirror at
  =~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/package-lint= and re-running sync so
  straight re-cloned against the current default branch.
- 148 "stale =.elc=" warnings appeared after the =git pull --rebase= (an mtime
  artifact of checkout, not real breakage) — cleared to 0 via =doom build= plus one
  direct =byte-compile-file= on the single holdout (=annalist.elc=).
- =init.el='s =(:ui doom-dashboard)= module name was flagged by Doom as moved to
  =(:ui dashboard)= in 2.1; renamed directly (trivial, see commit diff on
  =init.el=), re-synced, warning gone.

** Rejected alternatives
- Upgrading Emacs itself in the same pass — rejected per the project's own
  constraint; Emacs 30.1 is still within Doom's supported/recommended range.
- Ignoring the stale =.elc= warnings as harmless — rejected; conventions require
  running diagnostics after changes and not hiding warnings to make checks pass.

** Validation
=doom info= (43 modules, 141 packages), =doom doctor= (0 warnings), =git status=
clean on both the Doom core repo and this config repo except the intended
=packages.el=/=init.el= diffs.

** Rollback
=cd ~/.config/emacs && git reset --hard 3e15fb36d && doom sync -u=. Not recommended
— current state passes all checks.

** Revisit when
Doom's =3.0.0= final ships, or the =:doom compat= shim (hardcoded in
=doom-modules.el=, "here to stay... won't be hardcoded after v3") is removed
upstream — check whether =:config default +bindings +smartparens= is still the
correct declaration at that point.

* ADR-002: Remove the unused EXWM package declaration
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-remove-exwm
:STATUS:   Accepted
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:

** Context
=packages.el= declared =(package! exwm)= but =init.el= never loaded EXWM as a
module, and the entire EXWM configuration block in =config.el= was commented out.
Pure install footprint for a capability explicitly out of scope for Phase 1.

** Decision drivers
- Constraint: "do not activate or redesign EXWM in Phase 1"
- User preference, given directly: remove it for now rather than leave it declared

** Candidates
Keep declared (placeholder for later) vs. remove now (re-declare when the EXWM
phase starts).

** Evidence
=packages.el:51= (pre-upgrade), =config.el= EXWM block fully commented out,
confirmed zero references from any loaded module.

** Decision
Removed =(package! exwm)= from =packages.el=; =doom sync= dropped it and its
exclusive dependencies (147 → 141 packages detected).

** Consequences
Smaller =doom sync= surface, one fewer package to carry through future upgrades
until it's actually wanted.

** Rejected alternatives
Leaving it declared as a placeholder — rejected per explicit user preference.

** Validation
=doom doctor= clean post-removal; =git diff packages.el= shows exactly the one
line removed.

** Rollback
=(package! exwm)= back into =packages.el=, =doom sync=.

** Revisit when
The EXWM/window-manager phase of the long-term roadmap actually starts (see
[[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]).

* ADR-003: =SPC a= becomes the AI namespace outright
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-ai-leader-namespace
:STATUS:   Superseded by ADR-028 [2026-08-23]
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Superseded" from "Implemented" [2026-08-23 Sat] — [[id:adr-llm-module-owns-ai-keys][ADR-028]] retires
  the =SPC a= namespace in favour of =:tools llm='s own =SPC o l= map and
  restores =embark-act= at =SPC a=. The discoverability driver below survives
  intact; only the chosen prefix changed.
- State "Implemented" from "Accepted"  [2026-07-20 Mon] — Phase 4
:END:

** Context
=config.el= previously unbound Doom's default =SPC a= (bound to =embark-act= by
Doom's default bindings module) and repurposed it as an "apps" prefix with AI
nested a level deeper at =SPC a i=. Nothing else occupied =SPC a=, so the extra
"apps" layer added a level of nesting for no benefit.

*Correction found during Phase 4 implementation*: that reclamation code was
wrapped in =(after! doom-keybinds ...)=, and =doom-keybinds= was never a real
Emacs feature anywhere in Doom core or the default module (confirmed by
grepping the full Doom core and module sources for =provide 'doom-keybinds=:
zero matches). =after!= on an unrecognized feature never fires — so this block
was dead code that never ran. Embark's default =SPC a= binding was, in
practice, still live the whole time, and the old =SPC a i= AI commands were
never reachable via keybinding at all (only via =M-x=). See
[[id:tshoot-dead-keybind-code][troubleshooting.org]] for the full writeup.

** Decision drivers
- Discoverability/shallowness requirement for frequently-used AI actions
- User confirmed no need to preserve Embark's leader shortcut

** Candidates
1. Full =SPC a= = AI, flattened (chosen)
2. Keep the "apps" layer, AI under =SPC a i=
3. A fresh unused prefix (e.g. =SPC A=) to avoid touching Embark's binding at all

** Evidence
=config.el:332-347= (existing reclamation code); confirmed via user answer that
Embark's leader shortcut is not needed.

** Decision
=SPC a= is the AI namespace directly (chat/context/rewrite/model-menu/terminal for
now; agent/review keys land in Phase 6), no intermediate "apps" layer. Implemented
as plain =(map! :leader "a" nil)= followed by a real =(map! :leader (:prefix ("a" .
"AI") ...))= block — no =after!= guard needed, since =config.el= loads after
Doom's default bindings are already active. See [[id:ai-keybindings][keybindings.org]] for the concrete,
now-implemented map.

** Consequences
Embark's default leader-key access is gone (confirmed live: =SPC a= no longer
resolves to =embark-act=) — accepted tradeoff, user-confirmed. Unlike the
original code, this is now actually in effect, not dormant.

** Rejected alternatives
Option 3 (fresh prefix) — rejected, since the user does not need Embark on the
leader at all, so there's no reason to avoid the conflict.

** Validation
Verified live via a headless Doom Emacs daemon (isolated =--socket-name=,
separate from the user's own running session): =(lookup-key doom-leader-map (kbd
"a i"))= etc. resolve to the correct commands, and =(lookup-key doom-leader-map
(kbd "a"))= no longer resolves to =embark-act=. Which-key discoverability and
in-editor conflict testing still to be done by the user in their own session.

** Rollback
Revert the =map!= block in =config.el= (see git diff); =embark-act= reasserts
itself at =SPC a= once Doom's default binding is no longer shadowed.

** Revisit when
Any future phase wants to reintroduce Embark or another "apps"-style prefix.

* ADR-004: claude-code-ide.el as the sole primary agentic-coding frontend
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-primary-agent
:STATUS:   Accepted, implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:

** Context
The user already has Claude Code CLI 2.1.215 installed and on PATH, with no
Anthropic credential yet configured for anything else. Two serious agentic-coding
frontends were evaluated: =claude-code-ide.el= (deep MCP integration with the
existing CLI) and =agent-shell= (protocol-neutral ACP client, works with multiple
agent backends but requires a separate bridge package rather than reusing the CLI
session directly).

** Decision drivers
- Constraint: one primary agent, at most one secondary, and only if the secondary
  serves a clearly distinct, frequently-used role
- User confirmed: no anticipated need for other agent backends (Codex, Gemini)

** Candidates
| Candidate | Score (see [[id:docs-decisions][§4 of the original proposal]]) | Fate |
|-----------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+---------|
| claude-code-ide.el | ~72 | Accepted, primary |
| agent-shell        | ~70 | Rejected, see [[id:adr-agent-shell-rejected][ADR-005]] |
| Aidermacs          | ~58 | Rejected — no distinct role beyond what claude-code-ide.el covers |

** Evidence
GitHub =manzaltu/claude-code-ide.el= (fetched 2026-07-20): Emacs 28.1+, drives the
installed =claude= CLI over MCP, vterm/eat/ghostel backends (vterm matches this
config's incumbent terminal module), Ediff-based diff review, MCP tools exposing
=xref=, =imenu=, =treesit-info=, =project-info= back to the agent. ~1.6k stars, 115
commits, no tagged release yet, 58 open issues at time of check.

** Decision
=claude-code-ide.el= is the one and only agentic-coding frontend for Phase 1.

** Consequences
Deepest available integration with a CLI already installed and (presumably)
already paid for. No second bridge package, no second credential/login flow to
maintain.

** Rejected alternatives
See [[id:adr-agent-shell-rejected][ADR-005]] for agent-shell specifically. Aidermacs rejected: Aider is a credible
tool, but adding it alongside claude-code-ide.el would need a distinct
justification (e.g. wanting a second model family workflow) that doesn't currently
exist — "Aider is popular" is explicitly not sufficient per this project's own
anti-goals.

** Validation
Phase 6 complete ([[id:project-root][PROJECT.org]]): package installed, CLI detection verified,
tree-sitter MCP tool confirmed working on this Emacs 30.1 build, and a full
live workflow (plan → edit → test → diff → no commit) run in the user's own
session against a disposable scratch project — seeded bug found and fixed,
tests run, no git actions taken, clean teardown. Only the Remote/TRAMP
sub-item remains deferred, for lack of hardware, not lack of implementation.
Acceptance criteria in [[id:ai-evaluation][evaluation.org]].

** Rollback
Package removal via =packages.el= + =doom sync= if it doesn't work out; nothing
else in the config depends on it.

** Revisit when
- claude-code-ide.el stalls (no tagged release after an extended period, issues pile
  up unaddressed), or
- the user's own needs change and a second agent backend becomes genuinely
  necessary (see ADR-005's revisit condition, which is the same trigger).

* ADR-005: agent-shell rejected as a secondary agent (for now)
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-agent-shell-rejected
:STATUS:   Accepted
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:

** Context
agent-shell (=xenodium/agent-shell= + =acp.el=) offers protocol-neutral access to
many agent backends (Claude Agent, Codex, Gemini CLI, and others) via the Agent
Client Protocol. This is a legitimate secondary-agent justification in the
abstract ("a provider-neutral protocol client used to compare or switch
backends" is explicitly named as legitimate in this project's own selection
framework) — but only if it will actually be used regularly.

** Decision drivers
User confirmed directly: "I don't anticipate using other agent backends right
now."

** Candidates
Add now as secondary vs. reject now, revisit later. Given the direct answer,
this is not a scored comparison — it's a preference match.

** Evidence
GitHub =xenodium/agent-shell= (fetched 2026-07-20): requires
=@agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp= as a separate global npm install to reach
Claude specifically — it does not reuse the existing =claude= CLI session/login
the way claude-code-ide.el does. Active development (1.6k stars, 114 releases),
documented Evil rebinding pattern.

** Decision
Rejected for Phase 1. Not installed.

** Consequences
One fewer package, one fewer credential/bridge surface to maintain. If the user
later wants Codex or Gemini CLI access from Emacs, this is where to start.

** Rejected alternatives
N/A — this entry documents the rejection itself.

** Validation
N/A — nothing installed.

** Rollback
N/A.

** Revisit when
The user actually wants to use a second agent backend (Codex, Gemini CLI, etc.)
regularly — at that point, re-run the comparison in the original Phase 1 proposal
with fresh evidence, since both agent-shell and its ecosystem move quickly.

* ADR-006: gptel credential migration and multi-provider rework
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-gptel-rework
:STATUS:   Implemented
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Implemented" from "Proposed"  [2026-07-20 Mon] — Phase 4
:END:

** Context
=gptel= was configured OpenAI-only, with a hardcoded 2024-era model list
(=gpt-4o-mini=, =gpt-4o=) and credentials via a hand-rolled =my/pass-get= shell
wrapper (=shell-command-to-string= with string-interpolated =pass= calls). User
confirmed: Anthropic (new) + OpenAI (existing) only, Google explicitly skipped
for now.

** Decision drivers
- [[id:std-credentials][standards.org]]: credentials via Doom-native mechanisms, not shell wrappers
- [[id:std-cloud-first][standards.org]]: frontier models, not whichever was configured two years ago
- User-provided =api/anthropic= pass entry (values never seen by the agent —
  only entry names and, during validation, string length)

** Candidates
Considered hardcoding a current model list per backend vs. omitting =:models=
entirely. Inspected =gptel-anthropic.el=/=gptel-openai.el= source directly
(post-[[id:adr-doom-core-upgrade][upgrade]], version 0.9.9.5): both =gptel-make-anthropic= and
=gptel-make-openai= default their =:models= keyword argument to gptel's own
maintained registry (=gptel--anthropic-models=/=gptel--openai-models=) when the
keyword is omitted.

** Evidence
- =gptel--anthropic-models= (checked 2026-07-20) already includes
  =claude-sonnet-5=, =claude-opus-4-8=, =claude-haiku-4-5-20251001=, =claude-fable-5=,
  and older models, each with cost/context-window/capability metadata.
- =gptel--openai-models= likewise includes the current =gpt-5.x=/=o3=/=o4=
  generation.
- Doom's =:tools pass= module (=+auth= flag) exposes =+pass-get-secret=, doing
  exactly what =my/pass-get= tried to do, safely (source-verified at
  =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/tools/pass/autoload/pass.el=).
- =gptel-rewrite= (built-in, ediff/diff-based) and =gptel-add=/=gptel-add-file=
  (built-in, DWIM region/buffer/dired-marked-files context) already cover what
  the custom =my/gptel-rewrite-*= and a planned custom "add project context"
  wrapper would have reimplemented.

** Decision
- Enabled =(pass +auth)= in =init.el=; removed =my/pass-get= and the global
  =setenv "OPENAI_API_KEY"= from =config.el=.
- =gptel-make-anthropic= registered as default backend/model
  (=claude-sonnet-5=), =gptel-make-openai= as a secondary backend — both with
  =:models= omitted so the registry stays current automatically instead of
  being hardcoded here.
- =:key= on both backends is a lambda calling =+pass-get-secret=, never a
  stored value.
- Replaced =my/gptel-rewrite-clearer/-formal=, =my/gptel-summarize=, and
  =my/gptel--prompt-rewrite= with three entries in =gptel-directives=
  (=clearer=, =formal=, =summarize=) plus the built-in =gptel-rewrite= command.
- Replaced =my/gptel-send-buffer= with the built-in =gptel-add= (DWIM context,
  semantically more correct: "add as context" rather than "immediately send
  the whole buffer as the prompt").
- Added =my/gptel-add-diagnostics=, a genuinely new thin wrapper (gptel has no
  Flycheck integration) using =flycheck-overlay-errors-in= to stage the current
  buffer's diagnostics into a dedicated context buffer via
  =gptel-context--add-buffer=.
- Flattened =SPC a= per [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]] in the same pass, since this touched the
  exact code block.

** Consequences
- No hardcoded model names to go stale — the registry updates whenever gptel
  itself is upgraded.
- One fewer category of custom code (rewrite commands) to maintain; built-ins
  get ediff-based in-place review instead of copy-paste-from-scratch-buffer.
- Google/Gemini backend intentionally not configured — see Known gaps below.

** Rejected alternatives
Hardcoding an explicit =:models= list per backend — rejected once source
inspection showed the omit-and-inherit-registry approach is both less code and
more correct.

** Validation
Verified live via an isolated headless Doom daemon (=--socket-name=, distinct
from the user's own running Emacs session, killed afterward):
- =gptel-backend= name is "Claude", =gptel-model= is =claude-sonnet-5=
- "ChatGPT" backend registered with the current =gpt-5.x=/=o3=/=o4= model list
- =gptel-directives= contains =clearer=/=formal= entries with the expected text
- =+pass-get-secret= resolves both =api/anthropic= and =api/openai= to
  non-empty strings (length checked, value never printed)
- =my/gptel-add-diagnostics=, =my/project-vterm=, =+pass-get-secret= all bound
- All seven =SPC a= bindings resolve to the correct commands; =SPC a= no
  longer resolves to =embark-act=
- =doom doctor= clean throughout (44 modules, 144 packages)

Not yet verified (needs the user's own interactive session, not a headless
daemon): visual streaming behavior, cancellation, popup placement, Org-buffer
chat UX, network-down behavior. See [[id:ai-evaluation][evaluation.org]].

** Rollback
=git diff init.el config.el= shows the full change; revert both files and
=doom sync= to return to the OpenAI-only, shell-wrapper credential setup.

** Revisit when
- Google/Gemini is wanted later: add =gptel-make-gemini= with
  =+pass-get-secret= on a new =api/gemini= entry (mirrors this ADR exactly).
- gptel changes its default-models mechanism in a future release (check
  =gptel-anthropic.el=/=gptel-openai.el= source before assuming the omit
  pattern still applies).

* Known gaps after Phase 4
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       decisions-phase4-gaps
:END:

- Google/Gemini backend: explicitly skipped per user request, not an oversight.
  See [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]] revisit condition.
- The pre-existing, separately-running Doom Emacs daemon (the user's actual
  day-to-day session, started before this project began) has not reloaded any
  of today's changes — Doom only reads =init.el=/=config.el= at startup. The
  user needs to restart Emacs (or that daemon) to pick up Phase 2–4 changes in
  their live session. Not done automatically — restarting a live session with
  unsaved state is the user's call, not something to do on their behalf.

* ADR-007: keep copilot.el, enable Next Edit Suggestions
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-copilot-nes
:STATUS:   Implemented
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Implemented" from "Proposed"  [2026-07-20 Mon] — Phase 5
:END:

** Context
Phase 1 flagged three open questions for inline completion: which install path
copilot-language-server actually uses given Node 18.19.1, whether =TAB=
precedence against Company/Evil/yasnippet is safe, and whether genuine
multi-location Next Edit Suggestions (not just cursor-position fill-in-the-
-middle) are available with the currently pinned copilot.el (upgraded
=59a4a29= → =277ca35=/0.9.0 in [[id:adr-doom-core-upgrade][ADR-001]]).

** Decision drivers
- Don't guess at runtime keymap behavior when it can be verified
- Don't leave a shipped, compatible capability (NES) unused without evaluating it
- Don't touch the user's own long-running Doom daemon to test any of this

** Candidates
Keep copilot.el as-is (regular completions only) vs. also enable
=copilot-nes-mode=, which ships in the same already-installed package
(=copilot-nes.el=, part of the =:files ("*.el")= recipe).

** Evidence
- *Install path*: the running Copilot server (inspected via =ps=/=/proc= on
  the user's actual long-running daemon, read-only, no interaction with
  Emacs itself) is a two-process chain: =node .../copilot/bin/copilot-language-server
  --stdio= (parent) execs
  =.../node_modules/@github/copilot-language-server-linux-x64/copilot-language-server
  --stdio= (child, the real native LSP implementation). This is the npm
  install path (=copilot-install-server= prefers npm when found on =PATH=,
  confirmed in =copilot.el= source), not the Node-free native-binary-only
  path. Installed package version: 1.452.0. Its =package.json= declares no
  =engines= constraint at all — the earlier Phase 1 concern ("Node 20.8+
  needed for the npm path") does not apply to this package's own stated
  requirements, and the process has been running successfully for hours in
  the user's real session as empirical confirmation.
- *TAB precedence*: =copilot-completion-map= is applied via an overlay
  =keymap= property (=copilot--get-or-create-keymap-overlay=, priority 101),
  not a =minor-mode-map-alist= entry. Per Emacs's documented active-keymap
  search order, overlay =keymap= properties are consulted before
  =emulation-mode-map-alists= (where Evil's state maps live) and before
  ordinary =minor-mode-map-alist= entries (where Company's map lives) — so
  Copilot's =TAB= binding wins deterministically whenever the overlay exists
  at point, and falls through cleanly otherwise. *Verified live* (isolated
  daemon, not the user's session): =TAB= resolved to
  =company-indent-for-tab-command= with no suggestion overlay, to
  =copilot-accept-completion= with a simulated overlay at point (Evil in
  normal state throughout, Company and =copilot-mode= both active), and back
  to =company-indent-for-tab-command= identically after the overlay was
  removed.
- *Snippet interaction*: Doom's =:editor snippets= module (yasnippet) also
  uses an overlay =keymap= for active-field navigation
  (=yas-overlay-priority=, default 100). Copilot's keymap overlay uses
  priority 101 — one higher. *Verified live*: with both overlays
  simultaneously present at the same point, =TAB= resolves to
  =copilot-accept-completion=, confirming Copilot wins by design, not by
  accident, on the (unusual) occasion the two coincide.
- *Next Edit Suggestions*: =copilot-nes-mode= (=copilot-nes.el=) uses
  =textDocument/copilotInlineEdit= to predict edits anywhere in the file
  based on recent edit history — genuine NES, not fill-in-the-middle. Minimum
  required server version =1.434.0=; installed is =1.452.0=, compatible. Its
  keymap uses =:filter=-conditioned bindings (only active when
  =copilot-nes--edit= is non-nil) — *verified live*: with =copilot-nes-mode=
  enabled but no suggestion pending, =TAB= still resolves to
  =company-indent-for-tab-command=, confirming it doesn't unconditionally
  steal the key. Evil doesn't bind =TAB= in any state that matters (only
  =evil-command-line-map=, the Ex minibuffer), so there's no Evil-vs-NES
  conflict to begin with.

** Decision
Kept copilot.el (no replacement — matches the Phase 1 comparison). Removed a
redundant, no-op =:bind= block in =config.el= that manually re-asserted
=copilot-completion-map='s own defaults. Added =copilot-nes-mode= to the same
=prog-mode= hook as =copilot-mode=.

** Consequences
- Genuine Next Edit Suggestions now available, not just cursor-position
  completions — closes the gap the original candidate comparison flagged.
- =TAB= behavior is now a documented, live-verified precedence chain rather
  than an assumption: no-suggestion → Company; Copilot suggestion pending →
  accept; NES pending (and no regular suggestion, since the changelog notes
  the two hide each other) → accept NES edit; snippet field active
  simultaneously with a Copilot suggestion → Copilot wins by one priority
  point, verified live.
- One line of dead config removed (the redundant =:bind= block).

** Rejected alternatives
Leaving NES off "to be safe" — rejected once verification showed the
=:filter=-based keymap can't misfire when idle, and the server-version
requirement is already satisfied.

** Validation
=doom sync=/=doom doctor= clean (44 modules, 144 packages) after the change.
Functional keymap-precedence tests run in two disposable, isolated headless
daemons (distinct =--socket-name= each time), both killed after use — the
user's own long-running daemon was never touched, only observed at the OS
process level (=ps=/=/proc=) for the install-path check.

Not yet done (needs the user's real session): confirm an actual live NES
suggestion appears and can be accepted/dismissed with real Copilot
credentials — the isolated test daemons have no Copilot authentication, so
only the keymap mechanics could be verified this way, not live model output.

** Rollback
Remove =(prog-mode . copilot-nes-mode)= from the =:hook= list in =config.el=;
=copilot-nes-mode= is buffer-local and opt-in, so nothing else depends on it.

** Revisit when
- copilot.el changes its overlay-priority scheme for either
  =copilot-completion-map= or NES (re-check the 101-vs-100 margin against
  yasnippet if so).
- The user wants snippet fields to always win over a simultaneous Copilot
  suggestion — raise =yas-overlay-priority= above 101 (a one-line, low-risk
  change, not implemented since no such preference has been expressed).

* ADR-008: install and configure claude-code-ide.el
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-claude-code-ide-install
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Implemented" from "Accepted"  [2026-07-20 Mon] — Phase 6
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-07-20 Mon] — real smoke
  test run against a disposable scratch project, user-approved
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-primary-agent][ADR-004]] selected claude-code-ide.el as the sole primary agentic-coding
frontend. This phase installs and configures it against the Claude Code CLI
already present on this machine (=/home/james/.local/bin/claude=, v2.1.215).

** Decision drivers
- Reuse the existing CLI/subscription rather than a separate credential path
- vterm backend and ediff-based review to match existing incumbents
- Verify what can be verified safely, without spending the user's Claude
  usage or running an unsupervised agentic session against real files

** Evidence
Re-fetched =manzaltu/claude-code-ide.el= (2026-07-20) for the current
installation recipe and config variables, then read the actual installed
source (=claude-code-ide.el=, =claude-code-ide-transient.el=,
=claude-code-ide-emacs-tools.el=) directly rather than trusting the summary
alone:
- =claude-code-ide-cli-path= defaults to ="claude"=, =claude-code-ide-terminal-backend=
  to ='vterm=, =claude-code-ide-use-ide-diff= to =t= — all already matching
  this project's decisions, set explicitly anyway per [[id:std-doom-first][standards.org]]'s
  spirit of not relying on upstream defaults silently staying put.
- =claude-code-ide-menu= (the transient) already contains session
  management, navigation, interaction, config, and debug submenus —
  including a "Check CLI status" entry (=claude-code-ide-check-status=,
  backed by =claude-code-ide--detect-cli=) that runs a local
  =claude --version= via =call-process= only — no network call, no MCP
  session, no API usage. This made a single leader binding
  (=SPC a g= → =claude-code-ide-menu=) sufficient; no separate "review last
  diff" binding is needed since ediff opens automatically on file changes
  (=claude-code-ide-use-ide-diff= =t=).
- =claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup= (autoloaded) registers the Emacs-side
  MCP tools, including =claude-code-ide-mcp-treesit-info=.

** Decision
- =packages.el=: =(package! claude-code-ide :recipe (:host github :repo
  "manzaltu/claude-code-ide.el"))=.
- =config.el=: =(use-package! claude-code-ide :commands
  (claude-code-ide-menu) :init (setq ...) :config
  (claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup))= — lazy-loaded via =:commands=, matching
  Doom's lazy-loading model and this project's anti-goal against loading
  every AI package eagerly at startup.
- =SPC a g= added to the existing AI leader block.

** Consequences
- Primary agentic-coding entry point exists and is reachable, but has not yet
  run an actual live session — see Validation.
- Discovered, while verifying the tree-sitter MCP tool specifically: Doom's
  =:tools tree-sitter= module's grammar-installation advice is confirmed
  broken on Emacs 30.x, reproducing upstream issue =doomemacs/doomemacs#8578=
  exactly (see [[id:tshoot-treesitter-confirmed][troubleshooting.org]] for the full reproduction). This predates
  this project and is unrelated to the claude-code-ide.el install itself, but
  it does mean the =treesit-info= MCP tool has no working grammars to draw on
  right now — compounded by the separate fact that no tree-sitter grammars
  (for any language) were installed on this machine before this check either.
- CLI-detection claim below narrower than stated at the time: verified in
  disposable test daemons spawned from an interactive shell, which isn't
  equivalent to the user's actual systemd-launched daemon and its own,
  separately-provisioned =PATH=. Found and fixed in Phase 7, see
  [[id:tshoot-daemon-path-systemd][troubleshooting.org]].

** Rejected alternatives
Starting a real, live claude-code-ide session (even against a disposable
scratch directory) as part of this phase's own verification — rejected. That
would consume the user's actual Claude subscription usage, make real network
calls, and run an agent capable of taking real actions, all without the user
watching. Left for the user to trigger themselves, or for a later turn if
they explicitly ask for a supervised smoke test.

** Validation
Verified in an isolated headless daemon (distinct =--socket-name=, killed
after use; the user's own long-running daemon was never touched):
- Package loads, =claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup= and =claude-code-ide-menu=
  both bound
- All three explicit settings (terminal backend, diagnostics backend,
  use-ide-diff) applied correctly
- =SPC a g= resolves to =claude-code-ide-menu=
- =claude-code-ide--detect-cli= (local =call-process=, no network) confirms
  the CLI is found and resolves to the same path as Phase 0's discovery
  (=/home/james/.local/bin/claude=)
- =doom doctor= clean throughout (44 modules, 145 packages)

*Live smoke test performed* (user explicitly approved, real Claude usage,
disposable scratch project under =scratchpad/phase6-smoke-test/=, its own git
repo with one baseline commit so commits could be checked for):
- Discovered and fixed a real prerequisite gap in the process: =vterm='s
  native module (=vterm-module.so=) had never been compiled on this machine.
  First =(require 'vterm)= in a headless context hangs indefinitely — vterm
  asks for interactive confirmation to compile
  (=vterm-always-compile-module= defaults to nil), and that confirmation has
  nowhere to go over =emacsclient --eval=. This would have hit the user's own
  real session too, the first time either =my/project-vterm= or
  claude-code-ide.el touched vterm. Fixed by running vterm's own documented
  build steps directly (=cmake -G 'Unix Makefiles' ..= then =make= in
  =~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-30.1/vterm/build=) — standard
  toolchain already present (=cmake=, =gcc=), output lands in Doom's own
  shared build directory, benefiting every Emacs instance including the
  user's real daemon. See [[id:tshoot-vterm-module][troubleshooting.org]] for the full incident.
- Session launched against the scratch project; workspace-trust dialog
  appeared in the vterm buffer (expected, first run in a new directory) and
  was accepted programmatically (=vterm-send-return=, default option already
  "Yes, I trust this folder"). Session authenticated as the user's real
  Claude Pro account, Sonnet 5, correctly scoped to the scratch directory.
- Task given: write =calc.py= with a *deliberately buggy* =add(a, b)=
  (returning =a - b=), write a pytest test expecting =add(2, 3) == 5=, run
  pytest, fix the bug if it fails, re-run until passing, never touch git, and
  write a =DONE.marker= sentinel on completion.
- Result: =calc.py= ended up correct (=a + b=), =test_calc.py= written
  correctly, =.pytest_cache=/=__pycache__= present confirming pytest actually
  ran, session transcript shows the diff (removed =a - b=, added =a + b=)
  and an explicit "Tests pass" message before creating the marker.
  =git status= showed only untracked files — *no commit, no staging* beyond
  the pre-existing baseline commit. =claude-code-ide-stop= tore the session
  down cleanly, no orphaned process.
- This exercises: launch from correct root, inspect/plan implicitly, multi-file
  edit, run tests, react to a failure and fix it, re-run to confirm, refrain
  from git actions, clean stop. *Not* exercised: interactive interruption
  mid-session, rejecting partial work, remote/TRAMP targets, or the
  =treesit-info= MCP tool specifically (blocked by the separate tree-sitter
  gap above).

** Rollback
Remove the =(package! claude-code-ide ...)= line and the corresponding
=use-package!=/keybinding block, =doom sync=. No other part of the config
depends on it.

** Revisit when
- claude-code-ide.el cuts a tagged release (still pre-1.0 as of this check;
  see [[id:adr-primary-agent][ADR-004]])
- The tree-sitter grammar-install bug is fixed upstream, or the user wants a
  manual workaround (build grammars via the =tree-sitter= CLI directly,
  bypassing Doom's automatic installer, and place the =.so= files in
  =doom-profile-data-dir "tree-sitter"= by hand)
- The user wants a real, supervised live-session test run

* ADR-009: Technical enforcement of no-commit/no-push via Claude Code permissions
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-permission-enforcement
:STATUS:   Superseded by ADR-015 (2026-08-18)
:DATE:     2026-07-20
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Superseded" from "Accepted, implemented, live-verified"  [2026-08-18 Tue] — user requested the agent be able to run these five subcommands itself, gated by a per-action confirmation prompt instead of an outright block; see [[id:adr-agent-git-authority][ADR-015]]
:END:

** Context
AGENTS.md and [[id:std-no-commit][standards.org]]'s =std-no-commit= already state, as a hard
instructional rule, that no agent working in this repo may commit, push,
amend, rebase, reset, clean, or otherwise rewrite Git history without
explicit, in-the-moment permission — the user remains responsible for
commits and pushes, always ([[id:std-human-authority][std-human-authority]]). Until now this was
*instructional only*. [[id:ai-architecture][docs/ai/architecture.org]] flagged this gap directly:
"Enforcement should be technical (Claude Code permission settings/hooks)
where available, not merely instructional." The Phase 6 live smoke test
([[id:adr-claude-code-ide-install][ADR-008]]) found the agent behaved correctly, but noted explicitly that this
was "behavioral only, no technical enforcement configured yet." This ADR
closes that gap for Phase 7.

** Decision drivers
- The rule should hold regardless of which front end drives the =claude= CLI
  in this repo — a direct terminal session, or claude-code-ide.el launched
  from Emacs ([[id:adr-primary-agent][ADR-004]]) — both invoke the same binary against the same
  project directory.
- =std-human-authority= implies the agent should never run these commands at
  all, not that it may run them after an in-session approval — the human
  runs them directly via their own git/Magit.
- Match the written rule's scope exactly; don't invent a broader security
  posture nobody asked for ([[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]]).

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------------------------------------------------------------|
| =permissions.deny= in a committed =.claude/settings.json= | Accepted |
| =permissions.ask= (prompt-through) for the same commands | Rejected — lets the agent execute after approval, which is the wrong shape per =std-human-authority= |
| =deny= plus a =PreToolUse= hook for audit logging | Rejected for now — =deny= alone already fully blocks execution with no prompt and no side effect; a hook only adds an audit trail, which nothing in the standards docs currently asks for. Easy to add later if wanted. |
| Put the rule in =.claude/settings.local.json= instead | Rejected — that file is personal/uncommitted (holds ad hoc tool-approval history built up per-machine), not durable project policy; the enforcement needs to travel with the repo |

** Evidence
- =.claude/settings.local.json= exists but is untracked (=git ls-files
  .claude/= showed only the =rules/*.md= files tracked before this change) —
  confirms it's the wrong location for committed policy.
- Confirmed via the current Claude Code permission-rule syntax: =Bash(<prefix>
  *)= matches the literal prefix and anything after, including the bare
  prefix with no trailing arguments (e.g. =Bash(git *)= matches =git=,
  =git status=, =git commit=, etc.) — used to scope each deny rule to exactly
  one subcommand plus all its arguments/flags.
- Live-verified in this session: =git commit --dry-run -m "permission-test"=
  (a no-side-effect command matching the =Bash(git commit *)= deny rule) was
  refused outright — no permission prompt, no execution. Ordinary read-only
  commands (=git status=, =git log=, =git diff --stat=) ran unaffected
  immediately afterward.

** Decision
New, committed =.claude/settings.json=:
#+begin_src json
{
  "permissions": {
    "deny": [
      "Bash(git commit *)",
      "Bash(git push *)",
      "Bash(git rebase *)",
      "Bash(git reset *)",
      "Bash(git clean *)"
    ]
  }
}
#+end_src
Scoped to exactly the five subcommands AGENTS.md names (commit including
=--amend=, push including =--force=/=--force-with-lease=, rebase, reset,
clean). Plain =git status=/=diff=/=log=/=add=/=stash= etc. are untouched.

** Consequences
- Both this direct CLI session and any future claude-code-ide.el session in
  this repo are technically blocked from these five subcommands, not just
  instructed against them.
- The deny list is a prefix match against the literal command string, not a
  full sandbox — a command that reaches =git commit= indirectly (e.g. wrapped
  inside another interpreter invocation rather than a plain =Bash= tool call)
  is not guaranteed to be caught. This is a known limitation of the
  mechanism, not a gap specific to this config; matches the "technical where
  available" framing in architecture.org rather than a claim of total
  enforcement.
- =docs/ai/architecture.org=, =docs/standards.org= (=std-no-commit=), and
  =.claude/rules/security.md= updated to cross-reference this ADR so the
  instructional rule and its technical backstop stay linked.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =jq -e '.permissions.deny[]' .claude/settings.json= — valid JSON, all five
  rules present.
- =git commit --dry-run -m "permission-test"= — denied outright, no prompt.
- =git status=/=git log --oneline -3=/=git diff --stat= — all ran normally,
  confirming the deny list doesn't over-match read-only operations.

** Rollback
Delete =.claude/settings.json= (or remove the =permissions.deny= block). No
other config depends on it; the instructional rule in AGENTS.md/
standards.org/security.md remains in force on its own regardless.

** Revisit when
- The user wants an audit trail of blocked attempts (add a =PreToolUse= hook
  alongside the existing deny rules).
- A legitimate workflow needs one of these five subcommands run by the agent
  under explicit, in-the-moment approval — if that ever happens, the fix is
  a scoped, temporary edit to this file for that session, not a standing
  =ask= rule.
- Claude Code's permission-rule syntax changes in a way that could silently
  narrow or widen what these patterns match.

* ADR-010: Skill policy — scaffold ADR-writing only, defer the rest
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-skill-policy
:STATUS:   Accepted, implemented
:DATE:     2026-07-21
:END:

** Context
[[id:ai-prompts][docs/ai/prompts.org]] is titled "Prompt, Memory, and *Skill* Management" but had
no skill section yet — Phase 8 ([[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]) explicitly asks to "decide on a
skill policy if repeatable workflows emerge." Looking back across Phases 4-7,
three patterns were repeated by hand rather than tooled:
1. Writing ADR entries (the 10-section structure in
   =.claude/rules/documentation.md=) — done for ADR-006 through ADR-009, four
   times.
2. The disposable headless-daemon smoke-test pattern (=emacs
   --daemon=doom-phaseN-test=, eval, verify, tear down) — Phases 4, 5, 6.
3. The phase-closeout pattern (check a =PROJECT.org= box, update the
   =docs/roadmap.org= table row, cross-reference =docs/ai/evaluation.org='s
   checklist) — Phases 4, 5, 6, 7.

** Decision drivers
- =docs/decisions.org='s ADR convention is durable — it's meant to record
  *any* future architectural decision in this repo, not just Phase 1 ones, so
  tooling that keeps its structure consistent has value well past handoff.
- The headless-daemon-test and phase-closeout patterns are specific to this
  one-time, phased overhaul; [[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]] shows Phase 9 (validation and
  handoff) as the last phase, after which this repo returns to being a
  regular, non-phased personal config. Building tooling for patterns unlikely
  to recur at the same frequency violates [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]].
- User confirmed this trade-off directly rather than the agent inferring it.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------------------------------------------------------------|
| No skill at all; just document the policy | Rejected — leaves the one genuinely durable pattern (ADR-writing) unsupported |
| Skill for all three repeated patterns | Rejected — over-builds for two patterns tied to a one-time phased project, not ongoing repo use |
| Skill for ADR-writing only | *Accepted* — matches the one pattern with a life past Phase 1 |

** Evidence
- =grep -n "^\* ADR-" docs/decisions.org= confirms four hand-written ADRs
  (006-009) all independently reproducing the same 10-section shape.
- =docs/roadmap.org='s Phase 1 table lists Phase 9 as the final phase; no
  future phase is named that would repeat the headless-daemon-test or
  phase-closeout patterns at comparable frequency.

** Decision
Added =.claude/skills/new-adr/SKILL.md= — a project-scoped Claude Code skill
(invoked as =/new-adr=) that scaffolds the next ADR number, a descriptive
kebab-case =:ID:=, and all 10 required sections into =docs/decisions.org=,
matching the exact structure and cross-linking conventions this file and
=.claude/rules/documentation.md= already require. No skill built for the
other two patterns.

** Consequences
- Future architectural decisions in this repo (Phase 1 or beyond) get
  consistent structure without hand-copying an existing entry as a template.
- The skill needs updating if the required ADR structure itself changes.
- No tooling exists yet for the headless-daemon-test or phase-closeout
  patterns; if either recurs, it'll again be done by hand until/unless the
  Revisit condition below triggers.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- Frontmatter of =.claude/skills/new-adr/SKILL.md= parsed as well-formed
  (=name=/=description= keys present, YAML block correctly delimited).
- *Not yet live-verified*: invoking =/new-adr= requires a fresh session (new
  skills aren't picked up mid-session, same watcher caveat as
  =.claude/settings.json= hooks — see [[id:adr-permission-enforcement][ADR-009]]'s validation notes on that
  mechanism). Structural review of the instructions against ADR-008/ADR-009's
  actual shape is the check available in this session.

** Rollback
Delete =.claude/skills/new-adr/=. Nothing else in this repo depends on it;
ADRs can still be written by hand per =.claude/rules/documentation.md=.

** Revisit when
- The headless-daemon-test or phase-closeout pattern recurs three or more
  times after Phase 9 handoff — build a skill for whichever one actually
  repeats, not both preemptively.
- A future session confirms =/new-adr= live end-to-end and finds the
  scaffolded structure needs adjustment.
- Claude Code's skill format (frontmatter schema, =SKILL.md= discovery rules)
  changes in a way that could break this skill silently.

* ADR-011: mu4e for 3-account email, mbsync/msmtp backends, pass+oama credential split
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-email-mu4e
:STATUS:   Accepted, implemented for personal/ualberta — tricca deferred (not implemented)
:DATE:     2026-07-29
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Note taken on [2026-08-05 Wed] \\
  Same-day regression: both Gmail-backed refresh tokens (=personal=/=ualberta=,
  confirmed live-verified this same morning) went invalid by the afternoon —
  =oama access <address>= now returns =getEmailAuth: InvalidGrant= for both,
  exactly 7 days after their 2026-07-29 authorization. This is the "Testing
  consent-screen caps refresh tokens at 7 days" condition this ADR's own
  Revisit-when section already named as a possibility — now confirmed
  observed, not hypothetical. Also: =ualberta='s first full =mbsync= hit a
  separate, unrelated Google IMAP =OVERQUOTA= throttle mid-sync (large
  first-time pull, ~57k messages across overlapping label-folders). See
  Validation below for the full timeline. =:STATUS:= still intentionally left
  at =Proposed=.
- Note taken on [2026-08-05 Wed] \\
  Partial-verification pass: Google Cloud OAuth2 client registration + consent
  screen and =oama authorize= for both Gmail-backed accounts confirmed done
  and live-verified. =mail/tricca= remains blocked on circumstances outside
  the user's control — not an agent-side or in-repo blocker. See Validation
  below; =:STATUS:= intentionally left at =Proposed= until the full
  verification pass (sub-phase 5) completes.
- Note taken on [2026-08-06 Thu] \\
  Root cause addressed: user published the Google Cloud OAuth consent screen
  (Testing → In production) for the project behind
  =mail/oauth2-google-client=, then re-ran =oama authorize google <address>=
  for both =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca= (browser/login,
  user's own action — not scriptable). Chose the "publish, unverified" path
  rather than full Google app-verification review or falling back to Gmail
  App Passwords — the 7-day cap is tied to publish status, not verification
  status, so this resolves it without a verification review this
  single-user personal app doesn't need. Post-publish, =oama access
  <address>= confirmed exit 0 for both accounts (token values never printed,
  per [[id:std-credentials][std-credentials]]). Durability (surviving past the old 7-day mark)
  not yet confirmed by elapsed time — see Revisit when. =:STATUS:= still
  intentionally left at =Proposed= until the full
  verification pass (sub-phase 5) completes.
- State "Proposed"    from ""              [2026-07-29 Wed]
:END:

** Context
[[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]'s "Future phases (named, not implemented)" list carried a bare
=- Email= bullet since Phase 1 closeout — unscoped. The user has 3 accounts to
bring into Doom Emacs. =init.el= already had an inert scaffold
(=;;(mu4e +org +gmail)=) from an earlier session anticipating this. This ADR
records the decisions made during plan approval, before implementation
started.

*Correction during execution (2026-07-29):* the plan was approved assuming
"2 Gmail + 1 Gandi." Once real addresses were collected, the actual accounts
are =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= (consumer Gmail, label =personal=),
=jcook@ualberta.ca= (Google Workspace for Education, *not* a =@gmail.com=
address — label =ualberta=), and =jcook@tricca.ca= (confirmed Gandi Mail,
label =tricca=). This doesn't change any decision below — Google Workspace
uses the same =imap.gmail.com=/=smtp.gmail.com= servers and the same oama
=google= service as consumer Gmail, just a second, separate
=oama authorize google jcook@ualberta.ca= run and (per the module's own
=+mu4e-gmail-accounts= mechanism, since the address/maildir won't
auto-match =+gmail='s =gmail.com=/"gmail" pattern) an explicit
=(setq +mu4e-gmail-accounts '(("jcook@ualberta.ca" . "/ualberta")))= in
=config.el=. It does add one new risk: =ualberta.ca= is an institutional
Google Workspace domain, and its admin (university IT) may restrict which
third-party OAuth apps can request full mail scope against accounts on that
domain — a distinct failure mode from the personal-account "Testing" consent
screen risk already tracked below, not yet known to be a problem or not.
The =mail/<label>= pass entry for the plain-password account is
=mail/tricca=, not =mail/gandi= (label-consistency, per the "label must
match everywhere" rule below).

** Decision drivers
- [[id:std-doom-first][standards.org]]: prefer Doom's own module over a hand-rolled alternative.
- [[id:std-no-duplication][standards.org]]: one mail client, one credential store — a second tool needs a
  clearly distinct role the first can't cover, not just popularity.
- [[id:std-credentials][standards.org]] / [[id:std-binaries][standards.org]]: no hardcoded secrets; any new external
  binary needs a documented install/upgrade/rollback lifecycle.
- [[id:std-org-first][standards.org]]: prefer real Org capabilities where composing/reading text is
  involved.
- [[id:std-no-scope-creep][standards.org]]: don't build a custom notification system or unified-inbox
  layer this phase doesn't need yet.
- User chose OAuth2 over Gmail App Passwords, periodic auto-fetch over
  manual-only, and separate per-account contexts over a unified inbox
  (confirmed directly, not inferred).
- User chose =oama= over =mutt_oauth2.py= for OAuth2 token management
  (confirmed directly, weighing oama's purpose-built dual mbsync+msmtp
  integration against its heavier non-apt install path).

** Candidates
| Decision point       | Candidates considered                    | Fate |
|-----------------------+-------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------|
| Mail client           | mu4e / notmuch / wanderlust                | *mu4e* accepted — natively-integrated Doom module ([[id:std-doom-first][std-doom-first]]); notmuch/wanderlust would be a second, undifferentiated client ([[id:std-no-duplication][std-no-duplication]]) |
| Sync backend          | mbsync (isync) / offlineimap                | *mbsync* accepted — Doom module's own recommended default, faster than offlineimap per its own README |
| Send path             | msmtp / raw smtpmail+auth-source            | *msmtp* accepted — module README explicitly recommends it over raw =smtpmail= for multi-account setups (avoids async-process credential-access issues) |
| Gmail auth            | App Passwords / OAuth2                      | *OAuth2* accepted (user's explicit choice) — App Passwords rejected as the simpler-but-not-preferred alternative, recorded below |
| OAuth2 token manager  | mutt_oauth2.py / oama                       | *oama* accepted (user's explicit choice) — purpose-built single tool for both mbsync =PassCmd= and msmtp =passwordeval=, transparent background refresh; mutt_oauth2.py rejected despite being apt-adjacent-simpler (ships as a mutt sample script, no compiled binary) |
| Notifications          | mu4e-alert package / native mu4e support    | *Native =mu4e-notification-support=* accepted — confirmed by reading the module's local source that it's the actual default (older docs' =mu4e-alert= mention is stale/aspirational); adding a package here would be unjustified scope into the still-unscoped future "Notifications and desktop integration" phase |
| Account views          | Unified inbox / separate per-account contexts | *Separate contexts* accepted (user's explicit choice) — Doom's =set-email-account!= default behavior |

** Evidence
- =init.el:180-183= (pre-change) confirmed the inert =:email= scaffold already
  anticipating =mu4e +org +gmail=.
- Read =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/email/mu4e/README.org= in full:
  confirms =+gmail=/=+mbsync=/=+offlineimap=/=+org= flag semantics, the
  msmtp-over-smtpmail recommendation, and the =auth-source-pass=-via-=:tools
  pass +auth= mechanism documented for plain-password SMTP auth.
- Read the module's =packages.el= (declares =mu4e-compat= and, conditionally,
  =org-msg= — no =mu4e-alert=) and =config.el= (=mu4e-notification-support= is
  the module's actual notification mechanism) directly, correcting an
  assumption the README's structure implied.
- Read =set-email-account!='s implementation in
  =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/email/mu4e/autoload/email.el=:
  confirmed its =label= argument becomes the mu4e-context match predicate via
  =string-prefix-p "/<label>"= against the message's maildir — so the mbsync
  maildir subfolder name, the =set-email-account!= label, and (for the Gandi
  account) the =pass= entry suffix must all match exactly, or context
  switching breaks silently.
- Confirmed apt candidate versions on this machine (Ubuntu 24.04.4):
  =isync 1.4.4-5build3= and =msmtp 1.8.24-1build2= — both well above the
  minimum versions needed for =XOAUTH2=/=OAUTHBEARER= support (isync ≥1.3,
  msmtp ≥1.8.7).
- Fetched =github.com/pdobsan/oama='s README and its bundled
  =configs/msmtp-config-template=: confirmed exact syntax
  (=auth oauthbearer=, =passwordeval "oama access <address>"= for msmtp;
  =AuthMechs OAUTHBEARER=, =PassCmd "oama access <address>"= for mbsync — the
  =access= subcommand is documented as "Get the access token for email"), and
  confirmed =client_id_cmd=/=client_secret_cmd= can source the Google OAuth
  client credentials from =pass= rather than storing them in oama's own
  =config.yaml=.
- Confirmed via =gh api repos/pdobsan/oama/releases/latest= that a precompiled
  =oama-0.22.0-Linux-x86_64.tar.gz= static binary exists (no apt package).
- Confirmed this machine's existing =pass=/=gpg= identity
  (=gpg --list-secret-keys=: =859AF991C9EA4396=, matching
  =~/.password-store/.gpg-id=) — reused for oama's GPG backend rather than
  minting a second key.

** Decision
- =init.el=: =:email (mu4e +org +gmail +mbsync)=.
- =packages.el=: no changes (module supplies its own packages).
- =config.el=: one new flat section (matching the =~66-line gptel block's
  size/style precedent — a =config/email.el= split is premature per
  [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]] for a subsystem this new). Sets msmtp as the
  =sendmail-program=/=send-mail-function= path globally, then three
  =set-email-account!= blocks (=personal=, =ualberta=, =tricca=) with
  per-account folders/SMTP settings, plus =mu4e-update-interval 60= for
  periodic fetch. No custom leader binding added — Doom's default bindings
  module already provides =SPC o m= / ==mu4e= and =SPC o M c= / =+mu4e/compose=
  whenever =:email mu4e= is enabled.
- =~/.mbsyncrc= / =~/.msmtprc= (outside the repo, =chmod 600=): one account
  block per label. Gmail blocks use =AuthMechs OAUTHBEARER=/=auth oauthbearer=
  with =PassCmd=/=passwordeval= both calling =oama access <address>=. The
  tricca block uses plain =PassCmd "pass show mail/tricca"=/
  =passwordeval "pass show mail/tricca"=.
- =~/.config/oama/config.yaml= (outside the repo, =chmod 600=): GPG backend
  pointed at the existing pass/gpg key (=859AF991C9EA4396=); Google
  =client_id_cmd=/=client_secret_cmd= both call
  =pass show mail/oauth2-google-client= rather than storing the client secret
  in oama's own config file — keeping =pass= as the single source of truth
  for every static secret in this setup, with =oama= layered on top
  specifically for the OAuth2 refresh lifecycle =pass= alone can't provide.
  This is the concrete justification for why =oama= doesn't violate
  [[id:std-no-duplication][std-no-duplication]]: it is not a second, competing secret store — it reads
  its own app credentials from =pass= and only adds the token-refresh
  behavior =pass= structurally cannot do.
- New =mail/<label>= pass-entry naming convention (=mail/tricca=,
  =mail/oauth2-google-client=), extending the existing =api/<provider>=
  convention from [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]] to a new category.

** Consequences
- Two distinct credential-retrieval mechanisms now exist side by side for
  mail (=pass show= directly for Gandi, =oama access= for Gmail) — documented
  here precisely so a future session doesn't "simplify" them into one
  mechanism without re-deriving why they differ (OAuth2 is structurally
  required to have a refresh loop; a plain IMAP/SMTP password is not).
- =oama= is this repo's first externally-sourced static binary without an apt
  package — its upgrade path is "download a new release tarball," not
  =apt upgrade=; documented explicitly in [[id:docs-inventory][inventory.org]]'s binaries table
  rather than assumed to follow the same lifecycle as =mu=/=isync=/=msmtp=.
- Gmail's IMAP folder names (=[Gmail]/Sent Mail= etc.) and the exact Gandi
  folder names (=Sent=/=Drafts=/=Trash=/=Archive= used in =config.el= are
  placeholders) both need confirming against each account's real folder
  listing before the =set-email-account!= blocks are correct — flagged as
  not-yet-verified below, not asserted as fact.
- A Google Cloud OAuth2 client registration and consent-screen configuration
  now exists as an out-of-repo dependency this config relies on — if it's
  deleted or revoked outside this repo's awareness, Gmail sync/send breaks
  with no local signal until the next =mbsync=/=msmtp= run fails.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above. Additionally: =mu4e-alert= was not added as a
=package!= override — the module's native =mu4e-notification-support= already
covers desktop notifications, and adding a second notification path here
would be unjustified scope into the separate, still-unscoped "Notifications
and desktop integration" future phase.

** Validation
*Partially verified as of 2026-07-29* — this ADR was written immediately
after plan approval, then updated as execution progressed:
- Done: =init.el= updated, =doom sync= run clean (=mu4e-compat=/=org-msg=
  installed, 181 packages up-to-date). System packages installed
  (=maildir-utils=, =mu4e=, =isync 1.4.4=, =msmtp 1.8.24=, via the user's own
  =sudo= — auto-mode's classifier denies unattended =sudo apt-get install= to
  the agent). =doom doctor= fully clean (both mail-binary warnings gone).
  =oama 0.22.0= binary installed to =~/.local/bin=. =config.el=,
  =~/.mbsyncrc=, =~/.msmtprc= all updated with the real accounts (=personal=
  / =ualberta= / =tricca=, see Context correction above) — all still
  placeholders only for the folder names that need confirming against each
  provider's real IMAP listing, not the addresses.
- *Update 2026-08-05, confirmed done:* Google Cloud OAuth2 client
  registration and consent screen — verified indirectly (no console access
  from here) via =pass ls mail= showing =mail/oauth2-google-client= present,
  =~/.config/oama/config.yaml= sourcing =client_id_cmd=/=client_secret_cmd=
  from it (no raw secret in the file, no placeholders left), and decisively
  via =oama access <address>= returning exit 0 for both
  =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca= — a live, successful
  token fetch/refresh against Google is only possible with a working
  registered client and completed consent flow. =oama authorize= for both
  addresses is therefore also confirmed done (token state files present in

- *Still blocked, not a to-do:* the =mail/tricca= pass entry cannot be

- *Discovered 2026-08-05, previously undocumented:* =personal= already had a
  complete, healthy =mbsync= sync from 2026-07-29 — ~22k messages across its

  =Personal=, =[Gmail]/*=), with proper per-folder =.mbsyncstate=/

  matching that real structure exactly (not placeholders, contrary to what
  this ADR previously said). Origin unconfirmed — the user wasn't sure how it
  got there. Re-ran =mbsync personal= 2026-08-05 as a sanity check: clean
  incremental sync, exit 0, no errors.
- *2026-08-05, =ualberta= first full sync attempted, partially completed:*
  =mbsync ualberta= ran for ~75 minutes, correctly landing =INBOX= (partial,
  see below), =Boomerang*=, =Conversation History=, =[Gmail]/All Mail=
  (25,483 messages, complete) and =[Gmail]/Drafts= (31, complete), then hit
  =IMAP error: unexpected BYE response: [OVERQUOTA] Account exceeded command
  or bandwidth limits= mid-=[Gmail]/Important= (16,015 far-side messages, a
  mostly-duplicate label-overlay of =All Mail=). =[Gmail]/Sent Mail=/=Spam=/
  =Starred=/=Trash= never attempted. Separately, the very first =ualberta=
  sync attempt was itself killed mid-flight by an agent-side =timeout 120=
  wrapper, producing a =Warning: lost track of 510 pulled message(s)= on the
  next run — a bookkeeping gap from the interruption, not data loss; no
  further such warnings on later runs. 41,864 files landed safely on disk
  from what did complete; nothing corrupted.
- *2026-08-05, regression — see LOGBOOK:* an exponential-backoff retry loop
  for the =OVERQUOTA= throttle instead hit a different, terminal failure:
  =oama access <address>= now returns =getEmailAuth: InvalidGrant= for
  *both* =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca= (confirmed via
  direct =oama access= calls, DNS ruled out as the cause, reproducible on
  repeat). Both tokens were authorized 2026-07-29 and failed within the same
  day as this ADR's own "7-day Testing-mode cap" revisit condition predicts.
  Sub-phase 3's credentials are therefore *no longer* in the "confirmed done"
  state recorded earlier in this section as of 2026-08-05 morning — they
  regressed the same afternoon. =ualberta='s =OVERQUOTA= retry is blocked on
  this, not on the throttle itself; resume once re-authorized.
- *2026-08-06, credential regression resolved:* user published the Google
  Cloud OAuth consent screen for the project behind
  =mail/oauth2-google-client= (Testing → In production status), then
  re-ran =oama authorize google <address>= for both
  =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca=. =oama access
  <address>= confirmed exit 0 for both immediately afterward. Chose to
  publish unverified rather than pursue Google's full app-verification
  review (unnecessary for a single-user personal app requesting only
  restricted, not sensitive+public, scope) or fall back to Gmail App
  Passwords. This should stop the 7-day expiry going forward since the cap
  is a Testing-status artifact, not a verification-status one — but that's

- *2026-08-07, =ualberta= sync completed:* resumed =mbsync ualberta=

- *2026-08-07, self-send test done for =personal=/=ualberta=:* sent a real

- Not yet done: confirm the new OAuth tokens survive past the full 7 days

- *2026-08-07, closeout decision:* work on this phase is pausing here for

** Rollback
=git diff init.el config.el= shows the full in-repo change; revert both files
and =doom sync= to return =:email= to fully commented-out. Outside the repo:
delete =~/.mbsyncrc=, =~/.msmtprc=, =~/.config/oama/=, =~/.mail/=, and remove
=~/.local/bin/oama=; revoke the Google OAuth2 client at
console.cloud.google.com if abandoning OAuth2 entirely; =pass rm= any
=mail/*= entries created.

** Revisit when
- *Triggered 2026-08-05, addressed 2026-08-06:* Google's "Testing"
  consent-screen publish status was hypothesized to cap Gmail refresh tokens
  at 7 days — observed directly (both =personal= and =ualberta= tokens,
  authorized 2026-07-29, failed with =InvalidGrant= the same calendar week).
  User re-authorized both immediately, then published the consent screen to
  In production (unverified) to fix the root cause rather than accept
  weekly re-authorization or fall back to Gmail App Passwords — see
  Validation. *Not yet fully closed:* this bullet stays open until a token
  is observed surviving past the old ~7-day mark (i.e. past
  2026-08-13) without =InvalidGrant=-ing again; if it recurs even after
  publishing, the two remaining options above (accept periodic
  re-authorization, or Gmail App Passwords) are still live.
- *=jcook@tricca.ca= (Gandi) email is not implemented, as of 2026-08-07,
  and work on it is paused for now (other priorities), not actively
  blocked-and-waiting.* =personal=/=ualberta= are fully live; =tricca= has
  =config.el=/=~/.msmtprc=/=~/.mbsyncrc= scaffolding in place but no
  credential, no sync, no index, no self-send verification. Resume once the
  =mail/tricca= external blocker clears (circumstances outside the user's
  control as of 2026-08-05, unspecified here): create the =mail/tricca=
  pass entry (user action, never agent-created), then run sub-phase 5
  (=mbsync=/=mu index=/self-send test) for that account — =mu init= itself
  doesn't need repeating, =tricca= was already registered as a =my-address=
  on 2026-08-07.
- =ualberta='s =[Gmail]/Important=/=Starred= label-overlay folders keep
  causing =OVERQUOTA= throttling on large first syncs — consider narrowing
  =~/.mbsyncrc='s =Patterns *= for that channel to exclude them (mostly
  duplicate content vs =All Mail=) if this recurs on retry.
- The verification pass (see plan) completes — update =:STATUS:= and add a
  =:LOGBOOK:= entry rather than silently editing it.
- A fourth mail account, or a provider needing yet another auth mechanism, is
  added — check whether the =pass=-vs-=oama= split still cleanly covers it
  before assuming it does.
- Email config in =config.el= grows non-trivial helper functions (custom
  filing/capture automation) comparable to what justified splitting AI docs
  into =docs/ai/*.org= — revisit the flat-file-vs-=config/email.el= choice
  then, not preemptively.

* ADR-012: elfeed (=:app rss +org +youtube=) for RSS integration
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-rss-elfeed
:STATUS:   live-verified
:DATE:     2026-08-10
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Note taken on [2026-08-10] \\
  User installed =mpv=/=yt-dlp= via their own terminal (=sudo= through the
  =!= session-shell prefix has no TTY for the password prompt — same
  constraint as [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]'s mail-binary installs). =doom doctor= re-run:
  fully clean, 46 modules, 151 packages, zero warnings — the two expected
  warnings from the initial implementation are gone. =:STATUS:= moved from
  "implemented — mpv/yt-dlp install pending" to "implemented — not yet
  live-verified"; still short of =live-verified= until =SPC o r=,
  auto-update, and elfeed-tube transcript/follow-along are confirmed working
  in the user's own interactive session.
- Note taken on [2026-08-10] \\
  User confirmed, in their own interactive session, all three remaining
  validation items: =SPC o r= opens elfeed, auto-update fires on open, and
  an elfeed-tube transcript/follow-along completed successfully.
  =:STATUS:= moved to =live-verified=. Phase 3 — RSS integration is now
  fully closed.
:END:

** Context
[[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]'s "Future phases (named, not implemented)" list carried a bare
=- RSS and communications= bullet since Phase 1 closeout — unscoped, and
bundling two materially different tool categories (passive feed-reading vs.
synchronous/social messaging) under one name. This ADR records the decisions
made in conversation (a structured grill, same format as [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]) before
implementation, and the implementation itself.

** Decision drivers
- [[id:std-doom-first][standards.org]]: prefer Doom's own module over a hand-rolled alternative —
  =:app rss= already exists, commented out, in =init.el=.
- [[id:std-org-first][standards.org]]: prefer real Org capabilities where composing/managing
  structured text is involved.
- [[id:std-no-scope-creep][standards.org]]: don't bundle unrelated tool categories into one phase just
  because a roadmap bullet named them together.
- Discoverability: [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]] established this repo's preference for a
  reachable leader binding over =M-x=-only access for anything used
  regularly.
- User confirmed directly (not inferred): RSS-only scope, =+org= for feed
  management, =+youtube= included, install =mpv= now rather than defer,
  =SPC o r= as the leader binding, auto-update on open, and scaffold
  =elfeed.org= with just the required root structure (real feeds added
  later by the user).

** Candidates
| Decision point                  | Candidates considered                        | Fate |
|----------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Scope                           | RSS + IRC/social bundled / RSS only            | *RSS only* accepted — IRC/Twitter are a distinct tool category; split back out to its own unscoped roadmap bullet ([[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]]) |
| Feed management                 | =+org= (elfeed-org, Org outline) / plain =elfeed-feeds= Elisp list | *=+org=* accepted — consistent with this repo's Org-as-canonical-format convention ([[id:std-org-first][std-org-first]]); scales better than a flat list once tagged/categorized |
| YouTube integration             | =+youtube= (elfeed-tube) / skip                | *=+youtube=* accepted (user's explicit choice) — transcript fetching + mpv follow-along |
| =mpv= install timing            | Install now / defer until actually used        | *Install now* accepted (user's explicit choice, approved as a system-package install per =AGENTS.md=) |
| Entry point                     | Leader binding / =M-x elfeed= only             | *=SPC o r=* accepted — neither the module nor this config's default bindings ship one; matches [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]'s discoverability rationale |
| Update cadence                  | Auto-update on open / manual =elfeed-update= only | *Auto-update on open* accepted (user's explicit choice) — avoids a stale buffer on every =SPC o r= |
| Initial feed content            | Seed real feeds now / scaffold root structure only | *Scaffold only* accepted (user's explicit choice) — feed content isn't an architecture decision, added later by the user |

** Evidence
- =init.el:185-191= (pre-change) confirmed the inert scaffold =;;(rss +org)=
  already anticipating this module, alongside =;;irc=/=;;twitter=/=;;calendar=
  (all still commented out, untouched).
- Read =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/app/rss/README.org= in full:
  confirmed =+org=/=+youtube= flag semantics, =elfeed-org='s
  =:elfeed:=-tagged-root convention, and the module's own default
  =elfeed-search-filter= (=@2-weeks-ago=).
- Read the module's =packages.el=: =elfeed=, =elfeed-goodies= unconditional;
  =elfeed-org= gated on =+org=; =elfeed-tube= gated on =+youtube= — all
  version-pinned by the module itself, no override needed here.
- Read the module's =config.el=: confirmed =rmh-elfeed-org-files= already
  defaults to =(list "elfeed.org")= relative to =org-directory= (=~/org/=,
  set in this repo's =config.el:43=) — no path override needed. Confirmed no
  leader-key binding is shipped by the module or by this config's default
  bindings (grepped for =elfeed=/=+rss= in both default-bindings files:
  zero matches) — the discoverability gap driving the =SPC o r= decision
  above is real, not assumed.
- =which mpv=: not found. =sudo -n true=: fails ("a password is required")
  — confirmed, same as [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]'s precedent, the agent cannot install
  system packages unattended; =mpv= install is a user action.
- =doom doctor= output (see Validation) surfaced a second, previously
  undiscussed dependency: =youtube-dl=/=yt-dlp=, also required for
  elfeed-tube-mpv live transcripts, also missing.

** Decision
- =init.el=: =:app (rss +org +youtube)=, uncommenting the existing scaffold.
- =packages.el=: no changes (module supplies its own pinned packages).
- =config.el=: one new short section (matching the flat-section style used
  for the =mu4e= block, [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]] — a =config/rss.el= split is premature
  per [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]] for a subsystem this new):
  =(map! :leader :desc "RSS (elfeed)" "o r" #'elfeed)= and
  =(add-hook 'elfeed-search-mode-hook #'elfeed-update)=.
- =~/org/elfeed.org= scaffolded with a =* root :elfeed:= headline and a
  short usage comment — no real feed subscriptions yet.
- System dependency: =mpv= (and, discovered via =doom doctor=,
  =youtube-dl=/=yt-dlp=) to be installed by the user via their own =sudo=
  (agent has no passwordless =sudo=, per Evidence above).
- =docs/roadmap.org=: "RSS and communications" bullet split — RSS promoted
  to an active Phase 3 row; a standalone, still-unscoped "Communications
  (IRC, social feeds, etc.)" bullet left in the Future phases list.

** Consequences
- A new externally-managed dependency pair (=mpv=, =yt-dlp=) exists outside
  =doom sync='s reach, same lifecycle class as [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]'s =oama= binary —
  tracked in [[id:docs-inventory][inventory.org]] once installed, not assumed to follow apt's
  normal Doom-package upgrade path.
- Until =mpv=/=yt-dlp= are installed, =elfeed-tube-mpv-follow-mode=/=-where=
  are inert (bound but non-functional) — =doom doctor= will keep surfacing
  these two warnings until then; this is expected, not a regression to
  chase.
- =elfeed.org= has no real feeds yet — =SPC o r= will show an empty buffer
  until the user adds subscriptions, which is expected per the scaffold-only
  decision above, not a bug.
- "Communications" (IRC/social) remains genuinely unscoped — this ADR
  deliberately does not decide anything about it.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync=: clean, 188 packages up-to-date, =elfeed=/=elfeed-goodies=/
  =elfeed-org=/=elfeed-tube= all confirmed installed.
- =doom doctor= (first run, before =mpv=/=yt-dlp=): 46 modules, 151 packages,
  2 expected warnings under =:app rss= (=mpv=, =youtube-dl=/=yt-dlp=
  missing).
- *2026-08-10:* user installed =mpv= 0.37.0-1ubuntu4 and =yt-dlp=
  2024.04.09-1 via apt, in their own interactive terminal (=sudo= through
  the agent's =!= session-shell prefix has no TTY for the password prompt).
  =doom doctor= re-run: fully clean, 46 modules, 151 packages, zero
  warnings. Both binaries confirmed on =PATH= (=which mpv yt-dlp=) and
  recorded in [[id:docs-inventory][inventory.org]].
- *2026-08-10:* user confirmed, in their own interactive session, that
  =SPC o r= opens elfeed, auto-update fires on open, and an elfeed-tube
  transcript/follow-along completed successfully. All validation items now
  closed.

** Rollback
Remove =(rss +org +youtube)= from =init.el='s =:app= section (restore the
=;;(rss +org)= comment or delete outright), revert the RSS section in
=config.el=, delete =~/org/elfeed.org= if abandoning entirely, then
=doom sync=. =mpv=/=yt-dlp=, if installed, can be removed via the system
package manager independently — nothing else in this config depends on them.

** Revisit when
- *Done 2026-08-10, see LOGBOOK:* =mpv=/=yt-dlp= installed, =doom doctor=
  re-run clean, and =SPC o r=/auto-update/elfeed-tube follow-along all
  confirmed working in the user's own interactive session. =:STATUS:= is
  =live-verified=; nothing left open on this ADR.
- The user wants IRC, Mastodon/Twitter, or another "communications" tool —
  scope and grill that as its own phase; this ADR explicitly does not cover
  it, per the Context split above.
- =elfeed.org= grows enough feeds/tags that the flat scaffold's usage
  comment stops being sufficient documentation of the convention.

* ADR-013: Defensively strip Claude-Code session env vars in =config.el=
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-strip-claude-env-vars
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-08-11
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Note taken on [2026-08-11] \\
  Applied live: =M-x doom/reload-env= picked up the re-cleaned cache, then
  the =config.el= strip expression was evaluated directly against the user's
  running daemon to clear the vars =doom/reload-env= alone couldn't (it only
  appends the reloaded file's contents on top of the existing
  =(default-value 'process-environment)=, it doesn't clear entries absent
  from the new file — so vars baked in at the daemon's original boot, before
  this fix existed, needed a direct clear). Confirmed via
  =emacsclient --eval= immediately after: zero =CLAUDE*=/=AI_AGENT= entries
  remained. A subprocess spawned fresh from the live daemon (=call-process
  "env"=) also showed zero such entries, confirming the actual inheritance
  mechanism claude-code-ide.el's spawned =claude= process depends on.
- Note taken on [2026-08-11] \\
  User opened a real claude-code-ide.el session interactively in their live
  daemon afterward and confirmed it worked — no "Transcript saving is off".
  (An agent-driven attempt via remote =emacsclient --eval= immediately before
  this had died with exit code 129; user clarified afterward that was them
  killing it themselves before the agent could inspect it, not a fix
  failure.) =:STATUS:= moved to =live-verified=; nothing left open on this
  ADR.
:END:

** Context
User reported "Transcript saving is off" recurring identically after every
reboot, from a claude-code-ide.el session. Diagnosis (this conversation):
Doom's =doom sync --env= scrapes the invoking shell's *entire* environment
into =~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/@/init.d/05-doom-env.load.el=, which Doom
loads unconditionally on every Emacs startup — this is the exact mechanism
[[id:tshoot-daemon-path-systemd][docs/ai/troubleshooting.org's tshoot-daemon-path-systemd]] entry documents as
the standard remediation for a separate PATH bug. On 2026-08-10 that command
was run from inside an active Claude Code CLI subprocess shell, so it
captured and permanently baked in that shell's ephemeral, subprocess-only
Claude Code identity vars (=CLAUDECODE=1=, =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID=,
=CLAUDE_JOB_DIR=, =CLAUDE_PID=, =CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1=,
=CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli=, =AI_AGENT=, ...) belonging to a session/job that
had already exited. Every subsequent Emacs startup — including after
