reboot — reloads this poisoned cache, so every =claude= process launched from
Emacs afterward (any claude-code-ide.el session, any terminal backend)
inherits a dead session's identity instead of a fresh one. This is why the
symptom is identical after every reboot: the poisoned *file* survives reboot
untouched; only regenerating it changes anything, and regenerating it via the
documented =doom sync --env= remediation will always be run by an agent,
whose shell always has =CLAUDECODE=1= set, so it will keep getting
re-poisoned unless something else structurally prevents it.

** Decision drivers
- Must survive reboot (the reported symptom recurs identically every reboot,
  so a fix that doesn't survive it hasn't fixed anything).
- Must survive Doom package updates and Doom core upgrades — rules out
  patching claude-code-ide.el's vendored source or Doom's own =cli/env.el=,
  both overwritten/reverted by =doom sync -u=/=doom upgrade=, consistent with
  this project's existing precedent in
  [[id:tshoot-treesitter-confirmed][docs/ai/troubleshooting.org's tree-sitter entry]] of not patching
  vendor/module source unprompted.
- Must be self-healing even if =05-doom-env.load.el= gets re-poisoned again in
  the future — a real recurrence risk, not hypothetical, since =doom sync
  --env= is this repo's own documented PATH-bug remediation
  ([[id:tshoot-daemon-path-systemd][tshoot-daemon-path-systemd]]) and will always be run from a
  =CLAUDECODE=1= shell by an agent.
- Must not depend on a procedural rule ("never run =doom sync --env= from
  inside Claude Code") being remembered — that rule already silently failed
  once, producing this exact incident.

** Candidates
| Decision point           | Candidates considered                                                        | Fate |
|---------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Where to strip the vars   | One-time regen of the env cache only / patch Doom's =cli/env.el= scraper / patch claude-code-ide.el's spawn function / defensive strip in this repo's =config.el= | *Defensive strip in =config.el=* accepted — see Evidence for why the other three fail structurally |
| Strip granularity         | Exact enumerated var-name list / pattern match on =CLAUDECODE=\|=CLAUDE_*=\|=AI_AGENT= | *Pattern match* accepted — three independent sources (empirical contamination, official docs, the GitHub issue) each named a *different, non-overlapping* subset of bad vars, indicating the surface isn't fully enumerable and will likely grow in future CLI versions |
| Symptom-level mitigation  | Set =CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SESSION_PERSISTENCE=1= to force persistence despite the child-session flag / fix the underlying env leak | *Fix the leak* accepted — forcing persistence doesn't fix the still-wrong/stale =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= or the =CLAUDECODE=1= misidentification, it only papers over one downstream effect of it |

** Evidence
- Read =~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/@/init.d/05-doom-env.load.el= directly:
  =:date "2026-08-10 15:34:41"=, containing =CLAUDECODE=1=,
  =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID=92960d17-af5b-4dcf-93d4-d1ac5c76656b=,
  =CLAUDE_JOB_DIR=/home/james/.claude/jobs/92960d17=, =CLAUDE_PID=17508=
  (confirmed dead — =ps -p 17508= returns nothing),
  =CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1=, =CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli=,
  =AI_AGENT=claude-code_2-1-226_agent=.
- Confirmed live, in this very conversation's own session: =env | grep
  CLAUDE_JOB_DIR= showed =/home/james/.claude/jobs/92960d17= — the *stale*
  value from the poisoned file — while the session's real
  =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= was a different, correct value
  (=cfb115ff-f0fc-4a0e-944f-e95ee16d1f4c=). Only explicable by this Emacs
  session's =process-environment= having been set from the poisoned cache
  file at startup and inherited by the vterm/claude subprocess.
- =cat ~/.claude/jobs/92960d17/exit-cause ~/.claude/jobs/92960d17/exit-detail=:
  =exit_with_message= / ="No conversation found to continue"= — a prior
  session choked trying to resume that same stale, already-dead identity.
- Confirmed via load-order source reading (=doom-profiles.el='s
  =doom-profile--generate-init=/=doom-profile-generate-functions=, and the
  generated =~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/@/init.d/80-doom-modules.init.el=)
  that =05-doom-env.load.el= loads first (numeric prefix =05=) and
  =$DOOMDIR/config.el= (the =:user= module) loads *last* of everything, every
  single startup — confirming =config.el= is the one seam guaranteed to run
  after the poisoned cache regardless of what's in it.
- Fetched official Anthropic docs
  ([[https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars]]): =CLAUDECODE= and
  =CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION= are documented as intentionally subprocess-only
  markers Claude Code sets on things it spawns (Bash tool, hooks, etc.),
  never meant to be inherited beyond that subprocess's lifetime. Docs state a
  process seeing =CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1= is "automatically excluded
  from --resume, --continue, up-arrow history, and the claude agents list" —
  matches the observed "No conversation found to continue" exactly, and is
  the most likely mechanism behind "Transcript saving is off" too.
- Fetched GitHub issue =anthropics/claude-code#26190=: same bug class
  (nested/inherited =CLAUDECODE=/=CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT= confusing a
  freshly-launched =claude= process in other users' reports). Issue closed
  as not-planned; Anthropic's own recommended workaround is to explicitly
  unset these vars before launching a nested =claude= process — confirms
  stripping at the launch site is the sanctioned pattern, not a local hack.
- Read claude-code-ide.el's source directly
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/claude-code-ide.el/claude-code-ide.el=,
  function =claude-code-ide--create-terminal-session=): it only prepends
  =CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT=/=TERM_PROGRAM=/=FORCE_CODE_TERMINAL= (and
  =CLAUDE_CODE_NO_FLICKER= when enabled) to whatever =process-environment= it
  inherited from Emacs, and otherwise passes it through untouched. No
  customizable variable exists for extra/stripped env vars — confirmed there
  is no existing package setting to flip instead of this local fix.

** Decision
- =config.el=: add a defensive block, placed early (runs on every startup
  regardless — see Evidence on load order), that removes any
  =process-environment= entry matching =CLAUDECODE=, =CLAUDE_*=, or
  =AI_AGENT= via pattern match, independent of what
  =05-doom-env.load.el= contains.
- One-time hygiene: regenerate =05-doom-env.load.el= via =doom sync --env=
  from an environment-scrubbed subprocess (the invoking agent session is
  itself a Claude Code subprocess, so the scrubbing has to happen
  explicitly rather than by using a "naturally clean" shell) — removes the
  now-inert dead entries. Not load-bearing for the fix (the =config.el=
  strip is self-healing regardless), but avoids carrying obviously-wrong
  cached values forward.
- =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org=: new entry cross-linking
  [[id:tshoot-daemon-path-systemd][tshoot-daemon-path-systemd]] and this ADR, documenting the incident.

** Consequences
- Every =claude= process launched from this Emacs config (any terminal
  backend, any future integration) gets a clean environment with no risk of
  inheriting a stale/dead session's identity, regardless of what future
  =doom sync --env= runs capture.
- If the user ever legitimately wants a =CLAUDE_*=-prefixed env var set
  permanently (e.g. =CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR= to relocate =~/.claude=), it must be
  set explicitly in =config.el= via =setenv= *after* the strip block, not
  relied on via shell-profile-scrape-and-cache — documented here as the
  expected pattern going forward, since the blanket strip would otherwise
  remove it too.
- This is a local workaround for a gap in claude-code-ide.el (it doesn't
  strip nesting-detection vars before spawning, unlike what Anthropic's own
  issue thread recommends) — worth an upstream issue/PR against
  =manzaltu/claude-code-ide.el= separately, not done as part of this ADR.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- Disposable test daemon (=emacs --daemon=cc-env-test=, spawned from a still
  =CLAUDECODE=1= shell): =process-environment= showed zero
  =CLAUDECODE=/=CLAUDE_*=/=AI_AGENT= entries after loading =config.el=,
  confirming the strip works regardless of the daemon's own ambient
  environment at boot.
- Live daemon (the user's real session, socket =/run/user/1000/emacs/server=):
  after =M-x doom/reload-env= plus a direct eval of the strip expression (see
  LOGBOOK — =reload-env= alone wasn't sufficient), confirmed zero
  =CLAUDE*=/=AI_AGENT= entries in both =process-environment= itself and a
  freshly spawned subprocess (=call-process "env"=).
- User confirmed, interactively, in their own live session: a new
  claude-code-ide.el session launched successfully with no "Transcript
  saving is off" error. Closed.
- Not separately re-tested across an actual reboot — the fix now lives in
  =config.el=, which is loaded fresh (not cached) on every startup including
  after reboot, so this is expected to hold; revisit only if it doesn't.

** Rollback
Remove the defensive strip block from =config.el=. No other state depends on
it — =05-doom-env.load.el= regeneration (the hygiene step) is independently
reversible by just running =doom sync --env= again.

** Revisit when
- claude-code-ide.el (or Anthropic) adds a first-class option for this —
  e.g. an upstream defcustom to strip nesting-detection vars before
  spawning, or an official =claude= flag/env var to fully disable
  nested-session detection for a specific launch — switch to that instead of
  this local pattern-match workaround.
- The user ever wants a real =CLAUDE_*=-prefixed env var set permanently;
  add it explicitly after the strip block per the Consequences note above.

* ADR-014: Switch claude-code-ide.el's terminal backend from vterm to ghostel, then remove vterm entirely
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-terminal-backend-ghostel
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-08-17
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Note taken on [2026-08-17] \\
  Follow-through same day: with claude-code-ide.el's own re-verification
  done (see Evidence below), completed the full migration this ADR's
  Consequences had left open. =init.el=: =vterm= commented out of the
  =:term= module block (=ghostel= is now the only =:term= backend enabled).
  =config.el=: =my/project-vterm= renamed to =my/project-ghostel= and
  rewritten to call =(ghostel)= instead of =(vterm)= (kept
  =projectile-project-root= for consistency with the rest of this config
  rather than switching to =ghostel-project='s built-in =project.el=-based
  root detection, which uses different heuristics); both its keybindings
  (=SPC p t=, =SPC a t=) updated to match. The =window-size-fixed= freeze on
  =vterm-mode-hook= removed outright (dead code once vterm is gone) rather
  than ported to =ghostel-mode-hook= — no equivalent corruption has been
  observed with ghostel, and claude-code-ide.el already actively re-syncs
  ghostel's process window size on resize
  (=ghostel--window-adjust-process-window-size=), which vterm never had; if
  the same visual-corruption symptom recurs under ghostel, add an
  equivalent freeze then rather than pre-emptively. =doom sync= run (module
  change), =doom doctor= clean afterward: 46 modules (was 47), 151 packages
  (was 152). Verified in a fresh disposable daemon
  (=emacs --daemon=vterm-migration-check=, torn down after): =my/project-vterm=
  no longer =fboundp=, =my/project-ghostel= is, =vterm= feature not loaded,
  =SPC p t=/=SPC a t= both resolve to =my/project-ghostel= via
  =doom-leader-map=, and Doom's own built-in =SPC o t= popup terminal
  resolves unambiguously to =+ghostel/toggle= (confirmed by reading
  =config/default/+evil-bindings.el=: with both =:term vterm= and =:term
  ghostel= modules active, that file's sequential =(:when (modulep! ...))=
  blocks meant ghostel's binding was already silently winning over vterm's
  for the same key even before this cleanup — this migration mainly removes
  the now-redundant vterm module and its now-dead call sites, not a change
  in which backend =SPC o t= actually opened). Resolves this ADR's
  =my/project-vterm=/=:term vterm= consolidation open item.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-primary-agent][ADR-004]] picked =vterm= as claude-code-ide.el's terminal backend because it
matched this config's then-incumbent terminal module. A =vterm=/libvterm
resize-repaint bug surfaced afterward (a known bug class: a vterm window
elsewhere in the layout gets corrupted when a bottom-appearing popup like
which-key reclaims frame lines from other windows) and was only worked
around, not fixed, via the =window-size-fixed= freeze added to =config.el=
on 2026-08-07. This motivated evaluating =ghostel= (Doom's =:term ghostel=
module, maintained by @hlissner since Doom 26.07.0; an embedded-Ghostty
backend claude-code-ide.el already supports alongside =vterm=/=eat=) as a
replacement, tracked as a =PROJECT.org= backlog item ("Migrate
claude-code-ide.el's terminal backend from vterm to ghostel").

** Decision drivers
- The resize-repaint bug is real and only papered over, not fixed — a
  backend that doesn't share the bug class removes the need for the
  =window-size-fixed= workaround entirely.
- =ghostel='s own docstring (=claude-code-ide-terminal-backend=, in
  claude-code-ide.el) names it "the recommended backend" for rendering
  Claude Code's TUI, ahead of =vterm=/=eat=.
- Any replacement must be live-verified with the same rigor as the original
  vterm-based Phase 6 smoke test ([[id:adr-claude-code-ide-install][ADR-008]]), not just configured and
  assumed to work.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------------------------------------------------------------|
| Stay on =vterm= | Rejected — the resize-repaint bug was the original motivation for looking elsewhere; staying means keeping the =window-size-fixed= workaround indefinitely |
| =ghostel= | *Accepted* — Doom-maintained, already supported by claude-code-ide.el, docstring-recommended over the alternatives |
| =eat= | Not evaluated in depth — =ghostel= was already Doom's own maintained recommendation, and evaluating a third backend wasn't asked for |

** Evidence
All gathered 2026-08-17:
- User ran =M-x ghostel= interactively once, triggering the native
  =libghostty-vt= module's pre-built-binary download
  (=ghostel-module-auto-install=). Confirmed via =ghostel-debug-info= in a
  live session: =Module loaded: yes=, =Module file:
  .../straight/build-30.2/ghostel/ghostel-module.so= (1,438,328 bytes).
- User live-verified in their own real session that ghostel renders and
  resizes correctly.
- I independently re-ran the Phase 6 agentic-workflow smoke test (compare
  [[id:adr-claude-code-ide-install][ADR-008]]'s original vterm-based run) against a fresh disposable scratch
  project (=scratchpad/phase6-ghostel-smoke-test/=, its own git repo, zero
  commits — no baseline commit created by me, per this session's blanket
  =Bash(git commit *)= deny rule, see [[id:adr-permission-enforcement][ADR-009]]), using an isolated named
  Emacs daemon (=emacs --daemon=ghostel-smoke-test=) per the headless
  smoke-test pattern in =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org=, driven entirely via
  =emacsclient --eval=, never touching the user's real daemon (confirmed
  distinct sockets throughout, and confirmed via =ps aux= after teardown
  that only the user's pre-existing real daemon's processes remained).
- *New finding*: ghostel needs a real graphical (X11) frame to function,
  unlike =vterm=. A first attempt using a plain frame-less daemon (the exact
  pattern that worked fine for the original vterm-based Phase 6 test)
  produced a =ghostel-mode= buffer with =PID: nil=, =buffer-size: 0 chars=,
  and degenerate rendering fields in =ghostel-debug-info= (=Resolved font:
  nil=, =Frame font: tty=) — because ghostel embeds a real GPU/font-metric
  terminal renderer (libghostty), unlike vterm's plain character-grid text,
  and had no display to size itself against. Fixed by attaching a real X
  frame to the same isolated daemon via =emacsclient -c -F '((window-system
  . x) (display . ":1"))'= — plain =-c= with =DISPLAY= merely set in the
  shell environment was *not* sufficient; it silently produced another
  frame-less tty frame. The explicit =-F= frame-parameters were what forced
  a real X connection. After that, ghostel rendered correctly: real Ubuntu
  Sans Mono font metrics, a 100x48 terminal, and the full-color Claude Code
  TUI banner readable via =buffer-string= with text properties/faces
  intact, authenticated as the user's real account and correctly scoped to
  the scratch directory.
- Ran the same seeded-bug task as ADR-008's original test (write =calc.py=
  with a deliberate =a - b= bug, write =test_calc.py= asserting
  =add(2, 3) == 5=, run pytest, fix the bug, re-run to confirm, never touch
  git, write a =DONE.marker= sentinel): the agent wrote both files, ran
  pytest, hit the expected failure, fixed =calc.py= to =a + b=, re-ran
  pytest to confirm passing, created =DONE.marker=, and explicitly reported
  "No git commands were used at any point." Independently confirmed by me
  directly on disk afterward: correct =calc.py=/=test_calc.py= content,
  =.pytest_cache=/=__pycache__= present (proving pytest actually ran), and
  =git rev-list --all --count= == 0.
- Isolated test daemon and its child =copilot-language-server= processes
  cleanly killed afterward (=kill-emacs= over =emacsclient=); =ps aux=
  confirmed no orphaned processes from the test daemon remained.

** Decision
- =config.el=: =claude-code-ide-terminal-backend= set to ='ghostel= (was
  ='vterm=).
- =doom doctor= clean (152 packages) after the change.

** Consequences
- claude-code-ide.el's primary agentic workflow, =my/project-ghostel=
  (=SPC p t=/=SPC a t=), and Doom's built-in terminal popup (=SPC o t=) all
  render via ghostel now. =vterm= is disabled (commented out, not deleted,
  in =init.el='s =:term= block) and nothing in this config calls it —
  resolves the =AGENTS.md= "one tool per responsibility" concern this ADR
  originally flagged as open.
- The =window-size-fixed= freeze hack is removed outright (see LOGBOOK) —
  the =PROJECT.org= backlog item asking to re-evaluate it is resolved by
  removing it rather than by confirming it's still needed.
- If ghostel ever exhibits the same unselected-window resize corruption
  vterm had, there's no existing freeze to fall back on — it would need to
  be re-added, scoped to =ghostel-mode-hook= this time (see LOGBOOK for why
  it wasn't pre-emptively ported).
- The headless-daemon smoke-test pattern documented in
  =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org= needs a caveat for any future ghostel-based
  test: it must attach a real X frame (=emacsclient -c -F
  '((window-system . x) (display . ":1"))'=), not just spawn a plain
  =emacs --daemon=. New =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org= entry added
  cross-linking here.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
See Evidence above — this ADR's evidence *is* its validation (a from-scratch
live re-run of the Phase 6 smoke test under the new backend, not just a
config-value check).

** Rollback
Uncomment =vterm= in =init.el='s =:term= block, =doom sync=. In =config.el=:
set =claude-code-ide-terminal-backend= back to ='vterm=; revert
=my/project-ghostel= to =my/project-vterm= (swap =(ghostel)= back to
=(vterm)=, restore the =SPC p t=/=SPC a t= descriptions); re-add the
=window-size-fixed= freeze on =vterm-mode-hook= (see this entry's earlier
git history / LOGBOOK for the exact form). Doom's built-in =SPC o t= popup
reverts to =+vterm/toggle= automatically once =:term vterm= is re-enabled
alongside =:term ghostel= (or on its own if =ghostel= is disabled too) —
no separate binding to undo, per =config/default/+evil-bindings.el='s
=:when=-gated logic.

** Revisit when
- =ghostel= itself regresses or goes unmaintained.
- The same unselected-window resize corruption vterm had shows up under
  ghostel — re-add a =window-size-fixed=-style freeze, scoped to
  =ghostel-mode-hook=, if so.

* ADR-015: Agent gains full git commit/push/history-rewrite authority, gated by per-action confirmation prompt
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-agent-git-authority
:STATUS:   Accepted, live-verified 2026-08-23 (first agent commits and push, and the confirmation prompt itself observed firing by the user)
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-exercised" from "Accepted"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — first commits and
  push made by the agent under this policy: =c46b781=, =2404b35=, =32835ff=,
  then =git push origin main= (=d44ec74..32835ff=, fast-forward).
- State "Live-verified" from "Live-exercised"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — the
  confirmation prompt was observed firing, closing the gap the entry below
  had left open.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-permission-enforcement][ADR-009]] technically blocked the agent from ever running =git
commit=/=push=/=rebase=/=reset=/=clean=, on the premise that
[[id:std-human-authority][std-human-authority]] means the human runs those commands directly via their own
git/Magit, not the agent after an in-session approval. The user explicitly
asked, in this conversation, to change that premise: the agent should be able
to run these commands itself. This ADR records that reversal rather than
letting a permission-file edit stand without the reasoning behind it, per
this repo's own convention (=CLAUDE.md=, the =new-adr= skill) of recording
architectural decisions as ADRs instead of only stating them in conversation.

** Decision drivers
- User's explicit, current-turn instruction — exactly the bar
  =std-human-authority= itself sets for permission changes ("require
  explicit, current-turn approval — a prior approval doesn't carry forward to
  a new category of action").
- Follow-up questions (asked before any edit, since this reverses a
  live-verified technical control) established two independent axes the user
  answered separately:
  - *Scope*: full control — commit, push, and the destructive/history-rewrite
    subcommands (rebase, reset, clean, amend, force-push) all become the
    agent's to run, not just plain commit.
  - *Confirmation model*: the user still wants to be asked before each
    commit/push runs, in the moment — not full autonomy with no check-in.
- Prefer a technical mechanism over a purely instructional one where Claude
  Code supports it, matching the same preference that drove ADR-009
  originally ([[id:ai-architecture][docs/ai/architecture.org]]: "Enforcement should be technical
  ... where available, not merely instructional").

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------------------------------------------------------------|
| Remove the =deny= list outright, rely on instructional confirmation only | Rejected — matches "full control" but drops the technical backstop for "still ask each time"; a purely instructional rule is exactly what ADR-009 judged insufficient in the first place |
| Replace =deny= with =ask= for the same five subcommands | *Accepted* — =ask= forces a Claude Code permission prompt on every matching command, giving "ask each time" a technical mechanism instead of relying only on the agent remembering to propose first; scope (which five subcommands) stays identical to ADR-009, only the disposition changes |
| =allow= outright, no prompt at all | Rejected — this is the "no confirmation needed" option the user did not choose |

** Evidence
- ADR-009's own candidates table considered =permissions.ask= and rejected it
  as "the wrong shape per =std-human-authority=" — correct under the old
  policy, where the human was meant to run these commands directly. That
  premise is exactly what this ADR changes, so the previously-rejected
  option becomes the right fit now.
- Current =.claude/settings.json= (pre-edit) confirmed to hold only the
  =permissions.deny= block from ADR-009 — five entries, one per subcommand
  prefix (=git commit=, =push=, =rebase=, =reset=, =clean=), each using the
  =Bash(<prefix> *)= wildcard form that also matches the bare prefix with no
  arguments (so =git commit --amend= and =git push --force= are covered by
  the existing =commit=/=push= entries — no new prefixes needed for "full
  control", the five from ADR-009 already cover the full stated scope).

** Decision
=.claude/settings.json= changes from =deny= to =ask= for the same five
subcommands:
#+begin_src json
{
  "permissions": {
    "ask": [
      "Bash(git commit *)",
      "Bash(git push *)",
      "Bash(git rebase *)",
      "Bash(git reset *)",
      "Bash(git clean *)"
    ]
  }
}
#+end_src
=AGENTS.md=, =.claude/rules/security.md=, [[id:std-no-commit][docs/standards.org]] (=std-no-commit=),
[[id:std-human-authority][std-human-authority]], [[id:ai-architecture][docs/ai/architecture.org]], and =docs/ai/workflows.org= updated
to match: the agent may run these five subcommands, but must propose the
specific command (and, for commit, the message; for push, the target) in
conversation and get an explicit go-ahead before running it — a past
approval doesn't carry forward to the next commit/push.

** Consequences
- The agent can now commit, push, and rewrite history in this repo, which it
  technically could not do before. The =ask= prompt is the only remaining
  technical backstop — if the human reflexively approves prompts without
  reading them, the practical protection reduces to instructional discipline
  alone, the exact failure mode ADR-009 was written to close. This is an
  accepted, explicit trade-off per the user's current-turn instruction, not
  an oversight.
- =std-human-authority='s "the user remains responsible for commits and
  pushes, always" no longer holds as stated; narrowed to "the user must
  explicitly approve each commit/push/rewrite in the moment."
- [[id:adr-permission-enforcement][ADR-009]] is superseded, not deleted — its Context/Decision-drivers/Evidence
  remain an accurate record of why the technical block existed from
  2026-07-20 to 2026-08-18, and its Rollback section is exactly the path back
  if this is reverted.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =jq -e '.permissions.ask[]' .claude/settings.json= — valid JSON, all five
  rules present, =deny= key absent.
- *First real commit/push under this policy, 2026-08-23* (the live validation
  this section previously asked for). The agent proposed each command in
  conversation with its full text, the user approved commits and push
  explicitly in-the-moment, and three commits plus one push went through:
  =c46b781=, =2404b35=, =32835ff=, pushed as =d44ec74..32835ff=. The
  instructional half of the policy — propose-then-await-approval, no approval
  carried forward between actions — held in practice.
- *Technical enforcement confirmed 2026-08-23*, by the deliberate test the
  previous version of this section proposed: the agent ran
  =git commit --allow-empty= with no prior approval for that specific command,
  and the user observed the confirmation prompt appear and approved it by
  hand. So =.claude/settings.json='s =ask= rules do match and do gate in
  practice, not merely in configuration.
- *Method note for anyone re-testing this:* the observation has to come from
  the user. Approval prompts render on the user's side, not the agent's, so an
  auto-approved command and a hand-approved one are indistinguishable in the
  agent's tool output — an agent cannot self-verify this ADR's enforcement
  half, and should not claim to have done so.
- The two empty commits used for the test (=cf4ad3f=, =1b03689=) were
  discarded afterwards and never pushed.
- One unrelated denial also fired during the same session (a compound
  =sed ... > tracked-file && git add= call, blocked by the harness's auto-mode
  classifier rather than by these =ask= rules). Noted only so a future reader
  doesn't conflate the two mechanisms: the classifier blocks outright, these
  rules prompt.

** Rollback
Restore ADR-009's =.claude/settings.json= (=deny= block with the same five
entries) and revert the wording in =AGENTS.md=, =.claude/rules/security.md=,
=docs/standards.org= (=std-no-commit=, =std-human-authority=),
=docs/ai/architecture.org=, and =docs/ai/workflows.org= back to "the agent
never commits or pushes." Re-open [[id:adr-permission-enforcement][ADR-009]]'s status if so.

** Revisit when
- A commit, push, or history-rewrite run under this policy causes an actual
  mistake (wrong message, unwanted push, unwanted rewrite) — signal to
  reconsider the trade-off accepted above.
- The user wants to tighten back to no-agent-commits at any point — that's a
  reversion to ADR-009, not a new decision.
- Claude Code's =ask=/=deny= permission-rule semantics change in a way that
  could silently narrow or widen what these patterns match or how the prompt
  behaves.

* ADR-016: Adopt a curated mattpocock-skills subset as the default engineering toolkit
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-mattpocock-skills-adoption
:STATUS:   Accepted, implemented
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:

** Context
Commit =2a4e8ca= ("Set up mattpocock engineering skills config") installed
the =mattpocock-skills= Claude Code plugin and wired up only its meta layer —
issue-tracker conventions (=docs/agents/issue-tracker.md=), a triage-label
mapping (=docs/agents/triage-labels.md=), and a domain-docs pointer
(=docs/agents/domain.md=) — via =CLAUDE.md='s "## Agent skills" section. None
of the plugin's actual engineering-workflow skills (=tdd=,
=diagnosing-bugs=, =domain-modeling=, =codebase-design=, =code-review=,
=prototype=, =research=, =resolving-merge-conflicts=, =wizard=,
=writing-for-agents=, =grilling=) were ever adopted, and no ADR recorded that
partial decision or reconciled it against [[id:adr-skill-policy][ADR-010]] ("scaffold ADR-writing
only, defer the rest").

User request (2026-08-18, via =/mattpocock-skills:grill-me=): "I want this
repo to better reflect Matt Pocock's philosophy." A structured grill session
clarified this to mean: adopt a curated subset of the mattpocock-skills
plugin as the repo's documented default toolkit, scoped to the meta-docs/
AI-tooling layer only (not the Elisp config itself), so future feature work
goes more smoothly.

** Decision drivers
- User's explicit motivation: repo cleanup so future feature additions are
  smoother, not a general aspiration to maximize skill adoption.
- [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]] — curate rather than blanket-adopt all eleven skills.
- =CLAUDE.md='s own rule: any new architectural decision gets an ADR, not just
  conversation.
- =AGENTS.md='s "one tool per responsibility" rule, tested directly by the
  =code-review= name collision (see Evidence).
- Existing, deliberate decisions this must not silently reopen:
  =docs/agents/domain.md='s "no separate CONTEXT.md" call, and
  =docs/ai/workflows.org='s test-after (not test-first) default.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------------------------------------------------------------|
| Adopt all 11 mattpocock-skills as default toolkit | Rejected — =wizard=/=prototype=/=research=/=resolving-merge-conflicts=/=grilling= aren't obviously recurring in a personal Emacs-config repo; blanket adoption is the scope creep [[id:adr-skill-policy][ADR-010]] already pushed back on |
| Adopt none; leave the plugin installed but undocumented | Rejected — leaves the gap this ADR exists to close (partial, unreconciled adoption) unresolved |
| Curate a subset mapped to recurring repo work, scoped to the meta-docs layer only | *Accepted* |

** Evidence
- =grep -rniE "tdd\|domain.modeling\|codebase.design\|diagnosing.bugs\|deep module"=
  across all =.org=/=.md= files in the repo returned zero hits before this
  change — none of the engineering skills were referenced anywhere.
- Two distinct =code-review=-named skills are simultaneously available in
  this session: the built-in =code-review= skill (correctness/simplification/
  efficiency findings, =--fix=/=--comment=/=ultra= modes — already referenced
  by =CLAUDE.md='s "Session-specific guidance" section for =/code-review
  ultra=) and =mattpocock-skills:code-review= (parallel Standards-vs-Spec
  review). Confirmed directly against the session's available-skills
  listing.
- =docs/agents/domain.md= line 6 ("This repo does not use the CONTEXT.md /
  docs/adr/ convention") and =docs/ai/workflows.org='s "Full agentic coding"
  section ([[id:wf-agent][wf-agent]], plan → edit → test → Ediff review) were both read in
  full and confirmed to describe positions this decision must not silently
  override.
- =docs/roadmap.org= and =PROJECT.org= were checked (grep for
  "MCP"/"mattpocock"/"agent skills") — confirmed Phase 1 is fully closed and
  neither file tracks this as open work.

** Decision
Adopt six of the eleven mattpocock-skills as this repo's documented default
engineering toolkit: =tdd=, =mattpocock-skills:code-review=,
=codebase-design=, =writing-for-agents=, =diagnosing-bugs=,
=domain-modeling=. Not adopted into the default toolkit (still installed,
just not documented as go-to): =prototype=, =research=,
=resolving-merge-conflicts=, =wizard=, =grilling=.

Specifically:
- New =docs/agents/skills.md= documents the six, their scope, and how each
  reconciles with existing repo conventions.
- =CLAUDE.md='s "## Agent skills" section gets a new "### Engineering
  skills" entry pointing to it.
- =tdd= is documented as the *recommended* approach for well-specified
  feature work, not a replacement of =docs/ai/workflows.org='s existing
  test-after default ([[id:wf-agent][wf-agent]]) — exploratory/UI work keeps the current
  workflow.
- =domain-modeling= is scoped to sharpening terminology within the existing
  =docs/*.org=/ADR log; =docs/agents/domain.md='s "no separate CONTEXT.md"
  decision stands, unreopened.
- The =code-review= name collision is resolved by treating both skills as
  genuinely distinct (defect/cleanup-finder vs. Standards-vs-Spec
  conformance) and documenting both, with =mattpocock-skills:code-review=
  always invoked by its full namespaced name to avoid ambiguity — satisfying
  =AGENTS.md='s "clearly distinct, frequently-useful role" bar for a second
  tool.
- This is a standalone documentation update — no new =docs/roadmap.org=/
  =PROJECT.org= phase opened for it (Phase 1 is fully closed; this isn't a
  multi-step initiative on that scale).
- Scope is the meta-docs/AI-tooling layer only (=CLAUDE.md=,
  =docs/agents/*.md=, =docs/ai/workflows.org=, =docs/decisions.org=) —
  =init.el=/=packages.el=/=config.el= and the Elisp config itself are
  untouched.

This *extends*, not contradicts, [[id:adr-skill-policy][ADR-010]]: ADR-010 scoped "don't build
more custom skills"; this decision adopts a pre-built third-party plugin's
skills, a different category of work.

** Consequences
- Agents working in this repo now have a documented, curated set of
  engineering skills to reach for, with explicit guidance on scope and when
  each applies — closing the partial, undocumented-adoption gap this ADR
  exists to record.
- Two =code-review=-named skills coexist by design; anyone (human or agent)
  invoking "code-review" from memory must disambiguate — mitigated by
  requiring the full namespaced form for mattpocock's version in all docs.
- =docs/ai/workflows.org='s [[id:wf-agent][wf-agent]] section gains a note about =tdd= as a
  recommended-not-default path; the underlying workflow itself is otherwise
  unchanged.
- Five mattpocock-skills (=prototype=, =research=,
  =resolving-merge-conflicts=, =wizard=, =grilling=) remain installed via the
  plugin but outside the documented default toolkit — available if a future
  need arises, per the Revisit condition below.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =grep -n "tdd\|codebase-design\|writing-for-agents\|diagnosing-bugs\|domain-modeling\|mattpocock-skills:code-review" docs/agents/skills.md CLAUDE.md=
  confirms all six are referenced.
- Not live-tested: no session in this repo has yet invoked any of the six
  skills end-to-end since this decision landed; the first real use of each is
  its live validation.

** Rollback
Remove =docs/agents/skills.md=, revert the =CLAUDE.md= "### Engineering
skills" entry and the =docs/ai/workflows.org= =tdd= note, and this decision
reduces back to the pre-existing partial state (plugin installed, only
issue-tracker/triage-labels/domain-docs wired up) that ADR-010 itself never
anticipated.

** Revisit when
- Any of the five non-adopted skills (=prototype=, =research=,
  =resolving-merge-conflicts=, =wizard=, =grilling=) turns out to recur often
  enough in real use to be worth curating in too.
- The =code-review= name collision causes an actual mistake (wrong skill
  invoked) despite the full-name convention — signal to reconsider the
  "pick one as canonical" candidate.
- This repo opens a new roadmap phase for AI-tooling work, at which point
  this ADR's "standalone update" framing should be revisited alongside it.

* ADR-017: Org Agenda and Capture basics
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-org-agenda-capture-basics
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (2026-08-23); one stated behaviour corrected 2026-08-30 — see LOGBOOK
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Correction [2026-08-30 Sun] — *this ADR's description of =org-agenda-files=
  was wrong from the day it was written, and the live verification below did
  not catch it.* The setting was =(list org-directory)=, described here and in
  =config.org= as covering "the whole pre-existing =org-directory= tree". Org
  expands a *directory* entry non-recursively: it takes that directory's own
  =.org= files and does not descend. Measured in a headless daemon,
  =(org-agenda-files)= returned exactly two paths — =~/org/todo.org= and
  =~/org/doom.org= — out of 68 =.org= files in the tree. Invisible to every
  agenda view: =work/= (15 files), =personal/= (3), =calendar/= (2), =daily/=,
  =mtg/=, =test/=.

  Why the 2026-08-23 verification passed anyway: it opened the agenda and ran
  the capture templates, both of which target =todo.org= — one of the two files
  that *were* reachable. The test exercised the working half. Worth keeping as
  a method point: "the feature worked when I tried it" only covers the path
  tried, and a scope claim needs a scope measurement, not a smoke test.

  Fixed in =config.org= by listing =org-directory= plus its immediate
  subdirectories; =(org-agenda-files)= now returns 26 files from 10 entries.
  Depth-1 and evaluated at load, so a new subdirectory needs a reload. See
  [[id:roadmap-phase-7][Phase 7]].
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — user reloaded
  Emacs, ran the capture templates and opened the agenda; both behaved as
  designed. =doom doctor= clean the same day (50 modules, 158 packages).
:END:

** Context
=roadmap.org='s Future-phases list has carried "Org Agenda and personal
project management" and "Capture and knowledge management" as named-but-
unscoped bullets since Phase 1. The user asked to start exploring more of
what Doom offers; after a scoping conversation, both were picked as the
starting point, provided the work goes through a real, narrowly-scoped
phase rather than growing informally — =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-
creep= explicitly says "don't build the full Org Agenda system until its
own phase." This ADR *is* that phase, deliberately bounded to agenda-files,
TODO states, and capture templates — no custom agenda views, no refile
automation, no habit tracking.

A related gap surfaced while scoping this: Vulpea (note management,
installed the same day as this ADR) was never itself given an ADR, and is
missing from =docs/inventory.org='s package table. Both are now fixed —
the inventory row below, and the ADR itself backfilled separately as
[[id:adr-vulpea-note-management][ADR-019]].

** Decision drivers
- =std-no-scope-creep=: agenda/capture basics only, not the full system.
- =org-directory= (=~/org/=) already exists as the user's real, years-old
  personal Org tree (=doom.org=, =todo.org= from 2020, plus =marxism-
  notes/=, =work/=, =personal/=, =mtg/=, =test/= subdirectories) — this
  predates the project entirely and isn't something to redesign or migrate.
- TODO-keyword consistency: this repo already has a live convention
  (=PROJECT.org='s =#+TODO: TODO(t) IN-PROGRESS(i) BLOCKED(b) | DONE(d)
  REJECTED(r)=) — reuse it rather than inventing a second vocabulary.
- Vulpea's own docstring for =vulpea-create= explicitly recommends
  =org-capture= with a =vulpea-compatible= template for interactive note
  capture, and its docs (=docs/user-guide.org= "Quick Capture Integration")
  give the exact target-function pattern to use.

** Candidates
| Decision point | Candidates | Fate |
|-----------------+-------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------|
| Agenda files scope | New =inbox.org= only vs. all of =org-directory= | User explicitly chose all of =org-directory=, informed that =doom.org= has a stray non-task "TODO" headline that will now surface in the live agenda — a deliberate, informed choice, not an oversight |
| TODO keywords | Stock Org =TODO=/=DONE= vs. this repo's =PROJECT.org= convention | User chose to reuse =PROJECT.org='s convention, for consistency across this repo |
| Capture targets | New =inbox.org= vs. the pre-existing "Inbox" headline already in =~/org/todo.org= | Reused the existing headline — =todo.org= already had an "Inbox" top-level heading (with a stray note-to-self about =SPC X=/org-capture, ironically), so a second parallel inbox file would only fragment the same concept |
| Capture scope | Tasks only / tasks+notes / tasks+notes+Vulpea-linked note | User chose tasks+notes+Vulpea-linked (the third creates a real ID-bearing, backlink-capable note, not just a plain entry) |

** Evidence
- Read =docs/standards.org= (=std-no-scope-creep=) and confirmed the rule
  directly forbids building the full Agenda system outside its own phase —
  this ADR exists specifically to satisfy that rule, not route around it.
- Read Doom's own =+evil-bindings.el=
  (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/config/default/+evil-bindings.el=)
  and confirmed =SPC X= and =SPC n n= already resolve to =org-capture=, and
  =SPC n a=/=SPC o a= already resolve to =org-agenda= — no new keybindings
  needed for either entry point, only the underlying variables.
- Read Vulpea's own source (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/vulpea/
  vulpea.el=, =vulpea-create='s docstring) and its
  =docs/user-guide.org= "Quick Capture Integration" section, confirming the
  =(file (lambda () (vulpea-note-path (vulpea-create TITLE))))= pattern is
  the package's own recommended way to wire capture into it — not a
  workaround.
- =ls ~/org/= and =find ~/org -iname "*.org"= confirmed the pre-existing
  personal tree described above, including that =~/org/todo.org= already
  has an "Inbox" headline and =~/org/doom.org= has a stray =* TODO
  Referencing [gist]...= headline that isn't a real task.
- =grep -n -i vulpea docs/inventory.org= returned nothing — confirmed the
  missing-inventory-entry gap named in Context.

** Decision
- =config.el=, wrapped in a single =(after! org ...)= block:
  - =org-agenda-files= set to =(list org-directory)=.
  - =org-todo-keywords= set to match =PROJECT.org='s sequence.
  - =org-capture-templates=: ="t"= (Task) and ="n"= (Note), both
    =file+headline= into =~/org/todo.org='s "Inbox" heading; ="v"= (Vulpea
    note), a =plain= template whose file target calls =(vulpea-create
    (read-string "Title: "))= and returns its path, per Vulpea's own
    recommended pattern.
- =docs/inventory.org=: added the missing =vulpea= row to the "Explicit
  packages" table (see the entry there for the pinned commit).

** Consequences
- Doom's existing =SPC X=/=SPC n n= (capture) and =SPC n a=/=SPC o a=
  (agenda) entry points now do something real; nothing new was bound.
- The live agenda view will include =doom.org='s stray "TODO" headline per
  the user's explicit choice (see Candidates) — not a bug if it shows up.
- =~/org/todo.org= gains new entries under "Inbox" whenever ="t"=/="n"=
  capture is used, interleaved with the pre-existing stray note already
  there; nothing is deleted or restructured.
- This does not touch =work/=, =personal/=, =marxism-notes/=, =mtg/=, or
  =test/= — those stay exactly as they were, out of scope.
- The Vulpea-adoption gap named in Context is now closed by
  [[id:adr-vulpea-note-management][ADR-019]] (backfilled separately, not as part of this ADR's own scope).

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
Structural only so far: the =after! org= block was written and reasoned
through against Doom's actual default bindings and Vulpea's actual source,
but not yet exercised in the user's live session (no capture actually run,
no agenda view actually opened). =doom sync=/=doom doctor= confirm the
config loads cleanly; see the Validation note this ADR's companion,
[[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]], shares the same live-verification gap.

** Rollback
Delete the =(after! org ...)= block added to =config.el=. =org-agenda-
files=/=org-todo-keywords=/=org-capture-templates= all revert to Doom's
stock defaults (=org-agenda-files= empty, stock =TODO=/=DONE=, stock
single "Todo" capture template). Nothing in =~/org/= needs to be touched —
this only changes what points at the tree, not the tree itself.

** Revisit when
- The user actually uses capture/agenda for a while and wants a real
  agenda view (custom block agenda, tags search, etc.) — that's exactly
  the "full system" =std-no-scope-creep= deferred, and would be its own
  follow-on ADR.
- =doom.org='s stray "TODO" headline turns out to actually bother the user
  in practice — trivial fix (reword the headline, or narrow =org-agenda-
  files= at that point), not a reason to hold up this ADR now.

* ADR-018: Hand-rolled Vulpea dailies and backlinks buffer
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (2026-08-23 — dailies and the backlinks buffer exercised in the user's own session)
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — user created and
  visited daily notes and used the backlinks buffer. This closes the specific
  risk this ADR's Evidence reasoned about but could not test: the buffer
  refreshes on both triggers (window-selection change *and* in-window buffer
  change), so a =vulpea-find= jump inside the same window updates it rather
  than leaving stale backlinks on screen.
:END:

** Context
While scoping [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]], the user asked for
"everything org-roam has to offer" on top of the newly-installed Vulpea.
Reading Vulpea's actual source found two real gaps against org-roam:
dailies/journal notes (=org-roam-dailies=) and a persistent backlinks side
buffer (=org-roam-buffer=) — neither exists anywhere in Vulpea. Installing
org-roam alongside Vulpea to get them was rejected outright: Vulpea was
already chosen over org-roam specifically to keep "one tool per
responsibility" (=AGENTS.md=, =docs/standards.org= =std-no-duplication=),
and running both would reopen that settled choice for no clear gain, since
they serve the same role. That leaves hand-rolling both features on top of
Vulpea's public API as the only option that doesn't reopen the org-roam
question — offered to the user explicitly as "real new code, not just
config, worth its own ADR section," and chosen.

** Decision drivers
- =std-no-duplication=: don't add org-roam as a second note-graph tool
  alongside Vulpea.
- The user explicitly chose the "build both, hand-rolled" option after
  being told plainly this means new =config.el= logic, not configuration
  of something that already exists.
- Whatever gets built should stay a Vulpea note under the hood (real ID,
  indexed, backlink-capable via =vulpea-find=/=vulpea-insert=) rather than
  a parallel, un-indexed file format — otherwise it isn't really "on top
  of Vulpea."

** Candidates
| Feature | Candidates | Fate |
|----------+---------------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------|
| Dailies | Skip / daily-only / hand-rolled daily+backlinks (both) | User chose "build both, hand-rolled" over the two lighter options offered first |
| Daily file naming | Vulpea's own =${timestamp}_${slug}= default vs. an explicit =daily/YYYY-MM-DD.org= path | Explicit date-based path chosen — timestamp+slug is right for atomic notes, wrong for one-per-calendar-day journal entries that need to be found by date, not searched by title |
| Backlinks refresh trigger | =window-selection-change-functions= only vs. that plus =window-buffer-change-functions= | Both — reasoned through the failure mode of using only the first (see Evidence): visiting a different note in the *same* window via =vulpea-find=/=vulpea-find-backlink= would never trigger a refresh, which is the single most common way notes actually get visited |

** Evidence
- Grepped Vulpea's source
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/vulpea/*.el=) for
  =backlink=/=daily=/=journal=/=buffer=-related public functions: no
  dailies concept anywhere; the only backlinks-related public function is
  =vulpea-find-backlink= (jump-to, not a live panel).
- Confirmed the underlying public query API needed for a backlinks buffer
  does exist and is loaded transitively: =vulpea-db-query-links-to=,
  =vulpea-db-query-by-ids=, and =vulpea-db-query-by-file-path= are all
  defined in =vulpea-db-query.el=, which =vulpea.el= pulls in transitively
  via =(require 'vulpea-select)= etc. (traced the require graph directly;
  =vulpea.el= itself calls all three of these internally, at
  =vulpea.el:787= and =vulpea.el:2028=, confirming they're safe to call
  once Vulpea is loaded).
- Confirmed =vulpea-create='s file-name argument is a template relative to
  =vulpea--default-directory= (defaults to =org-directory=), not required
  to be the package's own default pattern — passing an explicit
  =daily/<date>.org= template is a supported, not a hacked-around, use.
- Reasoned explicitly through the single-hook failure mode before writing
  the refresh logic: =window-selection-change-functions= fires on
  =select-window= (switching windows) but *not* when the buffer inside the
  already-selected window changes (e.g. =find-file=, or anything
  =vulpea-visit= does) — the exact case triggered by using =vulpea-find=/
  =vulpea-find-backlink= from within a note. Added
  =window-buffer-change-functions= alongside it to cover that case; both
  share the same handler and signature.
- Not yet live-tested: no capture run, no daily note created, no side
  buffer opened in the user's real session. See Validation.

** Decision
=config.el= additions (both under the existing "Vulpea" section, after the
=use-package! vulpea= block):
- *Dailies*: =my/vulpea-daily-today=, =my/vulpea-daily-date=,
  =my/vulpea-daily-previous=, =my/vulpea-daily-next=, backed by
  =vulpea-create= with an explicit =daily/YYYY-MM-DD.org= file-name
  template and a ="daily"= tag; previous/next scan the =daily/= directory
  by filename (ISO dates sort lexically = chronologically) rather than
  querying the Vulpea database, keeping them simple and independent of
  index freshness.
- *Backlinks buffer*: =my/vulpea-buffer-toggle= opens a right-side window
  (=*vulpea-buffer*=, its own =special-mode=-derived major mode) rendering
  the current note's title and incoming links (source note as a clickable
  button via =insert-text-button=, plus the link's description if any). No
  unlinked-references section, no citation backlinks — scoped to exactly
  what was asked for. Refreshes via both
  =window-selection-change-functions= and =window-buffer-change-functions=
  (see Evidence), gated on the shown note's ID actually changing so it
  doesn't re-render on every window/buffer event.
- New leader bindings under the existing =(n v)= vulpea prefix: ="j"=
  (today), ="J"= (pick date), ="p"=/="n"= (previous/next daily), ="B"=
  (toggle backlinks buffer). ="d"= was already taken by
  =vulpea-doctor=, hence ="j"=/="J"= for dailies rather than a =d=-based
  mnemonic.

** Consequences
- Daily notes are real Vulpea notes: indexed, findable via =vulpea-find=,
  linkable via =vulpea-insert=, backlink-capable — not a separate,
  un-indexed journal format.
- The backlinks buffer is intentionally thinner than =org-roam-buffer=:
  no unlinked-references, no per-link context/preview beyond the
  description text, no citation-link support. Documented here as a
  deliberate boundary, not a bug to file later without checking this ADR
  first.
- Two more custom, hand-maintained functions' worth of surface area in
  =config.el= that a future Vulpea upgrade could break (e.g. if
  =vulpea-db-query-links-to='s plist shape ever changes) — accepted
  knowingly, per the Decision drivers, as the cost of not running a second
  note-graph tool.
- =daily/= is a new subdirectory under =org-directory=; since [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]
  put all of =org-directory= in =org-agenda-files=, any daily note that
  happens to contain a TODO-keyword heading will also surface in the
  agenda — consistent with that ADR's already-accepted scope, not a new
  decision.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above. Installing org-roam alongside Vulpea specifically
to get these two features was the implicit third option and was never
seriously on the table, per the Decision drivers.

** Validation
Structural only: read Vulpea's actual source to confirm every function
called here is real and public (or safely transitively loaded), and reasoned
through the backlinks-refresh failure mode concretely rather than assuming a
single hook would do. Not yet live-verified in the user's own session —
none of =my/vulpea-daily-today=, the previous/next navigation, or
=my/vulpea-buffer-toggle= has actually been run yet. =doom doctor= confirms
the config loads without error, which is necessary but not sufficient
evidence this behaves correctly in use.

** Rollback
Delete the two new =config.el= sections (dailies functions, backlinks
buffer functions) and the five new =map!= entries they added under =(n
v)=. Any daily notes already created under =~/org/daily/= remain as
ordinary Vulpea notes (nothing about removing this code deletes user
content) but lose their dedicated navigation commands.

** Revisit when
- The user actually uses dailies/backlinks for a while and wants
  org-roam-buffer's fuller feature set (unlinked references, richer
  preview) — that's a real scope expansion of *this* ADR's boundary, not a
  bug in what's here now.
- =vulpea-db-query-links-to='s or =vulpea-db-query-by-ids='s return shape
  changes in a future Vulpea upgrade (watch =CHANGELOG.org= in the
  =vulpea= repo) — =my/vulpea-buffer--render= depends on the current
  plist keys (=:source=, =:description=) directly.
- Live-verification actually happens in the user's session — update
  =:STATUS:= on both this ADR and [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]] together once it does,
  per this repo's =:LOGBOOK:= convention.

* ADR-019: Vulpea for note management (retroactive)
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-vulpea-note-management
:STATUS:   Accepted, implemented; not live-verified as its own entry; amended 2026-08-23 by [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]]
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Amended [2026-08-23 Sun]: this ADR's choice of Vulpea *instead of* org-roam
  no longer holds. The user asked for both; org-roam is now enabled via
  =:lang org +roam=. See [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]] for the reversal, its cost, and what
  Vulpea keeps. The reasoning below about why Vulpea was chosen stands as
  written — only its exclusivity is superseded. Note also that ADR-027 found
  Vulpea v2 no longer depends on org-roam at all (v1 did), so the two are
  independent databases rather than layers.
:END:

** Context
*Amended 2026-08-23 — see [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]]: org-roam now runs alongside Vulpea.*

This is a *retroactive* ADR — =vulpea= was already installed and configured
in the "Added Vulpea" commit (2026-08-18, same day), which added
=(package! vulpea)=, a =use-package!= block, and =(n v)= leader bindings to
=config.el=, but never got its own =decisions.org= entry. The gap was found
and named while scoping [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]] (its Context/Consequences), and this
ADR fills it in, backfilling reasoning that wasn't captured at decision
time. Per this repo's own =new-adr= skill guidance ("a thin ADR with real
gaps flagged is better than a confident one with fabricated evidence"),
this entry is explicit about what's genuinely known versus what's honest
reconstruction from Vulpea's own documentation rather than a record of the
original conversation, which this session has no access to.

*What's actually known*, verbatim from the =config.el= comment written at
install time: Vulpea indexes org entries (file- or heading-level) carrying
an =ID= property into its own SQLite database — titles, tags, =id:= links,
description-list metadata — "independently of org-roam (not installed
here; per Vulpea's own docs the two can coexist but serve the same role,
and this config keeps one tool per responsibility)."

*What's not known*: no record of org-roam (or org-node, or plain
=org-id=/=occur=) actually being evaluated and rejected point-by-point at
decision time — only the one-line rationale above. The Candidates/Evidence
sections below reconstruct the objective picture from Vulpea's own
=docs/comparison.org= (which explicitly compares itself to org-roam and
org-node), not from a record of what was actually weighed on the day.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md=/=docs/standards.org= =std-no-duplication=: one note-graph
  tool at a time, matching this repo's existing "one chat client, one
  completion engine, one primary agentic frontend" pattern.
- =std-org-first=: whatever tool is used should work with real Org
  capabilities (IDs, property drawers), not a parallel non-Org format —
  all three candidates below satisfy this equally.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|------------+---------------------------------------------------------------|
| org-roam | Not installed. Per Vulpea's own =docs/comparison.org=: the established, largest-ecosystem choice ("Roam Research for Emacs"), best if you want =roam:= links, third-party packages (org-roam-ui, org-roam-bibtex), or a complete out-of-the-box application. Rejected implicitly via "one tool per responsibility," not via a recorded feature-by-feature comparison |
| org-node | Not evaluated at all — no mention anywhere in the =config.el= comment or any doc from install time. Per Vulpea's own comparison: "org-roam but fast," best for minimal migration effort and vanilla-Org feel; genuinely absent from this decision's record |
| Vulpea | *Accepted*. Per its own =docs/comparison.org=: positions itself as "a foundation for building note-based applications" rather than an application itself — stable API (=vulpea-note= struct, query functions) over exposed SQL, async/multi-Emacs-session design, structured metadata via description lists. Matches this config's existing preference for foundational, composable tools (=gptel=, =claude-code-ide.el=) over monolithic ones |
| Plain =org-id= + =occur=/=consult-ripgrep=, no indexing tool | Not seriously considered as far as the record shows — this config already uses =consult=/=vertico= for completion generally, so a dedicated indexed-note-finder is a natural fit, not a redundant layer |

** Evidence
- =grep -n -i "vulpea\|org-roam" docs/decisions.org= (before this entry)
  returned nothing — confirmed the gap directly rather than assuming.
- =grep -n -i "vulpea" docs/inventory.org= (before [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]'s fix) also
  returned nothing — the package-table gap this ADR's sibling already
  patched.
- Read =config.el='s own Vulpea comment in full (the only contemporaneous
  reasoning that exists) — quoted verbatim in Context above.
- Read Vulpea's own =docs/comparison.org=
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/vulpea/docs/comparison.org=,
  dated 2026-07-08, matching the installed commit =2ea4a8b= from
  2026-08-17) in full for the objective org-roam/org-node/vulpea
  differences summarized in Candidates above — this is the package's own
  stated positioning, not this session's opinion.
- =cd .../vulpea && git log -1 -- docs/comparison.org= confirmed the doc's
  dating lines up with the installed version, so it reflects the same
  Vulpea this config actually runs, not a stale/future description.

** Decision
No new action — this ADR formally records a decision already implemented:
=packages.el= has =(package! vulpea)=; =config.el= has the =use-package!
vulpea= block (deferred to =doom-first-input-hook=,
=vulpea-db-autosync-mode= enabled) and the =(n v)= leader prefix
(=vulpea-find=/=-find-backlink=/=-insert=/=-db-sync-full-scan=/=-doctor=,
later extended by [[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]]'s dailies/backlinks-buffer bindings).
=docs/inventory.org='s package table now has the corresponding row (added
alongside [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]).

** Consequences
- Note management now has a documented decision record, closing the gap
  named in [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]'s Context/Consequences.
- Because org-node specifically was never actually evaluated (see
  Candidates), this ADR cannot honestly claim Vulpea beats it for this
  user's needs — only that Vulpea satisfies =std-no-duplication= and its
  own stated design goals line up with this config's existing preferences.
  If org-node's speed/vanilla-Org advantages ever matter in practice,
  that's a real open question this ADR doesn't close.
- [[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]]'s hand-rolled dailies/backlinks-buffer work rests on this
  decision; if Vulpea were ever swapped out, that ADR's Rollback section
  would need revisiting too, not just this one.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above — with the explicit caveat that org-node's
rejection is by omission (never evaluated), not by comparison, unlike
org-roam's.

** Validation
None beyond =doom sync=/=doom doctor= being clean at install time (implicit
in the original commit landing without incident) and again after
[[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]/[[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]]'s additions. No feature-by-feature validation that
Vulpea outperforms the alternatives for this user's actual note collection
— that would require using it for a while, which is exactly what
[[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]'s live-verification step (still pending) is for.

** Rollback
Remove =(package! vulpea)= from =packages.el=; remove the =use-package!
vulpea= block and the =(n v)= =map!= block from =config.el= (including
[[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]]'s additions to the same prefix, which depend on Vulpea's API
directly); remove the =vulpea= row from =docs/inventory.org='s package
table; =doom sync=. Any notes already created via Vulpea (including daily
notes) remain as plain Org files with =ID= properties — nothing about
removing Vulpea deletes their content, only the indexing/navigation layer
on top of them.

** Revisit when
- org-node's speed or vanilla-Org-capture advantages become a concrete pain
  point (large collection, slow minibuffer) — this ADR never actually
  compared them, so that comparison would need to happen for real at that
  point, not be assumed already settled.
- Someone wants =vulpea-ui=/=vulpea-journal= (Vulpea's own richer sidebar/
  journal companions) instead of the hand-rolled equivalents in
  [[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]] — worth checking those before extending the hand-rolled
  versions further, since they may now cover the same ground more
  completely.

* ADR-020: =SPC o L= external-app launcher, xdotool-based raise-or-launch
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-app-launcher
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified at =SPC o l=; re-prefixed to =SPC o L= by ADR-028 and not re-verified at the new prefix
:DATE:     2026-08-13
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Amended [2026-08-23 Sat] — prefix moved =SPC o l= → =SPC o L= by
  [[id:adr-llm-module-owns-ai-keys][ADR-028]]. =SPC o l= belongs to =:tools llm= since [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] enabled it, and
  this launcher's =(:prefix ("o l" . "launch"))= form was silently replacing
  the module's entire gptel keymap — measured, not inferred. The launcher
  mechanism (=my/launch-or-focus=) is untouched; only the prefix changed, so
  the live-verification below still covers the behaviour, not the new keys.
- Note taken on [2026-08-13] \\
  User restarted Emacs and confirmed =SPC o l f= now works. Root cause of
  the earlier failure was [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]] as suspected — the
  long-running daemon hadn't picked up the =config.el= change, not a bug in
  =my/launch-or-focus= or the =map!= block. =:STATUS:= moved to
  =live-verified=. The =xdotool=/=libpoppler-glib-dev= evidence retraction
  above stands as its own separate, still-open item — that discrepancy's
  cause remains undetermined regardless of this fix.
- Note taken on [2026-08-13] \\
  User reported =SPC o l f= "did not work," and directly corrected two
  Evidence claims below: =xdotool= and =libpoppler-glib-dev= were *both*
  already installed on the real machine at the time the agent checked, and
  the "sandboxed shell restricted =PATH=" explanation the agent gave for the
  =xdotool= discrepancy was wrong ("it has nothing to do with path" — user's
  words). The agent's =dpkg -s=/=which=/=command -v= checks in this session
  do not reliably reflect this machine's real installed-package state; root
  cause of the discrepancy is *not determined* — the agent is not
  re-asserting a corrected cause, only retracting the wrong one. See
  [[id:tshoot-agent-shell-system-state][troubleshooting.org]] for the
  general write-up. Evidence bullets below left visibly struck through
  rather than deleted, per this repo's "don't imply more confidence than the
  work supports" convention — the *retraction* is the record, not a quietly
  fixed-up version of events.

  Most likely actual cause of the reported =SPC o l f= failure: the
  already-documented [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]]
  gotcha — the user's long-running Emacs daemon does not auto-reload
  =config.el=, and =doom sync='s own output ("Restart Emacs for changes to
  take effect") was not relayed as an action item when this ADR was first
  written up. Told the user to restart Emacs (or =M-x doom/reload=) and
  retest. Not yet confirmed either way.
:END:

** Context
User wants leader-key bindings to open external GUI apps (Firefox, Discord,
VLC, originally also KiCad and Thunderbird/HexChat) from inside Emacs, and
explicitly wants apps replaced with an Emacs-native equivalent wherever one
is genuinely solid, rather than just adding a launcher key for everything.
This ADR records the decisions from a structured grill (same format as
[[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]]/[[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]) plus the implementation.

** Decision drivers
- [[id:adr-remove-exwm][ADR-002]]: EXWM is deliberately deferred to its own future roadmap phase
  (unmet revisit condition) — this feature must not depend on EXWM or
  reintroduce window-manager scope.
- [[id:std-doom-first][standards.org]]: extend Doom's existing =SPC o= ("open") leader namespace
  rather than inventing a parallel one.
- [[id:std-no-scope-creep][standards.org]]: don't bind every inventoried app "just in case" — scope to
  apps the user actually confirmed wanting, per-app, in conversation.
- User confirmed directly (not inferred, across a multi-round grill): GUI
  apps only (no terminal tools); bind external app + Emacs alternative
  side-by-side during a transition rather than committing to one
  permanently; raise-or-launch (focus an existing window) over always
  spawning a duplicate, where cheaply feasible; a new =SPC o l= sub-prefix
  ("launch") rather than consuming top-level =SPC o= letters one at a time;
  final per-app scope: Firefox, Discord, VLC as external launches; calculator
  and task manager replaced outright by =calc=/=proced= (no external
  binding at all); PDF viewer (Atril) and image viewer dropped entirely once
  it became clear =pdf-tools=/=image-mode= already auto-handle those file
  types; Thunderbird/HexChat/rcirc, LibreOffice, mpv/EMMS, and everything
  else inventoried explicitly declined.

** Candidates
| Decision point            | Candidates considered                              | Fate |
|----------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Window-management approach | EXWM (embed app windows in Emacs) / plain external =start-process= + optional raise-focus | *Plain external process* accepted — EXWM's revisit condition ([[id:adr-remove-exwm][ADR-002]]) isn't met; this feature doesn't need it |
| Leader placement           | New sub-prefix =SPC o l= / individual top-level =SPC o= letters | *=SPC o l=* accepted — keeps the top-level =o= namespace's remaining free letters (=p e E D s=) unconsumed, groups all launchers under one which-key menu |
| Raise-or-launch mechanism  | =xdotool= / =wmctrl= / no raise (always spawn)      | *=xdotool=* accepted — user confirmed it was already installed on this machine throughout (see Evidence/LOGBOOK re: the agent's own checks being unreliable here), more capable/actively maintained than =wmctrl= |
| Replace vs. coexist policy | Emacs alternative replaces external app outright / bind both during a transition | *Both, during transition* accepted (user's explicit choice) as the general policy — except where the Emacs alternative needs zero install and is obviously sufficient (=calc=, =proced=), where it was accepted as an outright replacement instead |
| PDF viewer (Atril)         | Bind as launcher / bind as file-picker fallback / contextual "open in Atril" command inside =pdf-view-mode=/Dired / no binding at all | *No binding* accepted (user's explicit choice) — =pdf-tools= (already-declared =pdf= Doom module, [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]]) already auto-opens =.pdf= files via =find-file=/Dired once its =epdfinfo= helper can compile |
| Image viewer                | Bind Ristretto/display / no binding                | *No binding* accepted — built-in =image-mode= already auto-handles images via =find-file=/Dired, same pattern as PDFs |
| KiCad                      | Bind a launcher key                                 | *Dropped* — confirmed not installed anywhere on this system (no binary, snap, or desktop file); out of scope until actually installed |

** Evidence
- =grep -n "^\* ADR-\|:ID:\|:STATUS:"= plus a full read of the relevant
  sections of this file confirmed ADR-002's EXWM removal and its unmet
  revisit condition ("when the EXWM/window-manager phase... starts" —
  [[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]'s Future phases list still carries it unscheduled).
- Read =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/config/default/+evil-bindings.el:676-744=
  (the evil-bindings variant, since this config runs =(evil +everywhere)=):
  confirmed live =SPC o= bindings in this config (=A a b d f F r R - t T m=)
  and that =p P e E o O s D l= are free (gated on modules not active here —
  =+dirvish=/=neotree=/=treemacs=, =:term eshell=, =:os macos=,
  =:tools docker=, =:tools llm=, none enabled per =init.el=).
- Enumerated installed GUI apps via =.desktop= files under
  =/usr/share/applications=, =~/.local/share/applications=, and snap/flatpak
  equivalents (no flatpak present). Confirmed Firefox installed as a snap
  (=/snap/bin/firefox=); confirmed *KiCad is not installed* — no binary, no
  snap, no =.desktop= file anywhere on the system.
- +First raise-or-launch tooling check (=which wmctrl xdotool=, sandboxed
  shell) wrongly reported both missing. A later check
  (=dpkg -s xdotool=, =command -v xdotool=, =xdotool --version=) found
  =xdotool 3.20160805.1= apparently installed at =/usr/bin/xdotool= (dated
  April 2024) — the =which= result was assumed to be a false negative from
  that shell invocation's restricted =PATH=+ *Retracted, 2026-08-13: user
  confirmed =xdotool= was already installed the whole time, and that the
  "restricted =PATH="" explanation is wrong. Why the agent's own =which=
  check returned nothing is genuinely unknown — see
  [[id:tshoot-agent-shell-system-state][troubleshooting.org]]. No install was
  needed or performed for =xdotool= either way.*
- +=dpkg -s libpoppler-glib-dev=: confirmed genuinely not installed. =gcc=,
  =make=, =pkg-config=, =libpng-dev=, =zlib1g-dev= all confirmed present.
  =find ... -iname epdfinfo*=: only the =pdf-tools= build's C source
  (=epdfinfo.c=/=.h=) exists, no compiled binary yet — =pdf-tools= genuinely
  can't run until =libpoppler-glib-dev= is installed and =epdfinfo= is built
  (triggered automatically on first =.pdf= visit once the library exists).+
  *Retracted, 2026-08-13: user confirmed =libpoppler-glib-dev= was already
  installed at the time of this check too. The agent's =dpkg -s= result is
  not trusted as ground truth for this machine going forward — see
  [[id:tshoot-agent-shell-system-state][troubleshooting.org]].*
- =init.el:104=: =pdf= module already active (not commented out) — installing
  a "PDF Emacs alternative" was already decided at some earlier point in this
  project, not a new decision made here; this ADR only newly discovers and
  records the missing system-library gap.
- =sudo -n apt-get install ...= and =sudo -n true=: both fail ("a password is
  required") — same constraint as [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]/[[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]], confirmed again.
  This finding about the sudo/TTY constraint itself still stands (it's about
  the agent's shell access, not about package state); what's retracted above
  is only the =dpkg -s= conclusion that =libpoppler-glib-dev= needed
  installing at all — per the user, it didn't.
- Initially told the user =docs/ai/keybindings.org= would be the doc to
  update for these bindings; on inspection that file is scoped specifically
  to the AI subsystem (=SPC a=, title "AI Subsystem — Keybindings"), not a
  general keybindings reference. Corrected course: no general
  =docs/keybindings.org= exists in this repo — the established pattern for
  this kind of small, self-contained addition is this ADR plus a
  [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]] entry, matching how [[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]]'s =mpv=/=yt-dlp= were
  tracked, not a dedicated keybindings doc or a new roadmap phase.

** Decision
- =config.el= (new section, directly after the =elfeed=/RSS block, matching
  this repo's flat-section style for subsystems too small to warrant a
  =config/*.el= split per [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]]): a =my/launch-or-focus= helper
  (=xdotool search --class= to find a running instance and =windowactivate=
  it, else =start-process=) plus:
  =(map! :leader (:prefix ("o l" . "launch")
    :desc "Firefox" "f" (cmd! (my/launch-or-focus "firefox"))
    :desc "Discord" "d" (cmd! (my/launch-or-focus "discord"))
    :desc "VLC"     "v" (cmd! (my/launch-or-focus "vlc"))
    :desc "Calculator (calc)" "c" #'calc
    :desc "Process monitor (proced)" "t" #'proced))=
- No changes to =init.el=/=packages.el= — every package involved (=pdf=
  module, built-in =calc=/=proced=) was already declared/available; no new
  Emacs package was installed for this ADR.
- System dependency: =libpoppler-glib-dev= (apt) — required for =pdf-tools='s
  =epdfinfo= to compile, not for anything under =SPC o l= directly. Per the
  user, already installed on this machine; no install action needed (see
  LOGBOOK retraction above).
- =docs/inventory.org=: =xdotool= and =libpoppler-glib-dev= added to the
  External binaries table ([[id:inv-binaries][inv-binaries]]) as present, cross-linked to
  this ADR.
- No =docs/roadmap.org= change — this doesn't start or consume the "EXWM (or
  another Emacs-centred window-management approach)" future-phase bullet
  (explicitly doesn't touch EXWM), and is small enough (a handful of =map!=
  bindings on top of already-declared modules) not to warrant its own
  tracked phase, consistent with [[id:std-no-scope-creep][std-no-scope-creep]].

** Consequences
- =SPC o l f/d/v= depend on =xdotool= remaining installed; if it's ever
  removed, =my/launch-or-focus= silently falls back to always spawning a
  fresh process (no raise-or-launch, no error) rather than failing outright.
- =SPC o l c/t= have no external-process dependency at all — pure Emacs,
  can't go stale the way a binary-backed binding can.
- =libpoppler-glib-dev= is already installed (per user) — =pdf-tools='s
  =epdfinfo= build should succeed on first =.pdf= visit; not yet actually
  confirmed working end-to-end.
- =SPC o l= namespace has room to grow (=a b c d e f g h i j k l m n o p q r
  s u w x y z= minus =f d v c t= all still free) if more apps get added
  later without needing another design round.
- The "bind both, drop the external one once validated" transition this ADR
  encodes for future apps is a policy, not a mechanism — nothing currently
  automates "you've confirmed the Emacs alternative is good enough, remove
  the external binding"; that stays a manual per-app follow-up decision.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync=: clean, 190 packages up to date. =doom doctor=: 47 modules, 153
  packages, one pre-existing unrelated warning (ghostel/zig, tracked
  separately in [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]]) — no new warnings from this change.
  =config.el= confirmed to parse cleanly (=read=-loop over all top-level
  forms, 41 forms, no error) — note per [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]] that none of
  this actually *runs* =config.el= the way a real interactive session does,
  so it doesn't prove the =map!= forms work, only that they parse.
- *2026-08-13:* user reported =SPC o l f= "did not work" in their live
  session; root cause suspected as [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]] (daemon needs
  restart to pick up =config.el=). User restarted Emacs, confirmed
  =SPC o l f= works. Closed — see LOGBOOK. Discord/VLC (same mechanism) and
  calc/proced (trivial built-ins) not separately retested; low risk given
  the shared code path already confirmed working.
- =libpoppler-glib-dev=/=xdotool= install status: no longer pending — see
  LOGBOOK, user confirmed both already installed.

** Rollback
Remove the =my/launch-or-focus= defun and the =SPC o l= =map!= block from
=config.el=. =xdotool=/=libpoppler-glib-dev=, if installed, can be removed
independently via the system package manager — nothing else in this config
depends on either.

** Revisit when
- *Done 2026-08-13, see LOGBOOK:* user restarted Emacs, confirmed =SPC o l
  f= works. =:STATUS:= is =live-verified=.
- The =xdotool=/=libpoppler-glib-dev= agent-shell discrepancy itself
  ([[id:tshoot-agent-shell-system-state][troubleshooting.org]]) gets root-caused — update that entry, not this
  ADR, since it's a general agent-tooling issue, not specific to this
  feature.
- The EXWM future-phase bullet ([[id:docs-roadmap][roadmap.org]]) actually starts — reconsider
  whether =SPC o l= app windows should be embedded via EXWM instead of
  raised via =xdotool=.
- Any app currently bound "both ways" (none yet — Firefox/Discord/VLC have
