  no Emacs alternative, =calc=/=proced= already replaced their external app
  outright) gets a real Emacs alternative later; re-grill that app
  specifically rather than assuming the transition policy resolves itself.
- The user wants more apps added to =SPC o l=, or KiCad (or an EDA
  alternative) actually gets installed on this machine.

* ADR-021: Migrate in-buffer completion from Company to Corfu; scope the "one completion engine" rule per axis
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-corfu-migration
:STATUS:   Implemented, partially live-verified (TAB-precedence mechanics confirmed in an isolated daemon with simulated overlays; real model-backed Copilot suggestions and real corfu popups from actual LSP/dabbrev candidates not separately re-exercised — see Validation)
:DATE:     2026-08-18
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Implemented" from "Proposed" [2026-08-18 Tue]
:END:

** Context
=AGENTS.md= had a hard rule: "one completion engine" (grouped with "one chat
client, one primary agentic-coding frontend" under "one tool per
responsibility"). The user asked to review Doom's completion module family and
migrate to "the best supported completion engines," want multiple engines
where they serve genuinely different purposes. Reviewing Doom's own module
READMEs (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/completion/*/README.org=)
surfaced two things:
- This config was already running two completion engines
  (=company +childframe= for in-buffer completion, =vertico= for
  minibuffer/=completing-read= completion) on two different axes — not a rule
  violation once "completion" is understood as two distinct responsibilities,
  but the rule as literally worded ("one completion engine") didn't say that.
- Company is explicitly deprecated by Doom itself: "*This module is
  deprecated* in favor of the Corfu module... in maintenance mode until it is
  eventually removed" (=completion/company/README.org=). Corfu is Doom's
  current recommendation for the same axis (in-buffer/cap(f) completion).
  Vertico (minibuffer axis) is already Doom's current recommendation, not
  deprecated — no change needed there.

** Decision drivers
- Don't leave a config module on Doom's own deprecated/maintenance-mode list
  when the modern replacement is a drop-in swap on the same axis
  (=AGENTS.md=: package installs/module changes need explicit approval, which
  the user gave for this migration specifically)
- Don't let a mis-scoped hard rule ("one completion engine," unqualified) block
  a config that was already correctly running two engines for two distinct
  problems (in-buffer vs. minibuffer completion)
- =docs/decisions.org= ADR-007 (Copilot/NES) proved TAB-precedence claims
  live rather than assumed; the same standard applies here since Corfu's
  keymap-activation mechanism (=minor-mode-overriding-map-alist=) is
  structurally different from Company's (=minor-mode-map-alist=), so ADR-007's
  Company-specific evidence doesn't automatically carry over
- Doom's own corfu README flags a known Evil/TAB interaction gotcha
  ("Fixing TAB Keybindings" troubleshooting section) — reason to verify, not
  assume

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate |
|-----------+------|
| Keep Company | Rejected — Doom's own README marks it maintenance-mode/deprecated; no reason to stay once Corfu covers the same role |
| Corfu (+orderless +icons) | *Chosen* — Doom's current recommendation for this axis; native =completion-at-point-functions=-based, lighter, actively maintained (2 maintainers vs. Company's original author only), and layers =cape= (dabbrev/file/keyword capfs) and =yasnippet-capf= automatically since =:editor snippets= is already enabled |
| Add a second in-buffer engine alongside Company/Corfu (e.g. a second AI backend) | Rejected — no distinct, frequently-useful role identified that Copilot doesn't already cover on the in-buffer axis; would violate the (now axis-scoped) hard rule for real, not just its old over-broad wording |
| Replace Vertico with Ivy/Helm/Ido | Not seriously considered — Vertico is Doom's current recommendation and not deprecated; Ivy is itself deprecated in favor of Vertico per its own README; Helm/Ido have no dedicated Doom maintainers and no distinct capability Vertico lacks here |

** Evidence
- *Doom's own deprecation notices* (read directly from source, not inferred):
  =completion/company/README.org= — "This module is deprecated in favor of
  the Corfu module... in maintenance mode until it is eventually removed;"
  =completion/ivy/README.org= — same wording pointing at Vertico. Neither
  =corfu= nor =vertico= module READMEs carry a deprecation notice.
- *Packages already present*: =doom sync= (2026-08-18) reported =corfu=,
  =cape=, =nerd-icons-corfu=, and =orderless= all "up-to-date" immediately
  after the =init.el= flag swap — these were already pinned/fetched
  dependencies of the (previously disabled) corfu module declaration, no new
  package downloads were needed. =company=/=company-dict=/=company-box=
  dropped from the package set (151 packages after, matching the swap).
- *=doom doctor= clean* after the swap: 46 modules, 151 packages, one
  pre-existing unrelated warning (missing =zig= for optional =:term ghostel=
  compilation, present before this change too, not introduced by it).
- *Corfu's actual TAB semantics differ from Company's*, read from source
  (=corfu.el= =defvar-keymap corfu-map=): upstream binds =TAB= to
  =corfu-complete=, but Doom's own corfu module keymap wiring (confirmed live
  below) resolves =TAB= to =corfu-next= (cycle) while a popup is showing —
  acceptance happens on =RET= (=corfu-insert=), not =TAB=. This is a genuine
  user-facing behavior change from the old Company flow and is called out in
  Consequences below, not just an implementation detail.
- *Keymap-activation mechanism differs from Company's*, read from source:
  Corfu installs its popup keymap into =minor-mode-overriding-map-alist= via
  =(setcdr (assq #'completion-in-region-mode minor-mode-overriding-map-alist)
  corfu-map)= (=corfu--setup=, =corfu.el:917-919=), a different (higher-
  priority) alist than Company's plain =minor-mode-map-alist= entry. Per
  Emacs's documented active-keymap search order, both sit *below* the
  =keymap= text/overlay property at point (where Copilot's overlay and
  yasnippet's field overlay live) and *below* =emulation-mode-map-alists=
  (where Evil's state maps live), but =minor-mode-overriding-map-alist= sits
  above ordinary =minor-mode-map-alist= entries — relevant to the
  Corfu-vs-NES case below.
- *Live-verified* in an isolated, disposable Doom daemon (distinct
  =--daemon=/=--socket-name=, this repo's real =init.el=/=config.el= as
  edited today, killed immediately after; no frame created, no interaction
  with the user's actual running daemon). Copilot/yasnippet/NES state was
  built from the exact primitives their own source uses
  (=copilot--get-overlay=, =copilot-completion-map=, =yas-keymap=,
  =yas-overlay-priority=, =completion-in-region-mode= + =corfu-map=) rather
  than through real network-backed suggestions — same method ADR-007 used,
  same caveat applies (mechanics verified, not live model output). Results,
  =(key-binding (kbd "TAB"))= in a real =emacs-lisp-mode= buffer, both Evil
  states:
  | Scenario                                          | TAB resolves to             |
  |----------------------------------------------------+-----------------------------|
  | Nothing active                                     | =indent-for-tab-command=    |
  | Corfu popup only                                   | =corfu-next= (cycle)        |
  | Corfu popup + Copilot suggestion overlay           | =copilot-accept-completion= |
  | Corfu popup + real-width yasnippet field overlay   | =yas-next-field-or-maybe-expand= |
  | Copilot overlay + yasnippet field (no corfu popup) | =copilot-accept-completion= (ADR-007's finding repeated, still holds) |
  | NES pending only                                   | =copilot-nes-accept=        |
  | Corfu popup + NES pending simultaneously           | =corfu-next= (Corfu wins — new case, ADR-007 had no third overriding-map-alist layer to test against) |
  All six held identically in both =evil-normal-state= and =evil-insert-state=.
  One simulation error was caught and fixed mid-verification: an initial
  zero-width yasnippet overlay was not picked up by keymap search at all
  (Corfu won that malformed case) — real yasnippet fields are never
  zero-width (they span the field's placeholder text), so the overlay was
  corrected to span real buffer text before trusting the result.
- *Not verified*: the GUI-only =display-graphic-p= branch of Doom's =[C-i]=/
  =TAB= disambiguation hack (=doom-emacs.el= "HACK: Emacs can't distinguish
  C-i from TAB" block, ~line 823) genuinely requires a real graphical frame
  receiving a real hardware keypress to exercise; synthesizing it would have
  required opening a visible frame on the user's actual display from the
  isolated daemon, which risked a visible on-screen disruption for a
  non-completion-relevant edge case (it only matters for =evil-jump-forward=
  reachability in Normal state when no popup/overlay is present at all — moot
  whenever completion is actually in play). Flagged, not silently assumed.

** Decision
- =init.el=: disabled =(company +childframe)=, enabled
  =(corfu +orderless +icons)= under =:completion=.
- =AGENTS.md=: reworded the "one completion engine" hard rule to be scoped
  per completion axis (in-buffer vs. minibuffer) rather than an unqualified
  singular, since the prior wording didn't match the config's own correct,
  pre-existing shape (Company/Corfu + Vertico as two engines for two
  problems).
- No change to Vertico, Copilot, or copilot-nes-mode.

** Consequences
- *TAB no longer accepts a completion candidate* — it cycles
  (=corfu-next=/=corfu-previous= via =TAB=/=S-TAB=). Accepting now requires
  =RET= (=corfu-insert=), per Corfu's own documented default. Users muscle-
  -memoried to "TAB completes" from the Company era need to adjust to
  "TAB cycles, RET inserts" for the non-Copilot completion path. Copilot/NES
  acceptance via =TAB= is unaffected (still wins outright).
- Corfu brings =cape= along automatically (dabbrev, file-path, and other
  CAPF sources layer onto the same popup rather than requiring a second
  frontend) and =yasnippet-capf= since =:editor snippets= is enabled — this
  is the "richer sources without a second engine" outcome the user was
  actually looking for, achieved within one engine on the in-buffer axis
  rather than by stacking engines.
- =docs/ai/architecture.org=, =docs/ai/workflows.org=, and
  =docs/inventory.org= need their "Company +childframe" references updated
  to Corfu (tracked as a follow-up in the same change, not left stale).
- New, previously-undocumented precedence fact: if a Corfu popup and a
  pending NES edit ever coincide (no regular Copilot overlay), Corfu wins,
  not NES. Low real-world likelihood (different trigger conditions) but now
  documented rather than unknown.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync= clean, corfu/cape/nerd-icons-corfu/orderless confirmed present;
  =doom doctor= clean (46 modules, 151 packages, one pre-existing unrelated
  =:term ghostel=/=zig= warning).
- TAB-precedence mechanics live-verified per Evidence above, in an isolated,
  disposable daemon killed immediately after use — the user's real daemon was
  never touched.
- Not yet done (needs the user's real session, same caveat ADR-007 left
  open): confirm a real Corfu popup from actual LSP/dabbrev candidates
  renders and cycles/inserts as expected, and that a live Copilot suggestion
  still wins over it, with real model output rather than simulated overlays.

** Rollback
Swap the =init.el= flags back: =(company +childframe)= enabled,
=(corfu +orderless +icons)= disabled, then =doom sync=. =company-box=/
=company-dict= are still pinned in the (deprecated) company module's own
=packages.el=, so nothing else needs restoring. Revert the =AGENTS.md= rule
wording if reverting the engine too (they're coupled: the axis-scoping
rationale referenced Corfu's arrival, though the axis distinction itself
would still be independently true even under Company).

** Revisit when
- The user actually exercises Corfu in a live buffer and finds the
  TAB-cycles/RET-inserts behavior worse than Company's TAB-accepts flow —
  =+corfu-want-ret-to-confirm= and remapping =TAB= back to
  =corfu-complete=/=corfu-insert= in =corfu-map= are both one-line config
  changes, not a reason to revert the whole migration.
- Doom changes Corfu's keymap-installation mechanism (currently
  =minor-mode-overriding-map-alist=) — re-check the Corfu-vs-NES and
  Corfu-vs-yasnippet precedence findings above if so.
- The user wants a genuinely distinct second in-buffer completion role (e.g.
  a offline/local model alongside Copilot) — that would be a new ADR, not an
  amendment to this one, since it's a different decision (adding an engine)
  from this one (swapping one deprecated engine for its replacement).

* ADR-022: UI "rice" pass — font, transparency, cursor pulses, zen/indent-guides/minimap/tabs modules, dynamic dashboard banner
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-ui-rice-pass
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (transparency, pulsar, zen =+focus=, tabs, and font all confirmed in the user's running daemon 2026-08-23; the minimap alone was never separately exercised)
:DATE:     2026-08-21
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — user confirmed
  transparency, pulsar, and zen mode visually after a reload; centaur-tabs
  confirmed separately via [[id:adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard][ADR-024]]. Only the minimap went unexercised.
:END:

** Context
The user asked, via a =/grilling= session (the =mattpocock-skills= plugin's
interrogative design-tree skill), to "rice" this Emacs config — make it visually
richer — rather than continue the config's prior UI minimalism (no prior ADR
covered theming/UI at all). The session mapped the request into a design
tree over several rounds: aesthetic direction (heavy, r/unixporn-style
flourish, not minimal polish), whether to keep the existing theme, font
choice, which specific chrome/motion extras were in scope, and tolerance for
new packages. A follow-up request (after the plan was confirmed and mid-
implementation) asked to make the dashboard banner derive from the machine's
hostname dynamically rather than being hardcoded, since the config may run on
a different machine later.

** Decision drivers
- User's explicit answers during grilling: keep =doom-zenburn= (no reason to
  change a working, deliberate choice); heavy-flourish direction including
  transparency, custom dashboard art, animated cursor, and (once offered the
  full extras list) all of indent-guides/minimap/tabs/zen-mode too; willing
  to accept a small number of well-known new packages, proposed individually
  before install (=AGENTS.md= hard rule: package installs need explicit,
  current-turn approval, not inferred from a broader prior approval)
- =AGENTS.md=/=.claude/rules/doom-emacs.md=: Doom-first (module flags over
  hand-rolled equivalents where a module already exists), one clean
  =doom sync= + =doom doctor= after any module/package change
  - =docs/agents/skills.md=/=CLAUDE.md=: new architectural decisions get
  recorded here, not left only in conversation
- Repo-wide plaintext/diffable convention (org files, elisp, no binary
  blobs) — relevant to rejecting an image-based dashboard logo
- Portability: the user does not want the dashboard banner hardcoded to this
  one machine's name

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Cursor motion feedback | Doom's built-in =:ui nav-flash= (plain line-blink, no new package) | Rejected — doesn't match the "animated cursor" flourish the user asked for |
| Cursor motion feedback | =pulsar= (animated colored pulse) | *Chosen* — matches the flourish direction; running it alongside nav-flash would double-fire on the same events, so nav-flash stays disabled |
| Dashboard banner | Doom's default ASCII logo, recolored | Not chosen — user specifically wanted the machine's hostname depicted, not the stock logo |
| Dashboard banner | Raster image logo (binary asset) | Rejected — breaks this repo's plaintext/diffable convention; the odd one out among otherwise all-text config |
| Dashboard banner | Hardcoded hand-drawn ASCII art for the literal string "yogurt" | Superseded — worked, but the user asked afterward for portability across machines |
| Dashboard banner | Hand-rolled Elisp block-letter font table covering A-Z/0-9/hyphen, rendering =(system-name)= | Rejected (user's explicit choice between two presented options) — self-contained but a few hundred lines of hardcoded glyph data to write and maintain |
| Dashboard banner | Shell out to the external =figlet= tool with =(system-name)= at render time | *Chosen* (user's explicit choice) — matches this config's existing pattern of calling well-known external tools (ruff, black, xdotool) via =call-process=/=shell-command-to-string=; far less code; falls back to plain =(system-name)= text if =figlet= isn't installed |
| Font | Full Nerd-patched "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font" (=ryanoasis/nerd-fonts= GitHub release) | Rejected, not by choice — GitHub (=github.com=, =api.github.com=) is unreachable from the agent's sandboxed Bash tool even with sandbox restrictions lifted (DNS resolution times out); only =raw.githubusercontent.com= resolved |
| Font | Plain "JetBrains Mono" via apt's =fonts-jetbrains-mono=, paired with the already-installed =Symbols Nerd Font Mono= for icon glyph fallback | *Chosen* — apt's Ubuntu archive mirror was reachable even when GitHub/MELPA were not; icon glyph coverage doesn't require the primary font itself to be Nerd-patched, since =nerd-icons.el= pairs any regular font with exactly this dedicated glyph-only fallback font by design |
| Theme | Switch away from =doom-zenburn= | Rejected — deliberate, already-working choice; no stated dissatisfaction with it |

** Evidence
- *Facts checked before asking the user anything*, per the grilling method
  (never ask for a fact retrievable directly): =init.el= module list read
  directly — =indent-guides=, =minimap=, =nav-flash=, =doom-quit=, =zen=,
  =emoji=, =tabs=, =treemacs=/=neotree= all disabled; =doom-theme= already
  =doom-zenburn=; =doom-font=/=doom-variable-pitch-font= unset (system
  default); only =Symbols Nerd Font Mono= present system-wide
  (=fc-list | grep -ic nerd= → 1).
- *Transparency feasibility*: =emacs --version= → 30.1; build-feature check
  → =cairo=t pgtk=nil=; session is GUI over X11 (=DISPLAY=:0.0=,
  =WAYLAND_DISPLAY=unset), Emacs itself running as a =systemd --user=
  daemon (confirmed via =systemctl --user status emacs.service=); no
  compositor process found (=picom=/=compton=/=xcompmgr= all absent) — not a
  blocker, since Emacs 29+ Cairo builds composite =alpha-background=
  internally without needing one.
- *Zen =+focus= prerequisite*: =init.el= line 100 confirms =:tools lsp= is
  enabled (plain =lsp-mode=, not =+eglot=) — the =+focus= flag's enhanced
  LSP-aware dimming (per =ui/zen/README.org=) has its prerequisite already
  met, no extra module needed.
- *Module→package mapping read directly from each module's =packages.el=*
  (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/ui/{minimap,tabs,zen,indent-guides}/packages.el=):
  minimap → =demap=; tabs → =centaur-tabs=; indent-guides → =indent-bars=;
  zen → =writeroom-mode= + =mixed-pitch= always, plus =focus= and (since
  =:tools lsp -eglot= is active) =lsp-focus= under the =+focus= flag — all
  Doom-declared/pinned, no manual =package!= needed for any of these; only
  =pulsar= (not tied to any module) needed a manual =package!= entry in this
  repo's own =packages.el=.
- *Dashboard override mechanism read from source*
  (=ui/dashboard/config.el=): =+dashboard-ascii-banner-fn= is a plain
  =defcustom= (no =provide=/feature to hang an =after!= block on regardless
  — config.el's own file loads unconditionally after all modules), so a bare
  =setq= is correct and sufficient, matching the =after!=-exemption already
  documented in this repo's =config.el= for "doom variables (which start
  with 'doom-' or '+')."
- *Network reachability, tested directly, not assumed*: =curl= to
  =github.com=, =api.github.com=, =melpa.org=, =stable.melpa.org= all timed
  out (exit 000) from the agent's Bash tool, both with and without
  =dangerouslyDisableSandbox=; =raw.githubusercontent.com= (301) and
  =archive.ubuntu.com= (200) both resolved and responded. Consistent with a
  domain-allowlist firewall rather than a total network outage. The user's
  own shell (via =!= prefix) had full network — font and =figlet= installs,
  and =doom sync= itself (which needed =github.com=/=melpa.org= for
  =pulsar=/=demap=/=centaur-tabs=/=indent-bars=/=writeroom-mode=/
  =mixed-pitch=/=focus=/=lsp-focus=), were run from there instead of from
  the agent's sandboxed tool.
- *=pulsar='s actual public API checked against source* after install
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/pulsar/pulsar.el=), since the
  config was written before the package was fetched: =pulsar-pulse= is
  =(make-obsolete-variable 'pulsar-pulse nil "1.3.0")= — obsolete/no-op in
  the installed version, removed from this repo's config rather than left
  in as a silent no-op that would warn on doctor/compile. =pulsar-delay=,
  =pulsar-iterations=, =pulsar-face=, =pulsar-pulse-functions=,
  =pulsar-global-mode=, and the =pulsar-cyan= face all confirmed present.
  =pulsar-pulse-functions='s own default list (read directly, lines 69-145)
  already covers =evil-goto-first-line=, =evil-goto-line=,
  =evil-scroll-down/up=, =evil-scroll-page-down/up=,
  =evil-window-top/bottom=, and =other-window= — only =switch-to-buffer= was
  actually missing and needed adding, not the fuller list originally
  planned before checking the source.
- *Font family name verified against what was actually installed*, not
  assumed from the package name: =fc-list | grep -i jetbrains= after the
  user's apt install showed the family is "JetBrains Mono" (with a space),
  not "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font" — the =doom-font=/=doom-variable-pitch-font=
  spec in =config.el= was corrected to match before considering this done;
  the original wrong value would have silently fallen back to a default
  font with no error.
- *=doom sync= output*, pasted by the user: "No packages need attention" /
  "All 194 packages are up-to-date" on the final successful run (prior runs
  hit transient network failures per the user, hence re-running); all 8 new
  packages' build directories confirmed present directly
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-30.1/{pulsar,demap,centaur-tabs,indent-bars,writeroom-mode,mixed-pitch,focus,lsp-focus}=)
  rather than trusting the sync log alone.
- *=doom doctor=* run twice (before and after the font-family fix): both
  clean — "Everything seems fine, happy Emacs'ing!," 50 modules, 159
  packages, =Symbols Nerd Font Mono= detected under the font check both times.
- *=figlet= output spot-checked* directly: =figlet -f standard "$(hostname)"=
  against the real hostname (=yogurt=) produced a correctly-shaped ASCII
  banner.

** Decision
- =init.el=: enabled =indent-guides=, =minimap=, =tabs=, and
  =(zen +focus)= under =:ui=; left =nav-flash=/=doom-quit=/=emoji=/
  =treemacs=/=neotree= disabled (out of scope — not requested).
- =packages.el=: added =(package! pulsar)=.
- =config.el=, new "rice pass" section:
  - =doom-font=/=doom-variable-pitch-font= set to =(font-spec :family
    "JetBrains Mono" :size 13)=.
  - =default-frame-alist= gets =(alpha-background . 92)=; a
    =+my/rice-update-frame-alpha= function, hung on
    =after-focus-change-function= (the modern, non-obsolete hook, correct
    for a daemon with multiple emacsclient frames), sets each frame's
    =alpha-background= to 92 when it's the selected frame and 80 otherwise.
  - =use-package! pulsar= hooked on =doom-first-input= to enable
    =pulsar-global-mode=; =pulsar-delay=/=pulsar-iterations=/=pulsar-face=
    ('pulsar-cyan') set, plus =switch-to-buffer= added to
    =pulsar-pulse-functions=.
  - =+my/dashboard-hostname-banner-fn=: renders =(system-name)= via
    =figlet -f standard=, falling back to plain =(system-name)= text if
    =figlet= isn't on =PATH=; assigned to =+dashboard-ascii-banner-fn=.
- System packages (installed by the user directly, not by the agent, since
  both needed either =sudo= or reachable-only-from-the-user's-shell
  network): =fonts-jetbrains-mono=, =figlet=.

** Consequences
- The dashboard banner degrades gracefully (plain hostname text, not an
  error) on any machine/checkout where =figlet= isn't installed — this repo
  now has one place (=+my/dashboard-hostname-banner-fn=) that assumes an
  optional external tool, following the same =executable-find= guard pattern
  already used for =ruff=/=xdotool= elsewhere in =config.el=.
- Icon glyph rendering (modeline, corfu, dired, etc.) depends on
  =Symbols Nerd Font Mono= remaining installed, not on =doom-font= itself —
  a fact worth remembering if =doom-font= is ever changed again to some
  other plain (non-Nerd) family; it will not silently break icons as long as
  that fallback font stays present.
- =docs/inventory.org='s =:ui= module line needs updating to list
  =indent-guides=, =minimap=, =tabs=, =(zen +focus)= (done alongside this
  ADR, cross-referencing this entry, matching how ADR-021 updated it for the
  completion-engine swap).
- Gap closed 2026-08-23: transparency, the pulsar pulse, and zen mode are all
  confirmed working in the user's running daemon (see Validation). The minimap
  is the only piece of this ADR never separately exercised, and it is also the
  one whose failure would be self-evident on sight.
- The Nerd-patched font gap (plain "JetBrains Mono" instead of the
  Nerd-patched variant) is an accepted, environment-forced compromise, not a
  deliberate aesthetic choice — see Revisit when.
- This ADR adopted the =:ui tabs= module but decided nothing about how tabs
  *behave*. The new-tab target (Doom dashboard instead of an =Untitled=
  buffer) and the tab-management keybindings were decided separately in
  [[id:adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard][ADR-024]], which also confirms centaur-tabs renders correctly in the
  user's real session — closing that one item from this ADR's open visual-
  confirmation gap (transparency, pulsar, zen =+focus= remain unconfirmed).

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync=: clean (run from the user's shell; the agent's sandboxed Bash
  tool cannot reach =github.com=/=melpa.org=), all 8 new packages' build
  directories confirmed present directly by the agent afterward.
- =doom doctor=: clean, run twice (before and after the font-family
  correction), both times "Everything seems fine, happy Emacs'ing!."
- =figlet -f standard "$(hostname)"= output spot-checked directly against
  the real hostname.
- *Live-verified 2026-08-23*, after the user reloaded Emacs: transparency
  and pulsar pulses confirmed visually by the user, and corroborated by
  read-only =emacsclient= queries against their running daemon —
  =alpha-background= is 92 both on the live frame and in
  =default-frame-alist= (so new frames inherit it), the build is Cairo/X11
  (=(featurep 'cairo)= → t, no external compositor needed),
  =doom-font= resolved to a real "JetBrains Mono" 13 XLFD rather than
  silently falling back, =pulsar-global-mode= is on with =pulsar-mode= active
  in 18 buffers (off only in minibuffer/internal buffers, as expected for a
  globalized mode), =pulsar-cyan= resolves to =#004065=, and =switch-to-buffer=
  is present in =pulsar-pulse-functions= — the one addition this ADR made to
  that list.
- centaur-tabs rendering confirmed separately: see
  [[id:adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard][ADR-024]], which required clicking a tab to verify.
- Zen mode confirmed working by the user the same day. Worth recording for
  future readers: a daemon query for =writeroom-mode-hook= had errored with
  "Symbol's value as variable is void" shortly beforehand, which was purely
  =:ui zen= being deferred and writeroom not yet loaded in that session — a
  lazy-loading artifact, not a fault, and the toggle worked fine when actually
  invoked. Don't read that class of void-variable error as breakage.
- The minimap was not separately confirmed — the one piece of this ADR still
  resting on mechanical verification alone.

** Rollback
- Revert =init.el= flags: disable =indent-guides=/=minimap=/=tabs=/=zen=,
  re-enable =nav-flash= if the plain blink is preferred back, then
  =doom sync=.
- Remove the "rice pass" section from =config.el= (font/transparency/
  pulsar/dashboard-banner =setq='s and function definitions) to fall back to
  Doom's stock font resolution, opaque frames, no cursor-pulse, and Doom's
  default ASCII logo.
- Remove =(package! pulsar)= from =packages.el=, then =doom sync= to purge it.
- System packages (=fonts-jetbrains-mono=, =figlet=) can stay installed
  harmlessly even if the config using them is reverted, or be removed with
  =sudo apt remove=.

** Revisit when
- The agent's sandbox (or a future environment) can actually reach
  =github.com=/=api.github.com= — install the real Nerd-patched
  "JetBrainsMono Nerd Font" and swap =doom-font=/=doom-variable-pitch-font=
  to it, matching the family name actually shipped by that release rather
  than assuming one.
- The user actually uses this config for a while and finds transparency,
  the pulsar pulse, or zen =+focus= dimming distracting rather than
  pleasant — each is an independent, easily-reverted =setq=/module-flag, not
  a reason to unwind this whole ADR.
- This config is ever checked out on a different machine — the dashboard
  banner (hostname-driven) and icon-fallback-font approach (not tied to a
  specific primary font) were both deliberately designed for that case
  already, but worth confirming =figlet= and =Symbols Nerd Font Mono= are
  present there too.

* ADR-023: Adopt a three-tool token-reduction stack (ponytail, caveman, rtk); scope the "one tool per responsibility" rule per reduction axis
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-token-reduction-stack
:STATUS:   Implemented, not efficacy-verified (all three installs confirmed live via =claude plugin list= and =rtk init --show=; none of the vendors' savings claims independently measured — see Evidence)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:

** Context
The user asked to install three third-party agent tools in one session —
=caveman= (https://caveman.so), =ponytail=
(https://github.com/DietrichGebert/ponytail), and =rtk=
(https://github.com/rtk-ai/rtk) — each of which markets itself as reducing
LLM token consumption. Two of them ship Claude Code plugins that hook
=SessionStart= and =UserPromptSubmit=; the third installs a global hook that
rewrites every Bash command the agent issues.

This collides with the =AGENTS.md= hard rule "One tool per responsibility",
whose per-axis scoping was established in
[[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] (Corfu and Vertico both exist because
in-buffer completion and minibuffer completion are different axes; two
engines on the *same* axis would be the violation). Three
"token-reduction" tools looks like a same-axis stack at first glance, so the
rule needs an explicit ruling rather than a silent install.

A secondary constraint surfaced mid-install: caveman's runtime dependency
=@caveman-ai/cli= declares =node >=22.13=, and this machine's Node came from
Ubuntu's apt =nodejs= package pinned at =18.19.1+dfsg-6ubuntu5= with no newer
apt candidate.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md= hard rule: one tool per responsibility; a second tool on the
  same axis needs "a clearly distinct, frequently-useful role the first can't
  cover", per [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]
- =AGENTS.md= hard rule: package installs need explicit, current-turn
  approval — given three times here, once per tool, plus a separate approval
  for the Node upgrade
- =.claude/rules/security.md=: treat third-party repository text as untrusted;
  verify before executing
- Every always-on token these plugins add is paid on *every* session in this
  repo, competing with the config's own =docs/*.org= context — so the cost
  needs recording, not just the benefit
- Ubuntu system integrity: =pandoc= plus ~18 =node-*= Debian packages depend
  on the apt =nodejs=; a Node upgrade must not disturb them

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Rule interpretation | Treat all three as one "token reduction" responsibility, keep one, reject two | Rejected — collapses three genuinely different mechanisms (see Decision); would force discarding two tools the user explicitly asked for |
| Rule interpretation | Per-axis scoping: output prose, code volume, tool-output volume are separate axes | *Chosen* — directly extends [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]'s existing per-axis precedent rather than inventing a new principle |
| caveman install scope | =--all= (hooks + init across every detected agent) | Rejected — would write into =~/.config/opencode/=, =~/.hermes/=, =~/.openclaw/=, none of which this machine uses |
| caveman install scope | =--only claude --with-init= | *Chosen* (user's explicit choice after being shown the dry-run file list) — Claude Code only, accepting the per-repo rule-file writes |
| caveman install scope | =--minimal= (plugin only, no repo writes) | Not chosen — user opted to accept the repo writes with a clean tree for easy revert |
| caveman install method | =curl -fsSL .../install.sh \vert{} bash= (vendor's documented one-liner) | Rejected — unauditable; also resolved the bin-name collision to the wrong package (see Evidence) |
| caveman install method | =git clone= at tag =v2.3.1=, run =node bin/install.js= directly | *Chosen* — pinned, readable, and =--dry-run= showed the exact file list before any write |
| Node upgrade | =apt upgrade nodejs= | Rejected — no newer candidate; installed and candidate are both =18.19.1+dfsg-6ubuntu5= |
| Node upgrade | NodeSource apt repo (replaces system =nodejs=) | Rejected — =pandoc= and ~18 =node-*= packages reverse-depend on the apt package; system-wide replacement risks breaking them |
| Node upgrade | =nvm=, per-user, leaving =/usr/bin/node= intact | *Chosen* — bypasses Ubuntu's pin without touching any apt-managed dependent; mirrors the existing =pyenv= pattern already in =~/.bashrc= |
| rtk install method | =curl \vert{} sh=, =cargo install=, or Homebrew | Rejected — no =cargo= and no =brew= on this machine; =curl \vert{} sh= unauditable |
| rtk install method | Prebuilt =x86_64-unknown-linux-musl= release tarball, SHA256-verified | *Chosen* — static-pie binary, zero deps, checksum verifiable against the release's own =checksums.txt= |

** Evidence
*Supply-chain checks performed before any install:*
- =ponytail= verified via =api.github.com/repos/DietrichGebert/ponytail=:
  MIT, 108,731 stars, created 2026-06-12, pushed 2026-08-07, not archived.
  (The star count was checked precisely because it looked implausible; the
  API confirmed it.)
- =rtk= verified the same way: Apache-2.0, 77,154 stars, created
  2026-01-22, pushed 2026-08-23.
- =caveman='s =install.sh= was read in full before execution. It is a shim
  that =exec npx -y "github:JuliusBrussee/caveman#v2.3.1"=.
- =caveman='s =bin/install.js= was read for its write surface before running.

*A real discrepancy found by reading rather than trusting:* running
=npx -y github:JuliusBrussee/caveman#v2.3.1= bare printed the *runtime* CLI's
help and exited 0 without installing anything. Cause: the GitHub package is
=caveman-installer= with =bin: {"caveman": "./bin/install.js"}= and
=engines: node >=18=, but its dependency =@caveman-ai/cli@1.2.5= also
provides a =caveman= bin, and npx resolved the collision to the dependency.
The =>=22.13= engine constraint therefore belongs to the *runtime*, not the
installer. Installing via a direct =node bin/install.js= call from a pinned
clone avoided the collision.

*Install footprints, verified after the fact:*
- =claude plugin list= → =caveman@caveman=, =ponytail@ponytail=,
  =mattpocock-skills@claude-plugins-official=, =claude-orgmode@claude-orgmode=,
  all enabled, user scope.
- =~/.claude/settings.json= diffed before/after each install: gained only
  =enabledPlugins= and =extraKnownMarketplaces= entries. caveman's installer
  reported "plugin manifest handles hooks", so *no* standalone hook wiring
  and *no* =statusLine= overwrite occurred — contradicting the broader write
  list implied by reading =install.js= in isolation.
- =rtk init --show= → hook installed (=rtk hook claude=, native binary
  command), =~/.claude/RTK.md= present in slim mode (961 B), =@RTK.md=
  reference added to global =~/.claude/CLAUDE.md=, settings.json hook
  configured.
- =caveman --with-init= appended a fenced =<!-- caveman-begin -->= block to
  this repo's tracked =AGENTS.md= and added five untracked directories
  (=.cursor/=, =.windsurf/=, =.clinerules/=, =.github/=, =.opencode/=) for
  editors this machine does not use.

*Node upgrade, verified:*
- =nvm= installed by =git clone= at tag =v0.40.7= (not =curl \vert{} bash=);
  =nvm install --lts= → Node =v24.19.0=, npm =11.17.0=, tarball checksum
  matched ("Checksums matched!").
- =/usr/bin/node= still reports =v18.19.1= — the apt package and its
  =pandoc=/=node-*= dependents are untouched.
- Ubuntu's default =~/.bashrc= returns early for non-interactive shells, so
  the appended =NVM_DIR= block applies to interactive terminals only;
  subprocesses must source =nvm.sh= explicitly to see v24.

*rtk binary, verified:*
- =rtk-x86_64-unknown-linux-musl.tar.gz= (v0.45.0) checked against the
  release's =checksums.txt= via =sha256sum -c= → =OK=.
- Installed to =~/.local/bin/rtk= (already on =PATH=); =rtk --version= →
  =rtk 0.45.0=; =file= → static-pie ELF, stripped.

*Not verified — stated plainly:*
- None of the three vendors' savings claims were independently measured.
  caveman advertises "65% fewer output tokens", ponytail "~54%" fewer lines
  of code, rtk "60-90%" of bash output. No before/after benchmark was run in
  this repo. rtk's own README concedes its token counts are estimated as
  =bytes / 4= with no tokenizer, and that bash output is only one contributor
  to input tokens, which are in turn only part of the bill.
- The =SessionStart=/=UserPromptSubmit= hooks from caveman and ponytail have
  not been observed firing; they require a session restart.
- No interaction test has been run between rtk's Bash-rewriting hook and this
  repo's =~/.config/emacs/bin/doom= absolute-path requirement (see
  =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org::tshoot-path-tilde=).

** Decision
Keep all three, on the grounds that they act on three distinct axes — the
same per-axis reasoning [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] used for
Corfu/Vertico:

| Tool | Axis | Acts on |
|------+------+---------|
| =ponytail= | Code volume | How much code the agent *writes* (YAGNI, stdlib-first, no unrequested abstractions) |
| =caveman= | Prose register | How verbosely the agent *talks* in its user-facing output |
| =rtk= | Tool-output volume | How much command output the agent *reads* back into context |

Two tools on the same one of those axes would be the violation; these three
do not overlap. Record the always-on cost (below) so the trade is legible.

*Install commands of record:*
- =claude plugin marketplace add DietrichGebert/ponytail= +
  =claude plugin install ponytail@ponytail=
- =node bin/install.js --only claude --with-init --non-interactive= from a
  pinned =v2.3.1= clone of =JuliusBrussee/caveman=
- prebuilt =rtk= v0.45.0 musl binary into =~/.local/bin=, then =rtk init -g=

** Consequences
*Always-on token cost, measured via =claude plugin details= (2026-08-23):*

| Plugin | Skills | Agents | Hooks | Always-on |
|--------+--------+--------+-------+-----------|
| =caveman= | 21 | 3 | 2 | ~2,681 tok |
| =mattpocock-skills= | 25 | 0 | 0 | ~1,609 tok |
| =ponytail= | 6 | 0 | 3 | ~983 tok |
| =claude-orgmode= | 2 | 0 | 0 | ~297 tok |
| *Plugin total* | *54* | *3* | *5* | *~5,570 tok* |
| =~/.claude/RTK.md= (via =@RTK.md=) | — | — | 1 | ~240 tok (961 B ÷ 4) |
| *Grand total* | | | | *~5,810 tok/session* |

Notable per-component costs (=on-invoke= is paid each time the skill fires):
- Most expensive always-on single plugin: =caveman= at ~2,681 tok — 48% of
  the plugin total, for 21 skills of which only =caveman= itself (~150
  always-on / ~2.3k on-invoke) is the headline feature.
- Most expensive on-invoke skills: =caveman-setup= ~3.9k, =wayfinder= ~4k,
  =ask-matt= ~3.8k, =claude-orgmode:notes= ~3.8k, =writing-for-agents= ~3.6k,
  =caveman-learn= ~3.4k, =teach= ~3.2k.
- Cheapest useful always-on: =claude-orgmode= at ~297 tok for 2 skills.

So the stack spends ~5.8k tokens of every session's context to save an
unmeasured amount later. That is a bet, not a proven win, and it is the main
thing the Revisit conditions below are aimed at.

*Other consequences:*
- =AGENTS.md= is now git-modified with a vendor-authored block instructing
  terse "caveman register" responses. This is a *tracked* file carrying this
  repo's hard rules, and the block applies to every agent working here, not
  just caveman-aware ones. It is fenced (=caveman-begin=/=caveman-end=) so
  it can be removed surgically.
- The plugin fans its rule text out to five other agent frontends
  (=.clinerules/caveman.md=, =.cursor/rules/caveman.mdc=,
  =.opencode/AGENTS.md=, =.windsurf/rules/caveman.md=,
  =.github/copilot-instructions.md=) — editors this repo does not use. These
  are now gitignored (this repo's first =.gitignore=, added 2026-08-23):
  regenerable vendor output for frontends outside this config's one-agentic-
  frontend rule, and tracking them would mean re-reviewing five copies of the
  same text on every plugin update. The tracked copy is the fenced block in
  =AGENTS.md= above. =.github/= is ignored *by file*, not as a directory, so
  it stays available for real repo infrastructure (workflows, issue
  templates — issues live on =Meta-James/doom-config=).
- Node is now dual-sourced: apt =v18.19.1= at =/usr/bin/node= for system
  packages, nvm =v24.19.0= for interactive shells. Anything invoked
  non-interactively (scripts, subprocesses, systemd units — including the
  Emacs daemon) still sees v18 unless it sources =nvm.sh=. This is a new
  footgun of the same family as
  =docs/ai/troubleshooting.org::tshoot-path-tilde=.
- rtk's hook rewrites Bash commands globally, i.e. in every project on this
  machine, not just this repo.
- Three tools now hook =SessionStart=/=UserPromptSubmit=
  (caveman, ponytail, rtk). Ordering between them is unspecified and
  unexamined.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above, which carries the fate and reason for each
rejected option.

** Validation
Confirmed at install time:
- =claude plugin list= shows all four plugins enabled at user scope.
- =rtk init --show= reports hook, RTK.md, =@RTK.md= reference, and
  settings.json entry all =[ok]=.
- =rtk --version= → =rtk 0.45.0=; release tarball SHA256 matched
  =checksums.txt=.
- =node --version= in an interactive shell → =v24.19.0=; =/usr/bin/node
  --version= → =v18.19.1=.
- =~/.claude/settings.json= before/after diffs contained only plugin and
  marketplace entries.

*Still to validate* (none of this has been done):
1. Restart a session and confirm the caveman/ponytail hooks actually fire.
2. Run a representative task twice — once with the stack enabled, once with
   all three disabled — and compare real token usage. Until then the savings
   are vendor claims only.
3. Exercise =~/.config/emacs/bin/doom sync= through rtk's rewriting hook to
   confirm it does not mangle the absolute-path invocation.

** Rollback
Each piece is independently reversible:
- ponytail: =claude plugin uninstall ponytail@ponytail=
- caveman: =npx -y github:JuliusBrussee/caveman -- --uninstall=, or
  =claude plugin uninstall caveman@caveman=
- rtk: =rtk init -g --uninstall=, then =rm ~/.local/bin/rtk=
- This repo's files: =git checkout AGENTS.md= and
  =rm -rf .cursor .windsurf .clinerules .github .opencode= (verify nothing
  else has since been added under =.github/= before removing it)
- Node: =rm -rf ~/.nvm= and delete the =NVM_DIR= block from =~/.bashrc=;
  =/usr/bin/node= needs no restoration, it was never modified

** Revisit when
- A with/without token measurement is actually run — if the stack does not
  clearly beat its own ~5,810 tok/session always-on cost, drop the weakest
  tool (on current numbers, =caveman= is both the most expensive and the most
  intrusive, so it is the first candidate).
- rtk's Bash-rewriting hook is observed mangling any command, particularly
  =~/.config/emacs/bin/doom= invocations or the =git= commands gated by
  [[id:adr-agent-git-authority][ADR-015]]'s confirmation prompts — a rewrite
  that changes a command's shape could bypass a permission rule that matches
  on command prefix.
- The caveman block in =AGENTS.md= is observed changing agent behaviour in a
  way that conflicts with this repo's own instructions (its terse register vs.
  this repo's preference for explicit reasoning in ADRs and docs).
- Any of the three tools adds an MCP server or raises its always-on cost
  materially above the figures recorded here — re-run
  =claude plugin details <plugin>= to check.
- Doom or a system package starts requiring Node ≥22 non-interactively, at
  which point the dual-sourced Node arrangement needs revisiting properly
  (likely by pointing the systemd user environment at the nvm build).
- =mattpocock-skills= is updated past =1.2.3=, which would reset the
  =disable-model-invocation= flags discussed alongside this work.

* ADR-024: centaur-tabs — new tab opens the Doom dashboard; add tab-management keybindings
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (user clicked the =+= button and exercised the new bindings in their own session, 2026-08-23)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-ui-rice-pass][ADR-022]] enabled Doom's =:ui tabs= module (centaur-tabs) as part of the
UI rice pass, but recorded no decision about how tabs actually *behave* — it
scoped only to turning the module on. Two gaps surfaced once the module was
in daily use:

1. Clicking the =+= button at the right end of the tab bar opens a blank
   =Untitled= buffer with =buffer-offer-save= set. That buffer has no
   relation to anything else in this config: it isn't the scratch buffer,
   isn't the dashboard, and prompts to save on kill. The user wanted the
   dashboard there instead — it is already =doom-fallback-buffer=, so a new
   tab and an emptied frame would then agree on what "nothing open" looks
   like.
2. centaur-tabs ships no keybindings of its own, and Doom's =:ui tabs=
   module adds only =gt=/=gT= (=+tabs:next-or-goto=/=+tabs:previous-or-goto=,
   which take a count — =4gt= jumps to the 4th visible tab). Every other tab
   operation — jump-to-arbitrary-tab, reorder, switch group, close the rest
   — needed =M-x=.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first: prefer module-provided mechanisms and Doom's own
  hooks/vars over reimplementation; use =after!=/=map!=, not raw
  =define-key=/=eval-after-load=.
- =AGENTS.md= Evil-first: new bindings must fit Doom's modal conventions
  (=g=-prefixed normal-state operators, leader-prefixed commands under the
  category they belong to), not fight them.
- Both the mouse (=+= button) and the interactive-command path must land in
  the same place — a config where clicking and =M-x= diverge is worse than
  either behavior alone.
- Don't shadow a binding the user actually uses. Shadowing something they
  don't is fine and is the point of a personal config.
- =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-creep=: this is a behavior/binding pass
  over an already-adopted module, not an occasion to restyle the tab bar.

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| New-tab target | Leave the stock =Untitled= buffer | Rejected — unrelated to anything else in this config, and prompts to save on kill (=buffer-offer-save t=) |
| New-tab target | Doom's scratch buffer (=doom/open-scratch-buffer=) | Rejected — the dashboard is already this config's =doom-fallback-buffer=; sending new tabs to scratch would create a *second* "nothing open" answer |
| New-tab target | *Doom dashboard* via =+dashboard/open= | *Chosen* — agrees with =doom-fallback-buffer=, and =+dashboard/open= is Doom's own public autoloaded entry point |
| Interception point | Rebind only the interactive command in =map!= and leave the internals alone | Rejected — the =+= button doesn't go through any user binding; =centaur-tabs-line-format--new-button= wires the mouse event to =centaur-tabs-new-tab--button=, which calls =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= directly. Rebinding only the command would leave click and =M-x= divergent |
| Interception point | =advice-add ... :override= on =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= | *Chosen* — the single chokepoint both entry points funnel through, so one override covers mouse and command together |
| Tab bar on the dashboard | Keep Doom's =+tabs-disable-centaur-tabs-mode-maybe-h= as-is | Rejected — it hides the tab bar in dashboard buffers, i.e. exactly when the new tab opens, leaving no visible way back to the other tabs. Self-defeating for a button whose whole job is opening a tab |
| Tab bar on the dashboard | Remove that hook from =+dashboard-mode-hook= | *Chosen* |
| Tab bar on the dashboard | Remove it from the deprecated =+doom-dashboard-mode-hook= too | Not done — nothing in current Doom runs that deprecated hook name, so removing from =+dashboard-mode-hook= alone is sufficient (see Evidence) |
| Jump-to-tab binding | =ga= (shadows evil's =what-cursor-position=) | *Chosen* — =what-cursor-position= is a debugging curiosity, tab-jumping is a per-minute operation; =M-x what-cursor-position= and =C-x == both still reach it |
| Reorder-tab bindings | =g<= / =g>= | *Chosen* — both unbound in evil and in Doom (see Evidence), and the angle direction matches the motion |
| New-tab leader placement | =SPC t= (toggles) | Rejected — opening a tab isn't a toggle |
| New-tab leader placement | =SPC b t= (buffers) | *Chosen* — a centaur tab *is* a buffer, so it belongs next to =SPC b N= (new empty buffer) |

** Evidence
- *Both entry points confirmed to share one chokepoint, read from source*
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/centaur-tabs=): the mouse path is
  =centaur-tabs-functions.el:637-641= (=centaur-tabs-new-tab--button=,
  which selects the event's window then calls =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab=),
  and the target itself is =centaur-tabs-functions.el:1367-1381=. A
  repo-wide grep for =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= returns exactly those two
  call sites plus the =defun= — no third path, so a single =:override= is
  sufficient.
- *What the stock behavior actually is*: =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= is
  context-aware — =eshell-mode= → =(eshell t)=, =vterm-mode= → =(vterm t)=,
  =term-mode= → =(ansi-term "/bin/bash")=, =eww-mode= → =eww= with a prefix
  arg — and falls through to =centaur-tabs--create-new-empty-buffer=
  (=centaur-tabs-functions.el:1359-1365=), which is the =Untitled= buffer
  with =buffer-offer-save t=. The =:override= discards that context-awareness
  deliberately (see Consequences).
- *The dashboard-hook conflict, read from Doom's own module*
  (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/ui/tabs/config.el=): the module
  registers =+tabs-disable-centaur-tabs-mode-maybe-h= on
  =+dashboard-mode-hook=, =+doom-dashboard-mode-hook= (marked =DEPRECATED= in
  that file), and =+popup-buffer-mode-hook=; the function calls
  =(centaur-tabs-local-mode)= when the mode is on — and =centaur-tabs-local-mode='s
  *on* state means "hide the bar in this buffer". So without removing it the
  bar would vanish the moment the new tab opened.
- *The dashboard is a singleton buffer*: =+dashboard-name= is ="*doom*"=
  (=ui/dashboard/config.el:7=) and =+dashboard/open=
  (=ui/dashboard/autoload.el:9-14=) does =(switch-to-buffer (doom-fallback-buffer))=
  then =(+dashboard-reload t)= — so repeated clicks select the existing
  dashboard tab rather than stacking up new ones. Deliberate, not a
  side effect that needs working around.
- *Binding-collision check, done against real sources rather than assumed*:
  =ga= is =what-cursor-position= in evil's own =evil-maps.el:68= — a real
  shadow, accepted knowingly. =g<= and =g>= return no hits in evil-maps.el
  or anywhere under Doom's modules — genuinely unbound. Doom's =SPC b= map
  (=modules/config/default/+evil-bindings.el:393-425=) has =t=, =T=, and
  lowercase =o= all free; note that Doom binds *uppercase* =SPC b O= to
  =doom/kill-other-buffers=, so the new =SPC b o= ("kill other tabs in
  group") sits directly beside its buffer-level analogue rather than
  clobbering it.
- *What Doom already provided, so it wasn't re-added*:
  =ui/tabs/autoload.el= defines =+tabs:next-or-goto=/=+tabs:previous-or-goto=
  as count-taking =evil-define-command='s, and =editor/evil/config.el:538=
  binds =gt=. Those were left untouched.
- *=centaur-tabs-ace-jump= arg semantics read from source*
  (=centaur-tabs-interactive.el:389-402=): plain call jumps, =C-u= swaps the
  current tab with the selected one, =C-u C-u= closes the selected tab —
  which is what the inline comment on the =ga= binding in =config.el=
  records.
- *Live verification*: the user exercised this in their own interactive
  session on 2026-08-23 — the =+= button opens the dashboard with the tab bar
  still visible, and the new bindings work.

** Decision
In =config.el= (lines 281-331), inside an =(after! centaur-tabs ...)= block:

- Define =+my/centaur-tabs-new-tab-dashboard=, calling
  =(+dashboard/open (selected-frame))=, and install it with
  =(advice-add 'centaur-tabs--create-new-tab :override #'...)= so the =+=
  button and the interactive command both land on the dashboard.
- =(remove-hook '+dashboard-mode-hook #'+tabs-disable-centaur-tabs-mode-maybe-h)=
  so the tab bar stays visible in the buffer the new tab lands in.

And in a =(map! :after centaur-tabs ...)= block:

- Normal state: =ga= → =centaur-tabs-ace-jump=, =g<= →
  =centaur-tabs-move-current-tab-to-left=, =g>= →
  =centaur-tabs-move-current-tab-to-right=.
- Leader, under =SPC b=: =t= → =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= ("New tab
  (dashboard)"), =T= → =centaur-tabs-switch-group=, =o= →
  =centaur-tabs-kill-other-buffers-in-current-group=.

No =init.el=/=packages.el= change — centaur-tabs was already installed and
pinned by =:ui tabs= ([[id:adr-ui-rice-pass][ADR-022]]).

** Consequences
- The stock context-awareness of =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= is gone: a
  new tab from an =eshell=/=term=/=eww= buffer now opens the dashboard rather
  than a second shell/browser. The =vterm= branch is moot in this config
  anyway ([[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]] removed vterm entirely, and
  centaur-tabs has no ghostel branch), but the eshell/eww cases are a real,
  knowingly-accepted loss — revisit if the user starts wanting them.
- This config now carries an =advice-add ... :override= on a *private*
  (double-dash) upstream function. That is the most upgrade-brittle shape in
  this repo: a rename or signature change in centaur-tabs silently
  reintroduces the =Untitled= behavior rather than erroring. Mitigated for
  now by Doom pinning centaur-tabs (=5ec350da6cacc34ac0efaac17d6ac5031ef82bd4=)
  — see Revisit when.
- Removing =+tabs-disable-centaur-tabs-mode-maybe-h= from
  =+dashboard-mode-hook= means the tab bar is now visible on *every*
  dashboard, not only dashboards reached via the =+= button. That's the
  intended trade; popups still hide the bar, since that hook remains on
  =+popup-buffer-mode-hook= untouched.
- =ga= no longer runs =what-cursor-position= in normal state. =C-x == and
  =M-x= still reach it.
- Fills the behavior/binding gap [[id:adr-ui-rice-pass][ADR-022]] left open — that ADR adopted the
  tabs module without deciding how tabs behave. It also settles ADR-022's
  open "centaur-tabs not yet visually confirmed" item as a side effect: tabs
  demonstrably render, since the user clicked one.

** Rejected alternatives
See Candidates table above.

** Validation
- Live-verified by the user in their own interactive session, 2026-08-23: the
  =+= button opens the Doom dashboard (not =Untitled=), the tab bar stays
  visible there, and the new bindings behave as described.
- Source-level checks in Evidence above: single-chokepoint confirmation via
  grep over centaur-tabs' whole source, hook conflict read from Doom's
  =:ui tabs= module, and collision checks for =ga=/=g<=/=g>=/=SPC b t T o=
  against evil's and Doom's actual keymap definitions.
- No =doom sync= run and no =doom doctor= re-run: this is a =config.el=-only
  change with no module, package, or pin change, which is outside the scope
  of =AGENTS.md='s "after any =init.el=/=packages.el= change" /"after any
  module or package change" rules. Stated explicitly rather than left to be
  inferred.

** Rollback
- Delete the =(after! centaur-tabs ...)= and =(map! :after centaur-tabs ...)=
  blocks from =config.el= (lines 281-331). That restores the stock
  =Untitled=-buffer new tab, Doom's bar-hiding on the dashboard, and evil's
  =ga=; =gt=/=gT= are Doom's and are unaffected either way.
- Partial rollback is safe and independent: dropping just the =advice-add=
  (and its =remove-hook=) keeps the bindings, and dropping just the =map!=
  block keeps the dashboard behavior.

** Revisit when
- Doom unpins or bumps centaur-tabs past
  =5ec350da6cacc34ac0efaac17d6ac5031ef82bd4= — re-check that
  =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= still exists under that name and is still
  the only path the =+= button takes, since a silent rename degrades to the
  old =Untitled= behavior with no error.
- Doom's =:ui tabs= module grows its own new-tab command or binding set
  upstream — prefer the module's version over this local override, per the
  Doom-first rule.
- The user starts using eshell or eww tabs and wants context-aware new-tab
  back — at that point the =:override= should become a conditional wrapper
  (dashboard only in the fall-through branch) rather than a blanket replacement.
- The dashboard stops being =doom-fallback-buffer= (a different fallback, or
  the dashboard module disabled) — the "new tab and emptied frame agree"
  premise is what justifies the target choice, and it would no longer hold.

* ADR-025: Calendar via Doom =:app calendar= — calfw view plus two-way org-gcal sync for both Google accounts
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-calendar-org-gcal
:STATUS:   Implemented in config; not live-verified, and the remaining work was re-scoped 2026-08-30 — see LOGBOOK
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Re-scope [2026-08-30 Sun] — two findings from [[id:roadmap-phase-7][Phase 7]] change what is left
  here, and both are worse than "blocked on a Google scope change".

  1. *=~/org/calendar/= was unreachable from the agenda.* =org-agenda-files=
     only ever resolved to two top-level files ([[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]]'s LOGBOOK has the
     measurement), and org-gcal's own output directory was not among them. This
     ADR's validation step — "events appear in the agenda" — could not have
     passed even with a working authorization. Fixed in =config.org=; the
     validation step is now meaningful for the first time.
  2. *The =pass= store does not exist.* =mail/oauth2-google-client= is gone
     rather than merely unreadable, so the first blocker may be *re-create the
     OAuth client in the Google Cloud console*, not *add a Calendar scope to
     the existing one*. See [[id:inv-homedir-state-loss][inventory.org]].

  Resolved in exchange: the client-id *field name*, an open question since this
  ADR was written, is =client_id=. Settled from source rather than guessed —
  =+pass-get-field= is a =defalias= onto =auth-source-pass-parse-entry=, which
  treats line 1 as the secret and splits later lines at their first colon into
  trimmed =key: value= pairs (=auth-source-pass.el:219-233=). =config.org=
  tries =client_id= / =client-id= / =login= / =user= in order, so any resolves.
- Implemented [2026-08-23 Sun]: =config.el= now carries the credential block,
  =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist=, the =SPC o c= / =SPC o C= bindings and the
  agenda-open hook; =~/org/calendar/= exists with the two org-gcal-owned
  files. =doom doctor= clean (77 modules, 230 packages, only the pre-existing
  =markdown +tree-sitter= warning). Verified in a disposable headless daemon:
  the hook is attached, the interval is 3600s, both leader bindings resolve,
  =(require 'org-gcal)= loads without error, and =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist=
  holds the two expected pairs. Still unverified because it needs the user:
  the Google Cloud scope change, the first authorization, and every live
  behavior in Validation below.
- API verified against the installed source [2026-08-23 Sun], resolving this
  ADR's largest evidence gap. =org-gcal-client-id=, =org-gcal-client-secret=,
  =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist= and =org-gcal-sync= all exist as assumed, at the
  module's pin (=7304b592=). Three corrections came out of the reading, and
  the implementation follows the source rather than this ADR's original text:
  1. *The credentials cannot be lazy.* Decision item 3 called for
     [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s =(lambda () (+pass-get-secret ...))= pattern. org-gcal reads
     both variables' literal values as it loads and copies them into
     =oauth2-auto-additional-providers-alist=, so they have to be real strings
     in the variables, followed by =org-gcal-reload-client-id-secret= to
     rebuild that alist. Deferred loading still keeps the =gpg= prompt out of
     startup; a lambda would simply not work.
  2. *Tokens are oauth2-auto's, not org-gcal's.* This version authenticates
     through =oauth2-auto= (a hard dependency), which stores tokens in
     =oauth2-auto-plstore=. Doom's module =defvar= of =org-gcal-token-file= is
     vestigial — the variable survives as a =defcustom= but nothing reads it.
     =config.el= points =oauth2-auto-plstore= at =doom-state-dir= rather than
     leaving it in =user-emacs-directory=, which here is Doom's own git
     checkout.
  3. *=org-gcal-sync= is already async* (=deferred=), so the Consequences
     preference for an async path in the hook is satisfied by the ordinary
     command; no special handling was needed. It does take a =SILENT=
     argument, used by the hook.
  Two hardening changes fell out of testing rather than reading. Both pass
  lookups use =+pass-get-field='s NOERROR argument: =+pass-get-secret= signals
  on a missing entry, and that signal escapes the =require= inside
  =org-gcal-sync=, which from the agenda hook would break opening the agenda
  at all — reproduced in the sandbox, where the password store isn't visible.
  And the hook now skips the sync entirely unless =oauth2-auto-plstore=
  exists, so a session with no stored token never starts an authorization flow
  (browser hand-off, plus a blocking =read-string= for the returned code in
  oauth2-auto's manual mode) from inside a hook that runs while the agenda is
  being drawn. Authorization stays a user-initiated act via =SPC o C=.
- Module enabled [2026-08-23 Sun]: the non-language module sweep
  ([[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]]) turned on =:app calendar=, so calfw, calfw-org/cal/ical and
  org-gcal are installed at the module's pins. Only the package half of this
  ADR is done — no OAuth credentials in =pass=, no
  =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist=, no =~/org/calendar/= directory yet.
:END:

** Context
"Calendar" has sat in [[id:roadmap-future][roadmap.org]]'s Future-phases list since Phase 1,
deliberately unscoped. With Phase 4 (Org Agenda and Capture basics) now
live-verified, the agenda exists but knows nothing about the user's real
Google calendars, so scheduling still happens outside Emacs entirely.

This entry scopes the phase; it does not implement it. Nothing here has been
installed or run yet.

Two prior decisions shape it more than anything else:

- [[id:adr-org-agenda-capture-basics][ADR-017]] set =org-agenda-files= to the whole of =org-directory=
  (=config.el:764=), so *any* org file under =~/org= is picked up by the
  agenda automatically. A calendar file placed there needs no agenda wiring
  at all — but equally, anything org-gcal writes there becomes agenda
  content immediately, correct or not.
- [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]] already established Google OAuth for both mail accounts
  (=mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca=, both authorized via
  =oama authorize google=), including the scar worth remembering: while the
  Google Cloud consent screen was in Testing mode, refresh tokens silently
  expired after 7 days. That was fixed by publishing the consent screen, and
  the same OAuth client (=mail/oauth2-google-client= in =pass=) is the
  natural credential source here — org-gcal would be its second consumer.

** Decision drivers
- User's explicit scoping answers, given after being shown the trade-offs:
  two-way sync (not read-only), both accounts, Doom's module as-is (not
  hand-picked packages), a dedicated org-gcal-owned directory, and sync on
  both a manual command and agenda-open.
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first: prefer the module over hand-declared packages, even
  when the module bundles something not strictly needed.
- =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-creep=: scope to a working calendar view
  plus sync for the two accounts that already exist. Not in scope: CalDAV for
  non-Google sources, the =tricca=/Gandi account (still blocked, see ADR-011),
  meeting-invite handling from mu4e, or org-gcal-driven event *creation*
  workflows beyond what plain org editing gives.
- =.claude/rules/security.md=: credentials come from =pass= via
  =+pass-get-secret=, never interpolated into shell strings, never printed.
- The =:tools pass= =+auth= arrangement from [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]] is the established
  pattern for exactly this and should be reused, not re-invented.

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Overall ambition | Read-only view of Google events in calfw | Rejected (user's explicit choice) — safest, but leaves scheduling itself outside Emacs, which is the actual problem |
| Overall ambition | Local org only, no Google at all | Rejected — smallest phase, but a calendar view of what the agenda already knows adds little |
| Overall ambition | *Two-way org-gcal sync* | *Chosen* (user's explicit choice, made with the overwrite risk stated) — events pull into org and edits push back |
| Packages | Doom =:app calendar= module as-is (calfw, calfw-org/cal/ical, org-gcal) | *Chosen* — Doom-first; org-gcal is wanted here anyway, so the bundling objection is moot |
| Packages | Hand-declare calfw/calfw-org in =packages.el=, skip org-gcal | Rejected — breaks the Doom-first rule for no benefit once two-way sync is in scope |
| Accounts | =personal= only | Rejected (user chose both) |
| Accounts | *Both =personal= and =ualberta=* | *Chosen* — with a known risk that =ualberta= is Google Workspace, whose admins can restrict third-party API access independently of the consent screen; mail working there does not guarantee calendar will |
| File layout | Single =~/org/calendar.org= for both accounts | Rejected — org-gcal maps one calendar id per file, so two accounts in one file is awkward and makes per-account rollback impossible |
| File layout | Sync into the existing =todo.org= | Rejected outright — org-gcal rewrites the files it owns; pointing it at a hand-edited file is how real notes get destroyed |
| File layout | *Dedicated =~/org/calendar/= with =personal.org= and =ualberta.org=* | *Chosen* — org-gcal owns the directory exclusively, nothing hand-written lives there, and per-account rollback is just deleting one file. Picked up by the agenda automatically via ADR-017 |
| Sync trigger | Manual command only | Rejected (user chose more) — most predictable, but requires remembering to sync |
| Sync trigger | *Manual command plus auto-sync on agenda open* | *Chosen* (user's explicit choice, made after being told it can delay =SPC n a=) — mirrors the elfeed auto-update-on-open pattern from [[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]]; needs the mitigation in Consequences |
| Sync trigger | Background periodic timer | Rejected — a timer writing to org files while the user may be editing them is the riskiest option under a two-way sync |
| Credentials | Reuse the existing =mail/oauth2-google-client= pass entry | *Chosen (provisional)* — same Google Cloud OAuth client, already published; needs the Calendar scope added to that client's consent screen |
| Credentials | Register a second, calendar-only OAuth client | Rejected for now — doubles the consent-screen maintenance that ADR-011 already showed to be the fragile part |

** Evidence
- *Doom's module read directly*
  (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/app/calendar/=): =packages.el=
  declares =calfw= (pinned =24fa167a=) with =calfw-org=/=calfw-cal=/=calfw-ical=,
  plus =org-gcal= (pinned =7304b592=). =config.el= already provides a full
  evil keymap for =calfw-calendar-mode-map= and =calfw-details-mode-map=
  (=D=/=M=/=T=/=W= view switching, =hjkl= navigation, =gr= refresh, =t= today),
  marks calfw buffers real, and defvars =org-gcal-dir= and =org-gcal-token-file=
  (=token.gpg= under Doom's profile cache dir). So keybindings and token
  placement are already handled upstream — this phase should not reimplement
  them.
- *Module is currently disabled*: =init.el:187= has =;;calendar= commented out
  under =:app=.
- *Agenda pickup needs no work*: =config.el:764= sets
  =(setq org-agenda-files (list org-directory))=, and =org-directory= is
  =~/org/= (=config.el:60=). =~/org/= currently holds =daily/=, =doom.org=,
  =marxism-notes=, =mtg=, =personal=, =test=, =todo.org=, =work= — no
  =calendar/= yet, so the proposed directory does not collide with anything.
- *Both accounts are Google-backed*: [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]] records =oama authorize google=
  runs for both =mrniceguyjames@gmail.com= and =jcook@ualberta.ca=, against a
  single Google Cloud OAuth client stored as =mail/oauth2-google-client=,
  whose consent screen was moved from Testing to In-production (unverified) on
  2026-08-06 after the 7-day refresh-token expiry regression.
- *=gpg= is present* at =/usr/bin/gpg=, which =org-gcal-token-file='s =.gpg=
  storage requires.

*Gaps in this evidence, stated rather than papered over:*

- *org-gcal's actual API was not verified.* [*Resolved 2026-08-23* — the
  package is installed and its source was read; see the LOGBOOK entry above
  for what held and what didn't. The original gap is kept below as written.]
  The package is not installed
  (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/repos/= has neither =org-gcal= nor
  =calfw=), and the agent's sandbox cannot reach =github.com= or =melpa.org=
  to read its source (the same restriction documented in [[id:adr-ui-rice-pass][ADR-022]]'s
  Evidence). So every org-gcal variable named in this ADR —
  =org-gcal-client-id=, =org-gcal-client-secret=, =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist=,
  =org-gcal-sync= — is *expected*, not confirmed, API. First implementation
  step is to install and read the source, and correct this ADR if it differs.
  ADR-022 has direct precedent for why this matters: =pulsar-pulse= was
  written into the config from memory and turned out to be obsolete in the
  installed version.
- *The =pass= entry was not verified to exist by this agent.* =pass= is not on
  =PATH= in the agent's sandbox and =~/.password-store= is not visible to it,
  so =mail/oauth2-google-client= is taken from ADR-011's record rather than
  checked. Verify before relying on it.
- *Whether =ualberta='s Workspace tenant permits third-party Calendar API
  access is unknown* and cannot be determined from here.

** Decision
Scope Phase 5 (Calendar) as:

1. =init.el=: enable =:app calendar=, then =doom sync=.
2. Google Cloud: add the Calendar scope to the existing OAuth client behind
   =mail/oauth2-google-client=; re-authorize both accounts. User action, not
   agent-doable.
3. =config.el=: set =org-gcal-client-id=/=org-gcal-client-secret= from that
   entry, and map =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist= to =~/org/calendar/personal.org=
   and =~/org/calendar/ualberta.org=. (*Corrected during implementation*: this
   item originally specified [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s lazy
   =(lambda () (+pass-get-secret ...))= pattern with no value stored in a
   variable. org-gcal's actual API makes that impossible — see the LOGBOOK
   entry above.)
4. =config.el=: a leader binding for manual =org-gcal-sync= (always syncs),
   plus an agenda-open hook gated by a staleness guard — a last-sync
   timestamp and a minimum-interval variable, skipping the fetch when the
   data is fresh enough. The threshold value is an implementation detail to
   settle with the user during the phase, not fixed here.
5. Live-verify in the user's own session: view renders, events pull down for
   both accounts, an edit pushes back, and a token survives past the ~7-day
   mark that bit ADR-011.

** Consequences
- =~/org/calendar/= becomes org-gcal-owned. Nothing hand-written may live
  there — that ownership boundary is the entire safety argument for choosing
  two-way sync, and it holds only as long as it is respected.
- Auto-sync on agenda open puts a network call in front of =SPC n a=, a
  binding used many times a day. A staleness guard is therefore *required*,
  not optional (user's explicit position when this was raised): the
  agenda-open hook must record the last successful sync time and skip the
  fetch if it is under a threshold, so opening the agenda repeatedly costs one
  network call rather than one per open. The manual sync command stays
  unguarded — an explicit request should always sync. If org-gcal offers an
  async fetch path, prefer it for the hook as well, so a hung call degrades to
  stale data instead of a frozen agenda.
- The Google OAuth client gains a second consumer. A scope change or
  re-authorization now affects mail *and* calendar together, so an ADR-011-style
  token regression would break both at once — a real coupling cost of reusing
  one client, accepted deliberately over doubling consent-screen maintenance.
- Two-way sync makes this the first part of the config that writes to a
  remote service rather than to local, git-recoverable files. The blast radius
  is real but modest, and worth stating precisely rather than dramatically:
  Google Calendar keeps deleted events in Trash for ~30 days and org-gcal's
  writes are ordinary event mutations, so a bad sync is usually recoverable by
  hand. The part that is *not* recoverable is narrower — an event with other
  attendees emails them on every change, so a spurious edit or delete notifies
  real people, and restoring the event does not unsend that. Solo events are
  cheap to get wrong; shared ones are not.
- calfw's keybindings arrive from Doom's module, so =docs/ai/keybindings.org=
  is not the right home for them; only the new leader binding for sync is
  this repo's own.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above, which carries the fate and reason for each.

** Validation
Nothing to validate yet — this ADR is scope, not implementation. The phase is
done when, in the user's own session: calfw renders both calendars, an event
created in Google appears in org after a sync, an edit made in org appears in
Google, =SPC n a= is not noticeably slower, =doom doctor= is clean, and a
refresh token has survived past ~7 days (the specific failure ADR-011 hit).

** Rollback
- =init.el=: re-comment =;;calendar= under =:app=, then =doom sync=.
- =config.el=: remove the org-gcal credential/file-alist block, the sync
  binding, and the agenda hook.
- Delete =~/org/calendar/=. Because org-gcal owns that directory exclusively,
  deleting it cannot lose hand-written content — the reason the layout was
  chosen.
- Per-account rollback is finer-grained: drop one file from
  =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist= and delete it.
- Google-side: remove the Calendar scope from the OAuth client's consent
  screen. Mail keeps working, since it uses a different scope on the same
  client.

** Revisit when
- org-gcal's real API turns out to differ from what this ADR assumes — correct
  this entry rather than silently coding around it.
- =ualberta='s Workspace admins block Calendar API access, in which case the
  phase drops to =personal= only and =ualberta= joins =tricca= as a
  provider-blocked account in [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]'s sense.
- The agenda becomes noticeably slower to open — revisit the auto-sync
  trigger before revisiting anything else, since it is the one piece added
  against a stated performance concern.
- A non-Google calendar source appears (a CalDAV server, an institutional
  Exchange calendar), at which point =calfw-ical= and a CalDAV path enter
  scope and this decision's Google-only assumption needs re-examining.
- The =tricca=/Gandi account becomes unblocked, since it would need an
  entirely different sync mechanism from org-gcal.

* ADR-026: =init.el= module-flag audit — annotate every flag, prune dead modules, enable the flags that earn their keep
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-init-flag-audit
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (user restarted Emacs 2026-08-23 and reported the config loading and running with no breakage; individual flags not itemized)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Second pass [2026-08-23 Sun]: after reviewing the preference-tier flags, the
  user enabled =:lang org +pretty +noter +dragndrop=, =:checkers spell
  +everywhere=, and all five modules this ADR had added commented-out that
  were not language modules: =:emacs eww=, =:emacs tramp=, =:editor
  whitespace +guess +trim=, =:ui smooth-scroll +interpolate=, =:tools llm=.
  The five =:lang= additions (=ada=, =graphviz=, =janet=, =odin=, =scad=)
  stay commented out. Module count 50 → 55, =doom doctor= package count 176
  → 188.
- Pending apt step completed [2026-08-23 Sun]: the user installed =gnuplot=
  6.0, =graphviz= 2.43.0 (=dot=), =sqlite3= 3.45.1, =wordnet= (=wn=), and
  =maim= 5.7.4. =doom doctor= is now down to a single warning — the standing
  "tree-sitter rudimentary before Emacs 31" caveat on =markdown
  +tree-sitter=, which no action here can clear.
- Caveat recorded, not yet exercised [2026-08-23 Sun]: =:tools llm= declares
  its own pinned =gptel= (=tools/llm/packages.el=) while =packages.el=
  already declares =gptel= unpinned for [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]], and =tools/llm/config.el=
  sets =gptel-display-buffer-action= to nil plus its own =set-popup-rule!=
  against this repo's =^\*gptel\*= rule in =config.el=. Doom resolves both
  in the user's favour by load order, but that is reasoning about load
  order, not an observation — verify in the live session that the Anthropic
  default backend and the right-side gptel popup still behave as [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]
  intended. The module also adds =gptel-magit=, =gptel-quick=, and
  =ob-gptel=, which are new capability, not overlap.
- Caveat resolved, module given precedence [2026-08-23 Sun]: the "load order
  resolves it in the user's favour" reasoning above was wrong on both counts.
  (a) The bare =(package! gptel)= in =packages.el= merged nothing — =package!=
  only merges non-nil keys (=lisp/doom-packages.el=), so the module's
  =:pin "ebf0f3d"= + =:recipe (:nonrecursive t)= were already what was
  installed. (b) This repo's =^\*gptel\*= popup rule never matched anything:
  gptel names its buffer =*<backend-name>*= (=gptel.el:1904=), i.e. =*Claude*=
