  under [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s Anthropic default — so the module's predicate rule (any
  =gptel-mode= buffer) was the only one ever firing, and the live popup is
  bottom/0.3/=:quit nil=, not ADR-006's right/0.42/=:quit t=. At the user's
  direction the module now owns gptel outright: =(package! gptel)=,
  =(setq gptel-default-mode 'org-mode)=, and the dead =set-popup-rule!= were
  deleted from this repo, leaving only what the module does not do — the
  =+pass-get-secret= backends, =gptel-directives=, =my/gptel-add-diagnostics=,
  and the =SPC a= namespace. =SPC a= is kept as-is per [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]; the module's
  =SPC o l= map is an additional entry point. =doom sync= + =doom doctor= clean
  (the standing tree-sitter warning only). *Correction, same day:* the "=SPC a=
  kept, =SPC o l= additional" half of this entry was superseded within the hour
  by [[id:adr-llm-module-owns-ai-keys][ADR-028]], which also found that =SPC o l= was never actually the module's
  — [[id:adr-app-launcher][ADR-020]]'s launcher had been shadowing it since this ADR enabled =:tools llm=.
- Sweep continued, one annotation already stale [2026-08-23 Sun]: this ADR
  stopped at "annotate and prune"; [[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]] then walked the user through the
  disabled *non-language* modules and enabled 22 of them (55 → 77 modules,
  188 → 230 packages). That pass also found this ADR's flag annotation for
  =:tools debugger= to be wrong on the day it was written — the module ships
  =dape= and =tools/debugger/README.org= says it has no flags, not =[+lsp]=.
  Treat the annotations as dated against doom core =ad55ed0=, exactly as
  =init.el='s header comment says.
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — the user
  restarted Emacs with all audited flags active and reported everything
  working. That restart is what this ADR's Validation section was waiting on.
  The five spot-checks it named (dirvish, a popup, corfu's dabbrev fallback,
  forge, a tree-sitter buffer) were not reported back one by one, so read this
  as "nothing broke on load or in normal use", not a per-flag exercise.
:END:

** Context
=init.el= had drifted from Doom's own module tree in three separate ways, none
of them visible from the file itself. Its comments were Doom's stock
one-line jokes, which say what a module *is* but never what it can be
*configured to do* — so the only way to learn a module's available flags was
to leave the file and read the module's =README.org=. Meanwhile the file
still listed five modules that no longer exist upstream, and omitted ten that
do. The user asked for the comments to reflect "every toggle I have access
to", then, once the flag inventory existed, for the flags worth having to be
turned on.

** Decision drivers
- The information already exists in this machine's checkout
  (=~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules=); the cost was purely that it
  wasn't *where the decision is made*. Per =.claude/rules/doom-emacs.md=,
  module names must be checked against that tree rather than assumed —
  putting the flag list in =init.el= makes that check the default rather
  than an act of diligence.
- =std-no-scope-creep=: annotate and prune first; enabling flags is a
  separate, explicitly-approved step, not a side effect of documenting them.
- Existing decisions constrain which flags are eligible: [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] (one engine
  per completion axis), [[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]] (ghostel is the only terminal), and this
  repo's live flycheck/doom-modeline/lsp-mode configuration in =config.el=,
  which several flags would silently replace.
- Nothing may be enabled whose prerequisite binary is absent without saying
  so — [[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]]'s precedent (=mpv=/=yt-dlp= installed by the user, recorded
  in inventory) is the pattern to follow, not a warning to live with.

** Candidates
| Decision point | Candidates | Fate |
|-----------------+-------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------|
| Where flag lists come from | Doom's online module index / each =README.org= "Module flags" section / each module's own =modulep!= calls / the new =.doommodule= =(flags ...)= form | Used README ∪ self-=modulep!=. The =.doommodule= form is authoritative but only two modules (=lang/org=, =term/ghostel=) have migrated to it in core =ad55ed0=, so it can't be the sole source yet |
| Annotation format | Separate flag-reference doc / per-line =[+a +b]= suffix / replace the joke descriptions | Per-line suffix, keeping Doom's descriptions — the decision is made on that line, so the data belongs on that line |
| Modules deleted upstream | Leave commented out / annotate as dead / delete | Deleted, after annotating them first so the removal was reviewable in the diff |
| Modules missing from the file | Ignore / add commented-out | Added commented-out, with upstream's own =#+subtitle= as the description — an absent line is indistinguishable from a rejected one |
| Flags that swap an engine (=+flymake=, =+eglot=, =+light=) | Enable / skip | Skipped — each would orphan working =config.el= code (see Evidence) |
| Flags needing a missing binary | Enable and accept warnings / skip / install the binary first | Install first where the binary was installable, then enable. Two remain pending on =sudo= (see Consequences) |

** Evidence
- Flag inventory built by script over
  =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/*/*/=: each module's =README.org=
  "Module flags" section unioned with =grep -rhoE "modulep!\s+\+[a-z...]"=
  over its own source (self-referential flags only — cross-module
  =(modulep! :category name +flag)= checks were matched separately so that
  another module's flags never leak into this one's list).
- Cross-checking those two sources found exactly three disagreements, all
  resolved against the source: =emacs/dired +ranger= (=dired/doctor.el:6-7=
  — "The +ranger flag was removed from this module and does nothing"),
  =lang/org +roam2= (=org/doctor.el:8-9= — deprecated alias for =+roam=;
  =lang/org/.doommodule= lists it under =(obsolete)= as =(+roam2 +roam
  "25.09")=), and =lang/org +uv=, which appears only at
  =lang/org/.doommodule:115= guarding a =gnuplot= executable check — an
  upstream typo for =+gnuplot=, not a real flag. All three were excluded
  from the annotations.
- Five modules in =init.el= had no counterpart in the module tree: =:ui
  hydra=, =:tools prodigy=, =:tools rgb=, =:tools taskrunner=, =:app
  twitter=.
- Ten modules existed upstream but were absent from =init.el=: =:lang= =ada=,
  =graphviz=, =janet=, =odin=, =scad=; =:emacs= =eww=, =tramp=; =:editor
  whitespace=; =:ui smooth-scroll=; =:tools llm=.
- Engine-swap conflicts were read out of this repo's own =config.el=, not
  assumed: =+flymake= would displace the flycheck checker chain at
  =config.el:385-410= (a =python-ruff= checker chained after =lsp=);
  =+light= would drop =doom-modeline=, which =config.el:184-212= configures
  in detail; =+eglot= would orphan the =lsp-managed-mode-hook= wiring and
  =claude-code-ide-diagnostics-backend 'flycheck= at =config.el:730=.
- Binary prerequisites were checked with =command -v= before deciding, not
  after: =aspell= and =enchant-2= present (=checkers/spell/config.el:23-28=
  shows the module already auto-detects aspell, so =+aspell= only pins what
  was already happening); =pandoc= present; =grip=, =gnuplot=, =jupyter=,
  =wn=, =texlab=, =marksman=, =bash-language-server=,
  =vscode-json-language-server=, =dot=, =sqlite3= all absent.
- =sqlite-open= is =t= on this Emacs (30.2, checked with =emacs -Q
  --batch=), so =+forge='s historical =emacsql-sqlite= compile cost does not
  apply here.
- =+booster= was rejected on a documented version constraint, not taste:
  =tools/lsp='s README scopes it to "Emacs 29 users only".
- =doom sync= after the change: exit 0, "All 219 packages are up-to-date"
  (that figure counts dependencies). =doom doctor='s own package count, the
  one earlier entries quote, went 158 → 176; module count stayed 50. Doctor
  reports 4 warnings, all binary-related and all named in Consequences.

** Decision
- =init.el=: every module line carries a =[+flag ...]= suffix listing the
  full flag set that module accepts, enabled or not; a line with no bracket
  takes no flags. A header NOTE states the convention, where the lists came
  from, and that they must be re-derived after a =doom upgrade= rather than
  trusted indefinitely.
- Deleted the five upstream-removed modules; added the ten missing ones
  commented out, described with upstream's own =#+subtitle=.
- Enabled, as flags on already-enabled modules: =corfu +dabbrev=, =vertico
  +icons=, =ligatures +extra=, =popup +all=, =dired +dirvish=, =ghostel
  +everywhere=, =spell +aspell=, =lookup +dictionary +docsets +offline=,
  =lsp +peek=, =magit +forge=, =json +lsp +tree-sitter=, =latex +lsp=,
  =markdown +lsp +grip +tree-sitter=, =org +pandoc +gnuplot +jupyter=,
  =python +tree-sitter=, =sh +lsp=, =default +gnupg=. (=org +roam= was
  enabled in the same pass but is its own decision — see [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]].)
- Installed the prerequisite binaries that did not need root: =grip= and
  =jupyter=/=ipykernel= via =pipx=, =bash-language-server= and
  =vscode-langservers-extracted= via =npm install -g --prefix ~/.local=,
  =texlab= and =marksman= as upstream release binaries into =~/.local/bin=.
- Explicitly *not* enabled, each for a stated reason: =+flymake=, =+eglot=,
  =+light= (would orphan working =config.el=); =+offlineimap= (mutually
  exclusive with =+mbsync=, [[id:adr-email-mu4e][ADR-011]]); =python +uv= (upstream documents it
  as incompatible with =+pyenv=); =python +conda= (a second environment
  manager beside pyenv/poetry, against [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]'s rule); =undo +tree=
  (upstream calls undo-tree "a little less stable" than the default
  undo-fu); =format +lsp=; =spell +hunspell=/=+enchant=; =lsp +booster=
  (Emacs 29 only); =sh +fish=/=+powershell= (user does not use either
  shell).

** Consequences
- =init.el= is now the flag reference, which means it is also a thing that
  can go stale. The header NOTE carries its own provenance (source tree,
  date, doom core commit) precisely so a future reader can tell whether to
  trust it — treat a =doom upgrade= as invalidating it.
- Package count rose 158 → 176 by =doom doctor='s count (219 including
  dependencies, per =doom sync=). The additions are real subsystems, not
  cosmetics: org-roam, dirvish, forge (+ ghub/closql/emacsql), lsp-ui,
  jupyter (+ zmq), grip-mode, dash-docs, wordnut, synosaurus, define-word.
  Startup cost has not been measured — see Validation.
- =doom doctor= is *not* clean: 4 warnings remain. Three clear with one
  =apt= command the agent cannot run (=sudo apt install gnuplot graphviz
  sqlite3=, for =org-plot/gnuplot=, org-roam's graph view, and dash-docs
  respectively); =wordnet= belongs in the same command for =+offline='s
  wordnut backend, which =doom doctor= does not check for. The fourth
  ("Tree-sitter support before Emacs 31 is rudimentary") is a standing
  upstream caveat on =markdown +tree-sitter=, not a fixable gap.
- =popup +all= and =dired +dirvish= are the two changes most likely to feel
  wrong in daily use rather than fail outright — the first rewrites where
  stray buffers appear, the second replaces the dired UI. Both are one-flag
  reversions.
- =node= is v18.19.1 and npm emitted =EBADENGINE= warnings for both language
  servers; both nonetheless run (=bash-language-server --version= → 5.6.0;
  the JSON server's "Connection input stream is not set" is its normal
  refusal to start without =--stdio=, which lsp-mode supplies). A Node
  upgrade would remove the warning class entirely and is worth doing on its
  own terms.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above, which carries the fate and reason for each.

** Validation
Structural and tooling-level only. =doom sync= exits 0 and =doom doctor=
reports exactly the four warnings above — no module or package errors. The
annotation pass was verified non-functional by diffing both files with
comments stripped ("ACTIVE CODE IDENTICAL") *before* any flag was changed, so
the annotation and the enabling are separable in review. What has *not*
happened: no live Emacs session has loaded this configuration. Nothing here
is live-verified until the user restarts Emacs and exercises, at minimum,
dirvish, a popup, corfu's dabbrev fallback, forge against this repo, and one
tree-sitter-backed buffer.

*Update [2026-08-23 Sun]:* the restart happened and the user reported all of
it good — the config loads and runs with the audited flags active, closing the
"no live session has loaded this" gap. The five spot-checks above were not
reported back individually, so the honest reading is "no breakage on load or
in normal use"; a flag never reached in practice is still untested.

** Rollback
Per flag, remove it from its module's flag list in =init.el= and =doom
sync=; nothing else in the repo depends on any of them. To revert the whole
pass, =git checkout main -- init.el= at the commit before this ADR and =doom
sync= — the package set shrinks back on its own. The installed binaries are
independent of the config: =pipx uninstall grip jupyterlab=, =npm uninstall
-g --prefix ~/.local bash-language-server vscode-langservers-extracted=, and
=rm ~/.local/bin/{texlab,marksman}=.

** Revisit when
- A =doom upgrade= lands — the flag annotations are a snapshot of core
  =ad55ed0= and upstream both adds flags and retires them (=+ranger= and
  =+roam2= are this pass's own evidence of that).
- More modules migrate to the =.doommodule= =(flags ...)= form, at which
  point the annotations can be regenerated from an authoritative list rather
  than a README-plus-grep union.
- The =sudo apt install gnuplot graphviz sqlite3 wordnet= step happens —
  =doom doctor= should then be clean except the Emacs-31 markdown caveat,
  and =docs/inventory.org= needs those four rows.
- Startup time becomes noticeable, in which case these new packages are the
  first place to look, and =org +pretty=-style cosmetics are the cheapest to
  drop.
- Emacs 31 becomes available, which resolves the markdown tree-sitter caveat
  and makes =lsp +booster= irrelevant permanently.

* ADR-027: Run org-roam alongside Vulpea rather than instead of it (amends ADR-019)
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-org-roam-with-vulpea
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (org-roam and Vulpea both active after the user's 2026-08-23 restart, no conflict or slowdown reported)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — user restarted
  Emacs and reported everything working with both packages active: no
  conflict, and no complaint about save latency in =~/org= with two autosync
  hooks running. The finer checks under Validation (=SPC n r f=, a note made
  by each tool being findable by the other) were not itemized back, so the
  "which finder do I use?" question in Revisit-when remains open.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-vulpea-note-management][ADR-019]] recorded Vulpea as this config's note-management layer, chosen over
org-roam. While auditing =init.el='s unused module flags ([[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]]), =:lang
org +roam= came up as a flag that ADR-019 appeared to forbid. The user's
answer was explicit: "we want org roam and vulpea", and asked for the docs to
reflect that rather than for the flag to be left off. This ADR records that
reversal and its cost, and amends ADR-019 instead of quietly contradicting
it.

** Decision drivers
- User instruction, given directly and unambiguously after the conflict with
  ADR-019 was raised.
- [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]'s "one tool per responsibility" rule is the thing being bent here,
  so it deserves the same treatment ADR-021 itself gave the completion axis:
  state precisely what the second tool is for, or admit the rule is being
  relaxed.
- Vulpea's existing surface is already load-bearing — the =(n v)= leader
  prefix, hand-rolled dailies, and the backlinks buffer from [[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]], all
  live-verified 2026-08-23. Whatever org-roam adds, it must not be by
  displacing those.

** Candidates
| Decision point | Candidates | Fate |
|-----------------+-------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------|
| org-roam's status | Not installed (ADR-019 status quo) / replace Vulpea / run both | User chose both |
| How they share =~/org= | Partition the tree between them / both index all of it | Both index all of it, by default — neither package is configured to exclude the other's notes. This is the choice with the real consequences (see below) |
| Keybindings | Rebind Doom's =SPC n r= org-roam prefix / leave it stock | Left stock. Vulpea is on =(n v)=, org-roam on =SPC n r=; they do not collide today |
| ADR-019's fate | Rewrite it / supersede it / amend it | Amended — ADR-019's reasoning about *why Vulpea was chosen* remains accurate history, and rewriting it would erase a real decision point |

** Evidence
- Read Vulpea's installed source, which corrected an assumption made earlier
  in the same conversation. =vulpea.el= (v2.7.0) declares
  =Package-Requires: ((emacs "29.1") (org "9.4.4") (emacsql "4.3.0") (s)
  (dash))= — *not* org-roam — and =vulpea-db.el:49-63= builds its own
  =emacsql-sqlite-builtin= database at =vulpea-db-location=. Vulpea v1 was a
  layer on top of org-roam; v2 is standalone. So this decision installs a
  *second, independent* note database over the same files, not a supporting
  library for the one already in use.
- =grep -n vulpea config.el= confirms the live Vulpea surface this must not
  disturb: the =use-package!= block with =vulpea-db-autosync-mode=
  (=config.el:744-747=), the =org-capture= "v" template
  (=config.el:776-777=), the dailies functions (=config.el:785-842=), and
  the backlinks buffer below them.
- =doom sync= installed =org-roam= (present in
  =~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-30.2/org-roam=) with no conflict
  against =vulpea=, which remains installed.
- =doom doctor= flags one org-roam-specific gap: =dot= (graphviz) is absent,
  so org-roam's graph visualization will not render. Its other features do
  not depend on it.
- Not checked, and deliberately not guessed at: whether org-roam's
  autosync and Vulpea's autosync both re-scanning =~/org= on every save
  produces noticeable duplicated work. Both maintain their own SQLite file
  and neither knows about the other.

** Decision
- =init.el=: =:lang (org ... +roam ...)=. Doom's stock org-roam
  configuration and its =SPC n r= bindings are accepted as-is.
- =config.el=: an =(after! org-roam ...)= block establishing Vulpea as the
  preferred tool wherever the two overlap, per the user's explicit
  instruction ("prefer vulpea when there are conflicts"). Three settings,
  one per real overlap:
  1. *Scope* — =org-roam-directory= is left at Doom's =~/org/roam/= default
     rather than pointed at =org-directory=. Vulpea owns the tree; org-roam
     owns a subdirectory of it. This is a decision to *not* write a line of
     config, so it is recorded here and commented there; otherwise a future
     reader would reasonably "fix" it.
  2. *In-buffer completion* — Doom sets =org-roam-completion-everywhere= to
     =t= (=contrib/roam.el:69=), installing a completion-at-point that
     offers roam nodes while typing in any org buffer, competing directly
     with =vulpea-insert=. Set back to =nil=.
  3. *Backlinks* — =+org-roam-auto-backlinks-buffer= is pinned =nil= (also
     its upstream default) so org-roam's right-side backlinks buffer cannot
     start auto-opening over =my/vulpea-buffer-toggle='s, which claims the
     same side.
- org-roam remains fully reachable under =SPC n r=. This ranks the two
  tools; it does not cripple one.
- Vulpea keeps every responsibility it currently holds: the =(n v)= prefix,
  the "v" capture template, dailies, and the backlinks buffer. org-roam is
  additive.
- =docs/decisions.org=: ADR-019 gains a =:LOGBOOK:= entry and an amendment
  note pointing here, so a reader arriving at ADR-019 first is not misled.
- [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]'s rule is explicitly relaxed for the note-management axis, and
  said so here rather than left as an unstated exception.

** Consequences
- Two independent databases now exist, but *not* over the same files, and
  this ADR originally said otherwise — corrected here after reading Doom's
  own org-roam config. =lang/org/contrib/roam.el:62-65= sets
  =org-roam-directory= to =(expand-file-name "roam" org-directory)=, i.e.
  =~/org/roam/=, not =org-directory=. Vulpea indexes the whole tree;
  org-roam indexes one subdirectory of it. The double-scan cost is therefore
  confined to =~/org/roam/=, and everything else in =~/org= is watched once.
- "Which finder do I use?" still has an answer, and it is now a configured
  one rather than a matter of habit: Vulpea. See the precedence rules in the
  Decision section.
- org-roam's own capture templates and =org-roam-directory= default apply;
  a note created through org-roam is ID-bearing and therefore visible to
  Vulpea's database too (both key on Org IDs), but a note created through
  Vulpea's =vulpea-create= is not automatically registered in org-roam's
  =org-roam-node= table unless org-roam's autosync sees the file. Neither
  direction has been exercised yet.
- Doom's =SPC n r= prefix is now live where it previously did nothing —
  worth a line in =docs/ai/keybindings.org= only if the user keeps it.
- org-roam's graph view works: =graphviz= 2.43.0 was installed by the user
  on 2026-08-23, clearing the =dot= warning =doom doctor= raised for it.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
*Live-verified [2026-08-23 Sun]:* the user restarted Emacs with both packages
active and reported no breakage, no conflict, and no save slowdown in =~/org=.
The pre-restart assessment is preserved below.

None yet — this is installed, not exercised. =doom sync= and =doom doctor=
pass (55 modules, 188 packages, one unrelated warning), and =dot= is now
installed so org-roam's graph view has its prerequisite, but no live session
has loaded either package since the change. The decision holds if, in the
user's own session: =SPC n r f= finds notes, a note created via org-roam and
one created via =vulpea-create= are both findable afterward (by whichever
tool the user reaches for), the =(n v)= bindings and backlinks buffer from
[[id:adr-vulpea-dailies-backlinks][ADR-018]] still behave exactly as they did when live-verified earlier the
same day, and saving a file in =~/org= does not feel slower with two
autosync hooks than it did with one.

** Rollback
Remove =+roam= from =:lang org= in =init.el= and =doom sync=. Vulpea is
untouched by this decision, so nothing of ADR-018's or ADR-019's work needs
restoring. org-roam's database file (=org-roam.db= under Doom's local dir)
can be deleted; it is a cache, and =~/org= itself is never modified by
removing the package.

** Revisit when
- The "which finder do I use?" question actually bites — that is the signal
  to give each tool a stated job (e.g. org-roam for graph/link exploration,
  Vulpea for capture and dailies) rather than leaving the overlap implicit.
- Saving in =~/org= becomes slow, in which case disable one autosync before
  investigating anything else.
- Vulpea gains, or org-roam loses, a feature that makes the pairing
  pointless in either direction.
- A future note-management change touches ADR-019's original reasoning, at
  which point both ADRs should be read together rather than separately.

* ADR-028: Retire the =SPC a= AI namespace for =:tools llm='s =SPC o l= map; move the app launcher to =SPC o L= (supersedes ADR-003, amends ADR-020)
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-llm-module-owns-ai-keys
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (user restarted Emacs 2026-08-23; the =SPC o l= / =SPC o L= layout loads with no reported problem)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — restart done,
  user reported everything good, closing the headless-only gap under
  Validation. Whether the =SPC o l= vs =SPC o L= shift distinction proves
  error-prone is a Revisit-when question only accumulated daily use can
  answer, not this restart.
:END:

** Context
Enabling =:tools llm= ([[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]]) brought in Doom's own gptel integration: a
pinned =gptel=, module-level =gptel-default-mode= / =gptel-display-buffer-action=
/ popup rule, three extra packages (=gptel-magit=, =gptel-quick=, =ob-gptel=),
and a full =SPC o l= keymap shipped by =:config default +bindings=. This repo
had independently configured all of the overlapping pieces during Phase 4
([[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]) and [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]. The user asked for the duplication to be removed with the
module, not this repo, taking precedence.

Auditing the overlap turned up a second, unrelated collision that had been
silently live since [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]]: [[id:adr-app-launcher][ADR-020]]'s app launcher occupies the *same*
=SPC o l= prefix the module wants, and wins by load order — so the module's
entire gptel keymap had never existed in this configuration.

** Decision drivers
- User instruction, twice restated: the module takes precedence; this repo
  should carry only what the module does not do.
- [[id:std-doom-first][standards.org]]: Doom-first — prefer a module's own mechanism over a private
  reimplementation of it.
- Two keymaps claiming one prefix is not "harmless aliasing"; =map!='s
  =:prefix= form *replaces* the prefix keymap, so one of them is dead code.
- [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]'s actual goal was a short, discoverable, non-nested AI binding. A
  module-supplied prefix satisfies that goal; it does not have to be =SPC a=.

** Candidates
| Option | Fate |
|--------+------|
| Move launcher to =SPC o L=; module owns =SPC o l=; add this repo's two novel commands into it | *Chosen* — module wins the prefix, launcher keeps a one-keystroke-different home, no keymap is shadowed |
| Keep launcher at =SPC o l=; hand-rebuild the module's nine gptel bindings under =SPC o a= | Rejected — re-creates by hand exactly the map the module ships, i.e. the duplication this ADR exists to delete |
| Keep [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]'s =SPC a= namespace; leave the module's map shadowed | Rejected by the user after it was offered — it preserves a permanently-dead module keymap |
| Delete =SPC a= but leave the two novel commands =M-x=-only | Not offered — fails [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]'s discoverability driver, which this ADR keeps |

** Evidence
- Module source read directly, not assumed:
  =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/tools/llm/{config,packages,autoload}.el=
  — it sets =gptel-default-mode 'org-mode=, sets =gptel-display-buffer-action=
  to nil, registers a =gptel-mode= predicate popup rule, and pins =gptel= to
  =ebf0f3d= with =:recipe (:nonrecursive t)=.
- =packages.el='s bare =(package! gptel)= was a no-op: =package!= merges only
  non-nil keys (=lisp/doom-packages.el=, the =cl-loop for (key value)= form), and
  =git log -1= in =straight/repos/gptel= showed =ebf0f3d= — the module's pin was
  already the installed commit.
- This repo's =(set-popup-rule! "^\*gptel\*" ...)= had never matched anything:
  =gptel= names its buffer =*<backend-name>*= (=gptel.el:1904=), i.e. =*Claude*=
  under [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s Anthropic default. The live popup was therefore the module's
  (bottom, =:size 0.3=, =:quit nil=), not [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s documented right/0.42.
- The launcher/module prefix collision was found by *measuring*, not reading:
  a headless daemon on the working tree returned =nil= for =o l l=, =o l e= and
  =o l g=, and =o l d= resolved to the Discord launcher closure. The agent had
  asserted the opposite ("both live, harmless aliases") one turn earlier; that
  claim was wrong and is retracted here.
- Post-change headless daemon (=emacs --daemon=kbtest2=,
  =lookup-key doom-leader-map=): =o l l/e/s/a/f/r/m/o/O/L= resolve to the module's
  gptel commands, =o l d= → =my/gptel-add-diagnostics=, =o l g= →
  =claude-code-ide-menu=, =o L f/d/v/c/t= → the launcher, =SPC a= → =embark-act=,
  =p t= → =my/project-ghostel=.
- Same daemon, with =gptel= loaded: =backend=Claude=, =model=claude-sonnet-5=,
  =gptel-default-mode=org-mode=, =gptel-display-buffer-action=nil=, directives
  list contains =clearer/formal/summarize= — i.e. [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s behaviour survives
  the deletions.
- =doom sync= and =doom doctor= both clean after the =packages.el= change (the
  standing "tree-sitter rudimentary before Emacs 31" warning only).

** Decision
Delete from this repo everything =:tools llm= already does, and give it the
prefix it ships with:
- =packages.el=: drop =(package! gptel)=; the module declares and pins it.
- =config.el=: drop =(setq gptel-default-mode 'org-mode)= and the dead
  =(set-popup-rule! "^\*gptel\*" ...)=.
- =config.el=: delete the =SPC a= prefix block *and* its =(map! :leader "a" nil)=
  unbind, restoring Doom's default =embark-act= at =SPC a=. This supersedes
  [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]].
- =config.el=: add only the two commands the module's map has no equivalent
  for, into the module's own prefix — =SPC o l d= =my/gptel-add-diagnostics=,
  =SPC o l g= =claude-code-ide-menu=.
- =config.el=: move [[id:adr-app-launcher][ADR-020]]'s launcher from =SPC o l= to =SPC o L=, so it no
  longer replaces the module's keymap. This amends [[id:adr-app-launcher][ADR-020]].
- =my/project-ghostel= loses its =SPC a t= alias and keeps its own =SPC p t=
  binding, which was always the primary one.

What stays in this repo, because the module does not do it: the
=gptel-make-anthropic= / =gptel-make-openai= backends with =+pass-get-secret=
keys, the =clearer=/=formal=/=summarize= =gptel-directives=, and
=my/gptel-add-diagnostics=.

** Consequences
- Every gptel key moves from =SPC a <x>= to =SPC o l <x>=, and the letters
  change (=SPC a i= → =SPC o l l=, =SPC a s= → =SPC o l s=, =SPC a a= →
  =SPC o l a=, =SPC a r= → =SPC o l r=, =SPC a m= → =SPC o l m=, =SPC a d= →
  =SPC o l d=, =SPC a g= → =SPC o l g=). This is real muscle-memory churn and
  the main cost of the decision.
- Four module commands become reachable that previously were not:
  =gptel-quick= (=o l e=), =gptel-add-file= (=o l f=),
  =+llm/open-in-same-window= (=o l L=), =gptel-org-set-topic=/=-set-properties=
  (=o l o=/=o l O=).
- =embark-act= returns to =SPC a= after having been unbound since [[id:adr-ai-leader-namespace][ADR-003]]
  (2026-07-20). Nothing in this config depends on =SPC a= any more.
- The app launcher's keys all shift by one modifier (=SPC o l f= → =SPC o L f=).
- The chat popup is now the module's geometry (bottom, 0.3, =:quit nil=,
  no TTL). If [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s right/0.42/=:quit t= is still wanted, it must be
  re-added as a =gptel-mode= *predicate* rule, not a =^\*gptel\*= regexp.
- =gptel-magit= (=M-g= in a commit buffer, plus a =magit-commit= transient
  suffix) and =ob-gptel= (=#+begin_src gptel= blocks with completion-at-point)
  are now live and unconfigured — new capability, not yet exercised.
- Any future =map!= using =:prefix= under =SPC o= must check the module tree
  first; this class of silent shadowing is invisible in =config.el= alone.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- Headless daemon binding dump (both before and after), quoted under Evidence —
  the "after" run is the validation, the "before" run is what exposed the
  collision.
- =doom sync= + =doom doctor= clean.
- =config.el= re-read by =emacs -Q --batch= without a parse error.
- *Live-verified [2026-08-23 Sun]*: the user restarted their long-running
  Emacs and reported the config good, satisfying
  [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]]'s restart requirement. Individual keys were not
  reported back one at a time, so "the workflow feels right" rests on the
  absence of complaints, not on an itemized pass.

** Rollback
- =git revert= the commit; the change is confined to =config.el=,
  =packages.el=, and docs. =doom sync= afterwards, since =packages.el= is
  touched.
- Partial rollback of just the keymap half: restore the =SPC a= block and
  =(map! :leader "a" nil)=, and change the launcher's =:prefix= back to
  ="o l"= — accepting that the module's gptel map returns to being dead.

** Revisit when
- The =SPC o l= vs =SPC o L= distinction proves error-prone in daily use (the
  two prefixes differ only by shift, and both are "open something") — that is
  the signal to move the launcher somewhere semantically distinct instead.
- The user wants [[id:adr-gptel-rework][ADR-006]]'s right-side 0.42 popup back, which now requires
  overriding the module's predicate rule rather than adding a regexp one.
- =gptel-magit= or =ob-gptel= gets used in anger and needs configuration, at
  which point this repo starts carrying module-adjacent config again and the
  "only what the module doesn't do" boundary needs restating.
- Doom's =:config default +bindings= changes the =SPC o l= map — the two
  bindings added here (=d=, =g=) are currently free upstream but are not
  reserved.

* ADR-029: Non-language module sweep — enable 22 more Doom modules; drop =confirm-kill-emacs nil= so =doom-quit= can fire
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-module-sweep-non-lang
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (user restarted Emacs 2026-08-23 with all 22 modules active and reported no breakage, no binding collision, no startup complaint)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — the restart this
  ADR was explicitly waiting on happened, and the user reported all of it
  good: nothing broken by the sweep, no binding collision, no startup
  complaint. The quit prompt and treemacs were not confirmed one by one, so
  Revisit-when still stands — the first real usage pass, not this restart, is
  the honest filter for modules that turn out to be unused.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] annotated every available flag in =init.el= and enabled the flags
that earned their keep, but deliberately stopped short of turning on modules
that were merely *available*: its scope was "annotate and prune first,
enabling is a separate step". That left roughly forty commented-out modules
whose value the user had never been asked about one at a time. The user asked
to be walked through the non-language ones — brief description, conflicts
with what is already on, and which flags to take — and stated a standing
preference: enable every flag a chosen module offers, even when that means
installing more system programs.

** Decision drivers
- The user's explicit selections. This ADR records a preference sweep, not an
  agent's judgement call about what a config "should" have; every module below
  was individually chosen.
- "Every flag enabled, even if I have to install more programs" — so the
  default is the maximal flag set, and a missing binary is an install task,
  not a reason to drop a flag. Same pattern as [[id:adr-rss-elfeed][ADR-012]] (=mpv=/=yt-dlp=)
  and [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] (=gnuplot=, =graphviz=, =texlab=, =marksman=).
- Existing decisions still constrain the eligible set: [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] (one engine
  per completion axis) rules out =:completion company/ivy/helm/ido=;
  [[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]] keeps =:term shell/term/vterm= off; =:email notmuch/wanderlust=
  would be a second mail client.
- Flags must be read out of the module tree, not out of =init.el='s own
  comments — =.claude/rules/doom-emacs.md=. This sweep found one comment that
  was already stale (see Evidence).
- =std-no-scope-creep=: modules the user did not pick stay commented out, and
  no configuration was written for the newly-enabled ones. They run at their
  Doom defaults until a specific need appears.

** Candidates
| Decision point | Candidates | Fate |
|----------------+------------+------|
| Which modules to offer | All ~40 disabled non-=:lang= modules, flat | Rejected — the multiple-choice box takes four options per question; a flat list would have been unreadable |
| Which modules to offer | *Grouped batches, conflicting modules pre-filtered out* | *Chosen* — eight grouped questions; =company=/=ivy=/=helm=/=ido=, =notmuch=/=wanderlust=, =shell=/=term=/=vterm=, =neotree=, =god=, =parinfer= and the =:input= methods were never offered, each because an existing decision or an enabled module already settles that axis |
| Project drawer | =neotree= | Rejected — same job as treemacs, less capable |
| Project drawer | *=treemacs +lsp=* | *Chosen by the user*, knowing it overlaps =dired +dirvish= (see Consequences) |
| =window-select= flags | =+numbers +switch-window= (both) | Rejected — not a valid combination: =ui/window-select/packages.el= installs =switch-window= *instead of* =ace-window= via an =if=, so the two are mutually exclusive |
| =window-select= flags | *=+numbers= (winum + ace-window)* | *Chosen* — the maximal *compatible* flag set |
| =doom-quit= vs =confirm-kill-emacs nil= | Enable the module, leave =config.el= alone | Rejected — the module would be silently inert (Evidence) |
| =doom-quit= vs =confirm-kill-emacs nil= | *Enable the module, delete =confirm-kill-emacs nil= from =config.el=* | *Chosen* — the user picked =doom-quit= after being told it conflicts, so the conflicting setting is what goes |
| =collab +tunnel= prerequisite | Drop =+tunnel= | Rejected — contradicts the standing "every flag" preference |
| =collab +tunnel= prerequisite | *Install =tuntox=* | *Chosen* — upstream release binary into =~/.local/bin=, same pattern as =texlab=/=marksman= |

** Evidence
- *Flags were re-derived from the module tree*, not from =init.el='s
  annotations: =grep -rhoE '\(modulep! \+[a-z0-9-]+\)'= across each candidate
  module directory, cross-checked against each =README.org= "Module flags"
  section. This found one stale annotation: =init.el= described =:tools
  debugger= as =[+lsp]=, but =tools/debugger/README.org:14-15= says /This
  module has no flags/ and the module now ships =dape=, not =dap-mode=. The
  annotation dated from [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] the same day; it was wrong when written.
- *=doom-quit= was genuinely inert as configured.*
  =ui/doom-quit/config.el:41= is =(setq confirm-kill-emacs #'+doom-quit-fn)=,
  which runs at module-config time; =config.el= then set
  =confirm-kill-emacs nil= afterwards, and =config.el= loads after modules.
  The module would have installed a prompt function that the user's own
  setting immediately overwrote. Removing the =nil= is what makes the chosen
  module do anything at all.
- *=window-select='s flags are exclusive*, from
  =ui/window-select/packages.el=: =(if (modulep! +switch-window) (package!
  switch-window) (package! ace-window))=. "Every flag" is not achievable here
  and =+numbers= alone is the maximum.
- *=:app calendar= is exactly [[id:adr-calendar-org-gcal][ADR-025]]'s module.* =app/calendar/packages.el=
  declares =calfw= (pinned =24fa167a=) with =calfw-org=/=calfw-cal=/=calfw-ical=
  and =org-gcal= (pinned =7304b592=) — the packages that ADR-025 scoped but
  never enabled. Enabling it here delivers ADR-025's package half only; no
  =org-gcal= credentials, calendar ids, or =~/org/calendar/= files exist yet.
- *=doom sync= completed clean*: "All 290 packages are up-to-date",
  "Finished in 2m 12s". =doom doctor= then reported 77 modules / 230 packages,
  up from 55 / 188 at [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]]'s close.
- *=doom doctor= flagged exactly two prerequisite gaps*, both from modules
  enabled here: =x Unmet dependencies: xdotool= (=:app everywhere=) and
  =! Couldn't find tuntox command= (=:tools collab= =+tunnel=). The standing
  "tree-sitter rudimentary before Emacs 31" warning on =markdown +tree-sitter=
  is unrelated and pre-existing.
- *=tuntox= 0.0.10 installed and confirmed runnable*: upstream release binary
  =tuntox-x64= into =~/.local/bin/tuntox=, =sha256
  76beab684624905ee6a0a913489cb2f03a21f37d800d28e7c4001545a09acb8f=,
  =tuntox -h= prints "Tuntox built from git commit 18b0864c…, toxcore version
  0.2.13".

** Decision
Enable these 22 modules in =init.el=, at the flag sets shown:

- =:ui= — =doom-quit=, =(emoji +ascii +github +unicode)=, =(treemacs +lsp)=,
  =unicode=, =(window-select +numbers)=
- =:editor= — =lispy=, =multiple-cursors=, =rotate-text=, =word-wrap=
- =:emacs= — =(ibuffer +icons)=
- =:tools= — =biblio=, =(collab +tunnel)=, =debugger=, =editorconfig=, =make=,
  =tmux=, =upload=
- =:os= — =(tty +osc)=
- =:app= — =calendar=, =emms=, =everywhere=, =irc=

Delete =confirm-kill-emacs nil= from =config.el='s "better default behavior"
=setq= so =:ui doom-quit= can install its prompt. Install the two missing
prerequisite binaries (=tuntox= via upstream release into =~/.local/bin=,
=xdotool= via apt). Write no configuration for any of the new modules — they
run at Doom defaults until a concrete need appears.

Explicitly *not* enabled, having been offered and declined: =:tools docker=,
=:tools ein= (redundant with =:lang org +jupyter= for this user's notebook
use), =:tools terraform=, =:tools ansible=, =:term eshell=, =:ui deft=
(superseded by org-roam/Vulpea, [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]]). These stay commented out with
their flag annotations intact.

** Consequences
- *Quitting Emacs now prompts.* This is a deliberate reversal of a
  long-standing personal setting, and it is the one change here that alters
  daily muscle memory rather than adding capability.
- *=treemacs +lsp= is a second file-tree UI next to =dired +icons +dirvish=.*
  [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]'s "one tool per responsibility" rule is scoped per axis, and the
  honest reading is that these are adjacent but not identical axes — a
  persistent project drawer versus a file *manager* — much like Corfu and
  Vertico. That is a defensible split, not an obviously correct one; if the
  drawer goes unused, it is the first thing to cut.
- *=editorconfig= now wins over =:editor whitespace +guess= wherever a
  =.editorconfig= file exists.* Not a conflict Doom will warn about; it is
  simply a higher-priority source of indent settings. Worth remembering the
  next time indentation in some repo "ignores" the guessed style.
- *=:app calendar= is now installed but unconfigured*, so [[id:adr-calendar-org-gcal][ADR-025]] moves from
  "no packages" to "packages present, sync not set up". Its remaining work
  (OAuth client id/secret in =pass=, =org-gcal-fetch-file-alist=, the
  =~/org/calendar/= directory) is unchanged.
- *=(tty +osc)= only matters under =emacs -nw=*, which this config does not
  otherwise target — it is cheap insurance, not a used path.
- *Nine modules add keybindings this config has never mapped* (treemacs,
  ibuffer, multiple-cursors, window-select, biblio, debugger, emms, irc,
  calendar). Doom's own leader bindings cover most; if any collide with this
  repo's =map!= blocks, the collision will be invisible in =config.el= alone —
  the same class of problem [[id:adr-llm-module-owns-ai-keys][ADR-028]] hit with =SPC o l=. A headless binding
  dump is the way to check.
- *=irc= and =calendar= will want credentials*, which per this repo's rules go
  through =pass= / =+pass-get-secret=, never into =config.el=.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync= clean (2m 12s, 290 packages up to date), quoted under Evidence.
- =doom doctor= after the sweep: 77 modules, 230 packages, and only the two
  prerequisite gaps this ADR then closed, plus the pre-existing markdown
  tree-sitter caveat.
- =tuntox= installed and runnable (=tuntox -h= output quoted above).
- =xdotool= and =xclip= installed by the user in their own terminal (the
  agent's non-interactive shell has no TTY for sudo's password prompt);
  =which= confirms =/usr/bin/xdotool= and =/usr/bin/xclip=.
- =doom doctor= re-run after both installs: *no errors, one warning* — the
  standing "tree-sitter rudimentary before Emacs 31" caveat on =markdown
  +tree-sitter=, which predates this sweep and no action here can clear.
- *Live-verified [2026-08-23 Sun]*: the user restarted Emacs with all 22
  modules active and reported no breakage and no binding collisions, which
  satisfies [[id:tshoot-daemon-validation][tshoot-daemon-validation]]. The quit prompt and treemacs were not
  confirmed individually — absence of complaint, not a checklist.

** Rollback
- Whole sweep: =git revert= the commit, then =doom sync=. The change is
  confined to =init.el=, =config.el= (one deleted line), and docs.
- Single module: re-comment its line in =init.el= and =doom sync=. Nothing in
  =config.el= depends on any of the 22, by design.
- The quit prompt alone: restore =confirm-kill-emacs nil= in =config.el= *and*
  re-comment =doom-quit= — leaving both would recreate the inert-module state
  this ADR diagnosed.
- =tuntox=: =rm ~/.local/bin/tuntox= and drop the =+tunnel= flag.

** Revisit when
- The user restarts Emacs and finds a binding collision, a slow startup, or a
  module that simply is not used — the sweep was chosen from descriptions, so
  the first real usage pass is the honest filter.
- =treemacs= goes unused for a month while dirvish stays the file UI — cut
  treemacs and record it, rather than carrying two drawers. (Partly answered:
  [[id:adr-cosmetic-module-bindings][ADR-031]] found the two drawers were in
  fact colliding on =SPC o p=, and kept both on a per-axis reading of the
  one-tool rule, with =dirvish-side= moved to =SPC o s=. The "goes unused"
  test still stands — it just now applies to =SPC o s=.)
- =:app everywhere= or =:tools direnv= is actually wanted — both were enabled
  here but left inert until [[id:adr-everywhere-direnv][ADR-032]] supplied the
  two pieces that cannot live in this repository: a GNOME hotkey and a
  =~/.bashrc= hook.
- [[id:adr-calendar-org-gcal][ADR-025]]'s sync work starts, at which point =:app calendar= stops being an
  idle module and needs its credential and file-layout half.
- =:tools debugger= is actually used: it ships =dape=, and this repo has no
  =dape= configuration or launch templates for Python (=:lang python +lsp=)
  yet.
- A future =doom upgrade= changes any of these modules' flag sets — the flag
  annotations in =init.el= are dated 2026-08-23 against doom core =ad55ed0=
  and this sweep already found one that had gone stale within a day.

* ADR-030: Torrent client — =transmission.el= against a user-session =transmission-daemon=
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-torrent-transmission
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Note taken on [2026-08-29 Fri] \\
  The two checks the 2026-08-23 entry left open are now closed by observation
  rather than by mechanism, on the first real transfers this daemon has seen.

  *Auto-refresh.* A Debian 13.6.0 =netinst= torrent was added from
  =cdimage.debian.org= and throttled to 400 kB/s (=transmission-remote -t 1 -d
  400=) purely so it would stay visibly downloading for tens of minutes instead
  of finishing in two. The user confirmed the =transmission-mode= list buffer's
  Have/ETA/Down columns ticking while that buffer was current. Upstream's
  poll-only-while-current design (=transmission.el:552-559=) is unchanged and
  still correct; what was missing was a changing torrent to prove it against.

  *Firefox.* Inspection first: the snap profile's =handlers.json=
  (=~/snap/firefox/common/.mozilla/firefox/s5v1taq3.default-release/=) records
  =magnet= as ={"action": 4}= — use-system-default, with no cached handler name
  and no =ask= flag — so there was never a stale qBittorrent association pinned
  inside Firefox to fight. =xdg-mime query default x-scheme-handler/magnet=
  resolves to =emacs-magnet.desktop= via =~/.config/mimeapps.list=. The desktop
  chain was then re-proven directly: =xdg-open= on a =magnet:= URI returned
  =#<process transmission>=, i.e. desktop entry to =~/.local/bin/emacs-magnet=
  to =emacsclient= to =transmission-add= to the daemon, which rejected the
  duplicate. Finally the user clicked a magnet in Firefox itself and a second
  torrent appeared on the daemon — that covers the one hop inspection cannot
  reach, snap Firefox's =xdg-desktop-portal= =OpenURI= call.

  *Unmitigated risk this exposed, recorded rather than fixed.* That second
  torrent was 9.32 GB and took =/= from 95% to 99% (3.4 GB free) before it was
  a quarter done. Nothing in this design guards against it: the daemon has no
  free-space precondition configured, =transmission-add= from a magnet commits
  to the full payload size sight-unseen, and the default seed ratio limit is
  unlimited, so completed torrents are never reclaimed on their own. A full
  =/= breaks far more than the torrent client — Emacs autosaves, =straight=
  builds, and system logs all share the volume. Options if this recurs, none
  taken yet: set =download-queue-enabled= plus a =free-space-limit= in the
  daemon's =settings.json=, point =download-dir= at a volume that is not =/=,
  or set a default =ratio-limit= so seeding terminates. Revisit if a magnet
  click ever actually fills the disk.
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented"  [2026-08-23 Sun] — the restart this
  ADR was waiting on happened. =SPC o B= opens the torrent list (user
  confirmed). The =magnet:= handler was checked two ways: in-Emacs,
  =browse-url-select-handler= on a magnet URI returns the =config.el= closure
  rather than =browse-url-default-browser=; end to end, a real magnet driven
  through the new desktop handler reached the daemon and created a torrent,
  which was then removed. =revert-buffer= in =transmission-mode= completed
  against the daemon, so the RPC path the list buffer depends on is live.
  Honest gap, deliberately not papered over: the auto-refresh was verified by
  mechanism, not by observation. =transmission-refresh-interval= is 2s and
  =transmission-timer-check= is on a buffer-local =post-command-hook= in all
  four refresh modes (=transmission.el:2114,2182,2264,2379=), but
  =transmission-timer-revert= (=transmission.el:552-559=) tests the =major-mode=
  of whatever buffer is current when the timer fires and cancels itself
  otherwise — so it polls only while you are sitting in the buffer, by
  upstream's design. With no torrents on the daemon there was nothing to watch
  tick. First real transfer is the honest filter for that one line.
- Amendment  [2026-08-23 Sun] — the desktop-level =magnet:= handler was added,
  which this ADR originally did not cover. See the amended Consequences and
  Rollback entries below.
:END:

** Context
The user asked to use Emacs as a torrent client. Nothing in this repo touched
torrents before this ADR: =grep -rniE 'torrent|transmission|aria2'= across
=*.el= and =*.org= returned no hits, and no Doom module ships a torrent client,
so this is a new responsibility rather than a change to an existing one.

The machine already had =qbittorrent 4.6.3-1build2= installed (GUI only, no
daemon running), which made "reuse what is already installed" the first
question to answer rather than an afterthought.

** Decision drivers
- The ladder in the repo's laziness rules: reuse an installed dependency before
  installing a new one, and write no glue code that a maintained package
  already provides.
- "One tool per responsibility" ([[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]]) — this is a new axis (torrent
  transfer), not a second client on an existing one, so it does not conflict.
- Downloads must land in the user's own files, owned by the user. A daemon
  running as a separate system account creates a permissions problem on every
  single download.
- No secret values in =config.el= (=.claude/rules/security.md=).
- The RPC socket must not be reachable off-host.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Emacs client | New system package | Fate |
|-----------+--------------+--------------------+------|
| =transmission-daemon= + =transmission.el= | Maintained, on MELPA, =tabulated-list-mode= so =evil-collection= covers the bindings for free | Yes — =transmission-daemon= | *Chosen* |
| Keep =qbittorrent=, drive its WebUI API from Emacs | None found; would mean hand-writing login-cookie handling, =torrents/info=, =torrents/add=, and a list UI | No | Rejected — trades one =apt install= for ~50–100 lines of glue this repo would then own and maintain |
| =aria2= + =aria2.el= | Exists, less polished than =transmission.el= | Yes — =aria2= | Rejected — same install cost as transmission, weaker Emacs side. Its extra HTTP/FTP download handling is not a need anyone stated |
| =rtorrent= + =mentor= | Exists, least actively maintained of the three | Yes — =rtorrent= | Rejected — worst maintenance story, no compensating advantage |

Honest gap: the "no maintained Emacs qBittorrent client exists" row rests on
prior knowledge, not on a live MELPA/GitHub search performed during this work.
If such a package turns up later, the qBittorrent row deserves re-costing,
because it would remove the system-package install entirely.

** Evidence
- =which transmission-daemon aria2c rtorrent deluge= — all empty before the
  install; =dpkg -l= showed only =qbittorrent 4.6.3-1build2=.
- The packaged system unit does *not* work on this machine. After the user's
  =apt install=, =systemctl status transmission-daemon= reported:
  #+begin_example
  Active: failed (Result: timeout) since Sun 2026-08-23 20:07:25 MDT
  transmission-daemon.service: start operation timed out. Terminating.
  #+end_example
  The unit is =Type=notify= and the daemon never sent readiness, so systemd
  killed a process that was otherwise healthy.
- The same binary run in the foreground as the user works immediately:
  =transmission-remote -l= returned the (empty) torrent table on first try.
- Generated user config at =~/.config/transmission-daemon/settings.json=:
  =rpc-port 9091=, =rpc-authentication-required false=, =rpc-whitelist
  "127.0.0.1,::1"=, =download-dir "/home/james/Downloads"= — but
  =rpc-bind-address "0.0.0.0"=, which this ADR changed to =127.0.0.1=.
- After installing the user unit: =systemctl --user is-active= reports
  =active=, and =ss -ltnp= shows =LISTEN 127.0.0.1:9091= owned by
  =transmission-da=, i.e. bound to loopback rather than every interface.
- Keybinding survey of the live =doom-leader-open-map= (via =emacsclient= and
  =which-key--get-keymap-bindings=) shows =t=Toggle ghostel popup= and
  =T=Open ghostel here=, so the mnemonic =SPC o t= was unavailable and
  =SPC o B= (BitTorrent) was free.
- =doom sync= clean in 9.5s; =transmission= built to
  =~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-30.2/transmission/=.
- =doom doctor=: 77 modules, 231 packages, *one warning* — the standing
  "tree-sitter rudimentary before Emacs 31" caveat on =markdown=, which
  predates this work (see [[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]]).
- RPC round trip from Emacs, not just from the shell: loading
  =transmission.el= and calling =(transmission-request "session-get")=
  returned the daemon's session alist, including =(config-dir
  . "/home/james/.config/transmission-daemon")= and =(download-dir
  . "/home/james/Downloads")= — confirming Emacs talks to the *user* daemon.

** Decision
- =packages.el=: =(package! transmission)=.
- Run the daemon as a *systemd user unit* at
  =~/.config/systemd/user/transmission-daemon.service=, =Type=simple=,
  =ExecStart=/usr/bin/transmission-daemon -f --log-level=error=,
  =Restart=on-failure=. This sidesteps the =Type=notify= timeout *and* the
  =debian-transmission= file-ownership problem in one move.
- Harden =~/.config/transmission-daemon/settings.json= by binding RPC to
  =127.0.0.1= instead of =0.0.0.0=. The whitelist already restricted callers;
  the bind address makes it structural rather than policy.
- =config.el=: bind =SPC o B= to =transmission=, set
  =transmission-refresh-modes= inside =after!= so the list/file/peer buffers
  poll while visible, and register a =browse-url-handlers= entry so any
  =magnet:= link resolves to =transmission-add= instead of the default
  browser.
- No credential lookup in =config.el=, because RPC authentication is off on a
  loopback-only socket. If authentication is ever enabled, the password comes
  from auth-source via =:tools pass=, never a literal.

** Consequences
- The *system* =transmission-daemon.service= is masked as of 2026-08-23
  (=sudo systemctl mask transmission-daemon=, run by the user —
  =/etc/systemd/system/transmission-daemon.service -> /dev/null=,
  =systemctl is-enabled= reports =masked=). It can no longer start or fail at
  boot. It was harmless before masking too — it never bound the port, so it
  could not conflict with the user unit — but a permanently red unit in
  =systemctl --failed= trains you to ignore that list.
  Caveat: masking does *not* clear the already-recorded failure. The unit
  still shows in =systemctl --failed= until =sudo systemctl reset-failed
  transmission-daemon= is run, or until the next reboot.
- Lingering is enabled for the user (=loginctl enable-linger james=,
  2026-08-23 — =Linger=yes=, =/var/lib/systemd/linger/james= exists), so the
  user unit and its seeding survive logout rather than dying with the login
  session. Notably this did *not* need =sudo=: polkit permits enabling linger
  for one's own account.
- =magnet:= links now bypass the browser Emacs-wide, including inside eww and
  elfeed. That is the point, but it means a magnet click no longer offers a
  GUI client choice.
- Amended 2026-08-23: the =browse-url-handlers= entry only governs links that
  originate *inside* Emacs. The desktop still routed =x-scheme-handler/magnet=
  to =org.qbittorrent.qBittorrent.desktop=, so a magnet clicked in Firefox went
  to the client this ADR chose against. Two files outside this repo now close
  that gap, and being outside =$DOOMDIR= they are not under git — this entry is
  their only record:
  - =~/.local/bin/emacs-magnet= — escapes the URI for an Emacs Lisp string
    literal, then calls =emacsclient -a '' -e '(transmission-add "…")'=. The
    =-a ''= starts a daemon when no Emacs is running so a magnet is not
    silently dropped. The URI arrives from a web page and is therefore
    untrusted: it is never interpolated into a shell command, and the only
    escaping needed inside an elisp string literal is =\= and ="=, both of
    which the wrapper handles. Verified with a URI carrying an embedded
    =(shell-command "touch /tmp/pwned")= payload — it arrived at
    =transmission-add= as one 70-character string and the file was never
    created.
  - =~/.local/share/applications/emacs-magnet.desktop= — =NoDisplay=true=,
    following the existing =claude-code-url-handler.desktop= pattern. Both
    =xdg-mime query default= and =gio mime= resolve to it; =qbittorrent= stays
    registered as an alternative, just not the default.
  Firefox caches scheme handlers per profile, so it may still offer its own
  qBittorrent dialog until that is cleared in Settings → General →
  Applications.
- =qbittorrent= stays installed and untouched. Two torrent clients now exist on
  the machine, but only one is wired into Emacs, so the "one tool per
  responsibility" rule is satisfied at the level this repo governs.
- Deliberately skipped: the elfeed-to-torrent command sketched while planning
  (add the current entry's enclosure as a torrent). Nobody has an actual feed
  that needs it yet; the =browse-url= handler already covers magnets in elfeed,
  and the dedicated command is a few lines whenever a real feed calls for it.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- =doom sync= clean, =doom doctor= clean apart from the pre-existing markdown
  tree-sitter warning (both quoted under Evidence).
- Daemon reachable and loopback-bound: =systemctl --user is-active= =active=,
  =ss -ltnp= shows =127.0.0.1:9091=.
- Emacs-side RPC confirmed by an actual =session-get= call returning the user
  daemon's config, not by assuming the defaults line up.
- Live-verified 2026-08-23 after the restart: =SPC o B=, both magnet paths, and
  the list buffer's RPC round trip. The auto-refresh is verified by mechanism
  only, never watched against a live transfer. See the =:LOGBOOK:= entry for
  what each check actually proved and where the gap is.

** Rollback
- Emacs side: =git revert= the commit and =doom sync=. The change is confined to
  =packages.el=, =config.el=, and docs.
- Daemon: =systemctl --user disable --now transmission-daemon= and
  =rm ~/.config/systemd/user/transmission-daemon.service=.
- System package: =sudo apt remove transmission-daemon=. Torrent state and
  settings survive in =~/.config/transmission-daemon/= unless separately
  removed.
- Desktop =magnet:= handler (added 2026-08-23, outside git):
  =xdg-mime default org.qbittorrent.qBittorrent.desktop x-scheme-handler/magnet=,
  then =rm ~/.local/share/applications/emacs-magnet.desktop ~/.local/bin/emacs-magnet=
  and =update-desktop-database ~/.local/share/applications=.

** Revisit when
- Emacs is restarted and =SPC o B= is actually used — the first real session is
  what promotes this ADR's status, or surfaces a binding/handler problem.
- A maintained Emacs qBittorrent client appears, which would let the
  system-package install be dropped entirely (see the honest gap noted under
  Candidates).
- Remote or seedbox access is wanted: that turns on RPC authentication, which
  pulls =:tools pass= and a credential entry into this decision for the first
  time.
- An Ubuntu update fixes the packaged unit's =Type=notify= behaviour, which
  would make the user unit redundant — though the file-ownership argument for
  running as the user stands on its own regardless, so this is a reason to
  re-read the decision, not to reverse it automatically.
- Lingering starts costing something: with =Linger=yes= the daemon now runs
  even when nobody is logged in, so a machine that should be idle isn't.

* ADR-031: Cosmetic =:ui= module keybinding audit — resolve the =SPC o p= sidebar collision by axis rather than by dropping a module
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-cosmetic-module-bindings
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented" [2026-08-23 Sat] — user reloaded and
  exercised all three bindings in their own session. =SPC s h= was confirmed
  first and separately: it returned four =hl-todo-occur= hits in =config.el=,
  all of them the literal word "TODO" in the ADR-017 comment block, the
  =org-todo-keywords= sequence, and the ="* TODO %?\n%U"= capture template —
  no real task markers, and a useful demonstration that =hl-todo= matches text
  without syntactic awareness. =SPC o h= and =SPC o s= confirmed working in the
  same pass.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-ui-rice-pass][ADR-022]] and [[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]]
between them turned on most of Doom's =:ui= modules, and
[[id:adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard][ADR-024]] gave =:ui tabs= its bindings, but no
pass had ever asked the flat question: of every cosmetic module now enabled,
which ones expose a command that has no key at all?

Auditing that surfaced two separate problems.

1. Two modules — =:ui dashboard= and =:ui hl-todo= — each ship a command
   reachable only from =M-x=. The dashboard is =doom-fallback-buffer= but has
   no "go back to it" key, and =hl-todo= binds point-to-point motion
   (=]t=/=[t=) without binding its list view.
2. =:ui treemacs= and =:emacs dired +dirvish= *both* claim =SPC o p= /
   =SPC o P= with the description "Project sidebar". Doom declares them in
   the same =:prefix-map ("o" . "open")= block, dirvish first and treemacs
   second, so treemacs silently wins and =dirvish-side= is reachable only
   from =M-x=. This is a live shadow, not a hypothetical: nothing warns, and
   reading Doom's binding file suggests dirvish is bound when it isn't.

Problem 2 is the substantive one, because it is a genuine
=AGENTS.md= one-tool-per-responsibility question — two project sidebars — and
not merely an unbound key.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md= one tool per responsibility, as scoped per-axis by
  [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] (Corfu and Vertico coexist because
  in-buffer completion and minibuffer completion are different axes) and
  extended again by [[id:adr-token-reduction-stack][ADR-023]]. The rule is
  already established as axis-scoped in this repo, so the question is whether
  a real axis separates treemacs from dirvish-side — not whether two sidebars
  are automatically a violation.
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first and Evil-first: bindings go through =map!= under the
  leader prefix whose category they belong to.
- =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-creep=: a binding audit, not an occasion
  to re-litigate which file manager this config uses.
- Don't remove a feature to settle a keybinding argument. Dropping either
  module costs real functionality (see Candidates); a free key costs nothing.
- Prefer no diff to a diff. Options that required zero config change were
  weighed first.

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Sidebar collision | Leave the shadow, document it only | Rejected — zero diff, but =dirvish-side= stays =M-x=-only and the next reader of Doom's =+evil-bindings.el= still sees a binding that doesn't exist |
| Sidebar collision | *Rebind =dirvish-side= to a free key, keep both modules* | *Chosen* — one line, no module churn, and the axis split is defensible under ADR-021's existing precedent |
| Sidebar collision | Drop =:ui treemacs=; =dirvish-side= inherits =SPC o p= | Rejected — costs the =+lsp= trees gated on =(modulep! :ui treemacs +lsp)=: =SPC c S= (symbols), =SPC c X= (errors list), =SPC c y=/=c Y= (call hierarchy), =SPC c R= (references tree) |
| Sidebar collision | Drop the =+dirvish= flag from =:emacs dired= | Rejected — costs previews, multi-pane, and =SPC o /=; plain =dired= would remain but the rice pass adopted dirvish deliberately |
| Sidebar collision | Make =SPC o p= dispatch (treemacs in a code project, dirvish-side otherwise) | Rejected — a conditional nobody will remember, and both modules stay installed anyway, so nothing is actually simplified |
| Key for =dirvish-side= | =SPC o s= | *Chosen* (user's pick) — confirmed unbound; =s= for "sidebar" |
| Key for =dirvish-side= | =SPC o w=, =o z=, =o n=, =o S=, =o k= | Not chosen — all equally free, no reason to prefer them |
| Key for the dashboard | =SPC o h= ("home") | *Chosen* — confirmed unbound; the dashboard is =doom-fallback-buffer=, so "home" describes its role |
| Key for the dashboard | =SPC o d= | Rejected — already =+debugger/start= |
| Key for =hl-todo-occur= | =SPC s h= | *Chosen* — confirmed unbound. Filed under *search*, not *toggle*: it answers "where are the TODOs", not "show/hide them" |
| Key for =hl-todo-occur= | =SPC t h= | Rejected — =SPC t= is the toggle map; occur toggles nothing |
| =+dired/dirvish-side-and-follow= | Give it =SPC o S=, pairing with =o s= as Doom pairs =p=/=P= | Not done — out of the scope the user asked for; one line whenever it's wanted |

** Evidence
Every claim below was read out of the *running* Emacs daemon via
=emacsclient -e=, not inferred from Doom's source:

- =(lookup-key doom-leader-map (kbd "o p"))= returns =+treemacs/toggle=, and
  =("o P")= returns =treemacs-find-file= — confirming treemacs wins the
  collision in practice. =("o /")= returns =dirvish=, so full dirvish was
  never shadowed; only the sidebar variant was.
- Doom's =modules/config/default/+evil-bindings.el= declares the dirvish
  sidebar at lines 690–693 and the treemacs sidebar at lines 697–699, inside
  one =:prefix-map ("o" . "open")=. Later declaration wins, which matches the
  observed behavior. (Doom's tree lives at
  =~/.config/emacs/sources/doom+/modules/= in this install, not the
  =modules/= directory at the Doom root.)
- Already-bound cosmetic commands, all confirmed present rather than assumed:
  =t z= → =+zen/toggle=, =t Z= → =+zen/toggle-fullscreen=, =t m= →
  =demap-toggle=, =t i= → =indent-bars-mode=, =t d= → =diff-hl-mode=,
  =g ]=/=g [= → =+vc-gutter/next-hunk=/=previous-hunk=, =i e= →
  =emojify-insert-emoji=, =i u= → =insert-char=, =b t= →
  =centaur-tabs--create-new-tab= (from [[id:adr-tabs-new-tab-dashboard][ADR-024]]).
- =(lookup-key evil-motion-state-map (kbd "]t"))= → =hl-todo-next= and
  =("[t")= → =hl-todo-previous=, so hl-todo motion was *already* covered and
  only its occur view was missing. This corrected an initial assumption that
  hl-todo had no bindings at all.
- =o h=, =s h=, and =o s= all returned =nil= from =doom-leader-map= before
  binding — no shadowing introduced.
- =(commandp 'hl-todo-occur)=, =(commandp 'dirvish-side)=, and
  =(commandp '+dashboard/open)= all return =t=. =+dashboard/open= takes a
  =FRAME= argument but declares =(interactive (list (selected-frame)))=, so
  it is bindable directly with no wrapper.
- After evaluating the new =map!= forms in the daemon, =o h= →
  =+dashboard/open=, =s h= → =hl-todo-occur=, =o s= → =dirvish-side=, with
  =o p= → =+treemacs/toggle= and =o /= → =dirvish= unchanged.

Not evidence: the user has *not* yet reloaded and used any of the three
bindings in their own interactive session. The verification above proves the
forms bind what they claim, in a live daemon, and nothing more.

** Decision
Keep both =:ui treemacs= and =:emacs dired +dirvish=. Treat them as occupying
different axes of the same nominal responsibility — treemacs is *structural
browsing* (a persistent multi-root project tree, and the anchor for the =+lsp=
symbol, error, and call-hierarchy trees); dirvish-side is *file manipulation*
in a narrow window (rename, chmod, wdired, TRAMP). This is the same per-axis
reading of the one-tool rule that [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] applied
to Corfu and Vertico, made explicit here rather than left as a silent shadow.

Three bindings added to =config.el=, each with a comment explaining the
prefix choice:

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(map! :leader
      :desc "Dashboard (home)" "o h" #'+dashboard/open
      :desc "TODOs in buffer"  "s h" #'hl-todo-occur)

(map! :leader :desc "Project sidebar (dirvish)" "o s" #'dirvish-side)
#+end_src

=SPC o p= stays treemacs. No module is added, removed, or reflagged, so no
=doom sync= is required and =init.el=/=packages.el= are untouched.

** Consequences
- =SPC o p= and =SPC o s= now mean two different sidebars. That is a real
  thing to remember, and it is the price of keeping both modules.
- The remaining cosmetic modules — =ligatures=, =smooth-scroll=, =ophints=,
  =modeline=, =unicode=, =vi-tilde-fringe=, =doom-quit=, =doom= — expose no
  command worth a key. They are configuration, not commands. This is recorded
  so a future audit doesn't re-derive it.
- =+dired/dirvish-side-and-follow= remains =M-x=-only. Deliberate, per the
  Candidates table.
- The one-tool rule now has a third per-axis carve-out (ADR-021 completion,
  ADR-023 token reduction, this one for project sidebars). If a fourth
  appears, the rule as written in =AGENTS.md= is probably the thing that
  needs rewording, not each individual case.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- Live daemon =lookup-key= before and after, quoted under Evidence — the three
  new keys were unbound beforehand and resolve to the intended commands
  afterward.
- =commandp= confirmed for all three commands, so none of the bindings can
  fail at press time with a non-interactive-function error.
- No =doom sync= or =doom doctor= run: this change touches =config.el= only,
  which per =AGENTS.md= triggers neither.
- Live-verified 2026-08-23 in the user's own session after a reload: all three
  bindings pressed and working. See the =:LOGBOOK:= drawer for what =SPC s h=
  actually returned.
- Still unproven by use rather than by press: whether =SPC o s= earns its place
  over time. See Revisit when.

** Rollback
Delete the two =map!= forms and their comment blocks from =config.el= and
reload. Doom's own bindings are untouched, so removal restores the exact
prior state — including the =SPC o p= shadow, which returns as it was.

** Revisit when
- =SPC o s= turns out to go unused for a month or two, which would mean the
  axis argument was rationalization and option 4 (drop the =+dirvish= flag)
  was right after all.
- A future Doom release reorders =+evil-bindings.el= so dirvish wins =SPC o p=
  instead — the =SPC o s= binding would still work, but =SPC o p= would
  silently change meaning.
- =+dired/dirvish-side-and-follow= is wanted, at which point =SPC o S= is the
  obvious home for it.
- A fourth per-axis carve-out to the one-tool rule appears, which is the
  signal to reword the rule in =AGENTS.md= rather than add another exception.

* ADR-032: Activate =:app everywhere= and =:tools direnv= — a GNOME hotkey and a bash hook, no Emacs config
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-everywhere-direnv
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (one bug found and fixed on first use)
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Live-verified" from "Implemented" [2026-08-23 Sat] — user pressed
  =Ctrl+Alt+Space= over a real application and reported it "mostly works". The
  one defect: the buffer opened carrying a =#+title:= line from Doom's org file
  template, which =C-c C-c= would have pasted back into the source app. Root
  cause traced and fixed the same day with a two-line =config.el= addition; see
  [[id:tshoot-everywhere-file-template][troubleshooting.org]] for the full
  three-way interaction and the reproduction. Re-pressed after the fix and
  confirmed clean by the user the same day.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]] enabled =:app everywhere= and
=:tools direnv= as part of the 22-module sweep, but that ADR scoped itself to
*turning modules on* and explicitly deferred whether each one was actually
usable. Both were left dormant in different ways:

- =:tools direnv= was already working inside Emacs and nobody had checked. Its
  =envrc-global-mode= runs on =doom-first-file=, the =direnv= binary predates
  this project, and the module even carries a =:before-while= advice that
  aborts gracefully when the binary is missing. What was missing was the
  *shell* half: =~/.bashrc= had no =direnv hook bash= line, so =.envrc= files
  were honoured in Emacs and ignored in every terminal.
- =:app everywhere= was unusable by construction. Its whole design is to be
  invoked from *outside* Emacs — you focus some other application's text
  field, press a global hotkey, and a small Emacs frame appears over it. The
  package's entry point is autoloaded specifically so a desktop hotkey can
  call =emacsclient --eval "(emacs-everywhere)"=. With no such hotkey
  registered, the module was installed and unreachable.

Neither gap is fixable from inside this repository, which is the interesting
part of this decision.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first. Both modules ship complete configuration —
  =tools/direnv/config.el= carries four hacks and advices (early
  =change-major-mode-after-body-hook= ordering, LSP/eglot restart on env
  change, internal-buffer debounce, graceful abort without the binary), and
  =app/everywhere/config.el= handles the modeline, yasnippet, and Hyprland /
  Niri support. Writing =config.el= equivalents of any of it would be
  reimplementation.
- =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-creep=: activate what ADR-029 enabled,
  don't redesign the shell environment or the desktop keymap.
- [[id:adr-torrent-transmission][ADR-030]]'s precedent for state that lives
  outside git: the =magnet:= desktop handler was recorded in the ADR with
  explicit rollback commands rather than pretended into the repository. The
  GNOME hotkey and the =~/.bashrc= line get the same treatment.
- =std-binaries=: don't claim a binary is present without checking, and record
  what the project newly depends on in [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]].
- The hotkey is muscle memory the user will own for years, so it was their
  choice, not a default picked for them.

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Emacs configuration | Add =use-package!=/=setq= blocks for either module in =config.el= | Rejected — both Doom modules are already complete; there is no gap to fill. Zero lines of =config.el= were written for this ADR |
| direnv shell half | Leave =~/.bashrc= alone, Emacs-only direnv | Rejected by the user — =.envrc= would keep working in Emacs and silently doing nothing in any terminal, which is a worse trap than not having direnv at all |
| direnv shell half | *=eval "$(direnv hook bash)"= appended to =~/.bashrc=* | *Chosen* — direnv's own documented install step; the hook is a =PROMPT_COMMAND= shim, not a persistent process |
| direnv shell half | A =.envrc= committed to this repo | Not done — nothing in this config needs a project-local environment yet. Revisit if the Python/LSP work grows one |
| everywhere invocation | Bind =emacs-everywhere= to an Emacs key | Rejected — meaningless. The point is to invoke it while focus is in *another* application; an Emacs binding can never fire at that moment |
| everywhere invocation | *GNOME custom keybinding running =emacsclient --eval "(emacs-everywhere)"=* | *Chosen* — the invocation the package documents, and the mechanism GNOME already uses for the user's existing =custom0= ("Start emacs") |
| everywhere hotkey | *=Ctrl+Alt+Space=* | *Chosen* (user's pick) — emacs-everywhere's own documented default, and confirmed unclaimed by scanning =org.gnome.desktop.wm.keybindings= and =org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.media-keys= |
| everywhere hotkey | =Ctrl+Shift+Alt+Super+Space=, matching =custom0='s four-modifier style | Rejected by the user in favour of the shorter chord |
| everywhere hotkey | =Super+E= | Rejected — free, but =Super= chords collide with GNOME's overview conventions more readily as extensions get added |

** Evidence
Everything below was read from the running system or the running Emacs daemon,
not inferred:

- Session is X11, not Wayland: =XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11=, =loginctl ... Type=x11=,
  =XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=ubuntu:GNOME=, Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS. This matters — on
  Wayland =emacs-everywhere= would have needed =ydotool= / =wtype= / =dotool=
  plus =wl-clipboard=, all four of which are *absent* on this machine. Nothing
  had to be installed only because the session happens to be X11.
- Required binaries all already present: =/usr/bin/direnv= =2.32.1=,
  =/usr/bin/xdotool= =3.20160805.1=, =/usr/bin/xclip= =0.13-3=, =/usr/bin/xprop=.
  No =apt install=, and so no sudo blocker of the kind Phase 2 and Phase 3 hit.
- Emacs side of direnv was *already* live before any change:
  =(featurep 'envrc)= → =t=, =envrc-global-mode= → =t=,
  =(executable-find "direnv")= → =/usr/bin/direnv=.
- direnv end-to-end, both halves, with a real =.envrc= in a scratch directory
  exporting =PONYTAIL_TEST=works=:
  - Emacs: =(envrc--find-env-dir)= resolved to the test directory and
    =(getenv "PONYTAIL_TEST")= returned ="works"= inside that buffer.
  - Shell: =_direnv_hook= exists as a function in a fresh interactive bash, and
    running it after a =cd= printed =direnv: export +PONYTAIL_TEST= with the
    variable set. The scratch =.envrc= was then =direnv deny='d and deleted.
- emacs-everywhere resolves its X11 configuration correctly *in the daemon*,
  which is where the hotkey's =--eval= actually runs:
  =(emacs-everywhere--system-compositor)= → =(x11 . ubuntu:GNOME)=,
  =DISPLAY= =":1"=, and after =(emacs-everywhere--configure-system)= the
