  private vars hold =("xdotool" "key" "--clearmodifiers" "Shift+Insert")=,
  =("xclip" "-selection" "clipboard" "%f")=,
  =("xdotool" "windowactivate" "--sync" "%w")=, and
  =emacs-everywhere--app-info-linux-x11=. The obsolete-since-0.3.0 variables
  (=emacs-everywhere-paste-command= and friends) are all =nil= — they are
  vestigial, not misconfigured.
- Emacs here is a *snap* install (=/snap/emacs/3690/usr/bin/emacs=), which
  raised a real question about whether it can execute host binaries at all.
  It can: =envrc= ran =/usr/bin/direnv= as a subprocess and got its output
  back, and =emacs-everywhere--cmd-executable-p= resolved both =/usr/bin/xdotool=
  and =/usr/bin/xclip=. Confinement is not blocking this.
- GNOME keybinding registered and read back:
  =custom-keybindings= now lists =custom0/= and =custom1/=; =custom1= has
  =name 'Emacs Everywhere'=, =command 'emacsclient --eval "(emacs-everywhere)"'=,
  =binding '<Primary><Alt>space'=. The pre-existing =custom0= is untouched
  (=name 'Start emacs'=, =command 'emacs'=,
  =binding '<Primary><Shift><Alt><Super>e'=).

Not evidence: nobody has pressed =Ctrl+Alt+Space= over a real application yet.
Every check above proves the pieces resolve; none proves the round trip
(capture text → edit in Emacs → =C-c C-c= → paste back into the source app).

** Decision
Write no Emacs configuration for either module — both Doom modules are already
complete, and this ADR is the record of that being a deliberate finding rather
than an omission. (Amended on first use: two lines of =config.el= *were*
needed after all, but for a Doom-module *interaction*, not for either module's
own configuration. See Consequences.)

Activate them through the two mechanisms that live outside the repository:

1. =~/.bashrc= gains =eval "$(direnv hook bash)"=, with a comment pointing back
   at this ADR and noting that Emacs is covered separately by envrc.
2. GNOME =custom1= keybinding: =Ctrl+Alt+Space= →
   =emacsclient --eval "(emacs-everywhere)"=.

Record =xclip= in [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]] (it was missing from the
table) and annotate the existing =direnv= and =xdotool= rows with what now
depends on them.

** Consequences
- Two pieces of this configuration now live outside git — =~/.bashrc= and the
  dconf keybinding — joining ADR-030's =magnet:= handler. A fresh machine
  restored from this repository alone will have both modules installed and
  both effectively inert until these two steps are redone. That is a real
  reproducibility gap, and it is the second one; the "Reproducible machine
  provisioning" future phase in [[id:roadmap-future][roadmap.org]] now has two
  concrete items waiting for it rather than one.
- =Ctrl+Alt+Space= depends on the Emacs server being up. Emacs here is a snap
  GUI process with no systemd unit and no =--daemon= flag, so if Emacs is
  closed the hotkey fails *silently* — GNOME reports nothing when a custom
  keybinding's command exits non-zero. The existing =custom0= ("Start emacs")
  is the manual recovery, which is a slightly awkward pairing: one hotkey
  starts Emacs, the other needs it already running.
- direnv now affects every new interactive bash shell, including the ones this
  and other agents run. A repository with a hostile =.envrc= could therefore
  influence an agent's shell environment — but only after someone runs
  =direnv allow= on it, which is exactly the consent gate direnv exists to
  provide. Worth knowing, not worth blocking on.
- The X11 dependency is now load-bearing. If this machine ever moves to a
  Wayland session, =emacs-everywhere= breaks and needs =ydotool= or =wtype=
  plus =wl-clipboard= installed before it works again.
- The "no Emacs configuration" finding survived contact with reality only
  barely. First real use surfaced a collision between =:app everywhere= and
  =:editor file-templates=: the temp file is extensionless, so it visits in
  =fundamental-mode=; =emacs-everywhere-apply-major-mode= then switches it to
  =org-mode= (pandoc is installed, so that is the default); and Doom checks
  templates on =doom-switch-buffer-hook= rather than =find-file-hook=, so the
  next buffer switch expanded org-mode's =#+title:= template into a buffer
  whose entire contents get pasted back into the source app. Fixed with
  =(setq-hook! 'emacs-everywhere-init-hooks +file-templates-inhibit t)= inside
  an =after!=, mirroring the =doom-inhibit-local-var-hooks= line the module
  itself carries. Full trace in
  [[id:tshoot-everywhere-file-template][troubleshooting.org]].
- That bug generalises: any package opening an extensionless temp file and
  setting its major mode afterwards can trip Doom's file templates.
  =org-capture= is special-cased inside =+file-templates-check-h=; nothing else
  is. Worth checking if a future module does the same thing.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- direnv verified in both halves against a real =.envrc=, quoted under
  Evidence, then cleaned up (=direnv deny= plus deletion of the scratch dir).
- emacs-everywhere verified as far as it can be without a keypress: compositor
  detection, all three resolved commands, the info function, and the snap
  process's ability to execute the host binaries.
- GNOME keybinding read back from =gsettings= after writing.
- No =doom sync= or =doom doctor=: no =init.el= or =packages.el= change, and
  in fact no =config.el= change either.
- Round trip exercised by the user 2026-08-23: the frame opened over a real
  application and the flow worked. The =#+title:= defect it surfaced was
  reproduced non-interactively in the daemon (visit the nonexistent temp path,
  =(org-mode)=, =(+file-templates-check-h)= → buffer contains
  =#+title: Emacs Everywhere 20260823 141530 Firefox=), then re-run after the
  fix → buffer empty, =+file-templates-inhibit= =t= and buffer-local.
- Re-pressed after the fix, 2026-08-23: clean round trip, no stray =#+title:=
  line. Nothing outstanding.

** Rollback
- Shell hook: delete the =eval "$(direnv hook bash)"= line and its comment from
  =~/.bashrc=. Existing shells keep the hook until they exit.
- GNOME hotkey:
  =gsettings set org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.media-keys custom-keybindings "['/org/gnome/settings-daemon/plugins/media-keys/custom-keybindings/custom0/']"=,
  then =dconf reset -f /org/gnome/settings-daemon/plugins/media-keys/custom-keybindings/custom1/=.
- Modules themselves: re-comment =everywhere= in =init.el='s =:app= block and
  =direnv= in =:tools=, then =doom sync= — this reverses part of
  [[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]], so amend that ADR rather than
  silently diverging from it.
- Docs: =git revert= the commit carrying this ADR and the
  [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]] rows.

** Revisit when
- A different application turns out to need markdown rather than org — the
  major mode is chosen globally by =emacs-everywhere-major-mode-function=, and
  pandoc's presence pins it to org for every app.
- =Ctrl+Alt+Space= turns out to collide with something after all. GNOME
  silently lets a later binding win, so the symptom would be the hotkey
  quietly doing the wrong thing rather than an error.
- Emacs is closed and the hotkey is pressed — the silent-failure mode above. If
  that becomes annoying, the fix is a proper =emacs.service= user unit
  (=--fg-daemon=), which is the same shape as ADR-030's
  =transmission-daemon.service= and would supersede the =custom0= launcher too.
- This machine moves to a Wayland session, at which point =emacs-everywhere=
  needs =ydotool= / =wtype= and =wl-clipboard= before it works again.
- The "Reproducible machine provisioning" future phase starts — it now has two
  out-of-git items to absorb (this hotkey plus =~/.bashrc=, and ADR-030's
  =magnet:= handler).
- A project in this config grows a real =.envrc=, which would move direnv from
  "available" to "load-bearing" and is worth noting in
  [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]] when it happens.

* ADR-033: Terminal-layer pass — ghostel configuration, the bash environment beneath it, and a zenburn Claude Code theme
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-terminal-layer-pass
:STATUS:   Implemented and fully live-verified — Claude Code theme 2026-08-24, Emacs half and shell half (rebuilt from scratch after a second regression) 2026-08-27
:DATE:     2026-08-23
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- Claude Code theme regressed, then re-applied [2026-08-27 Thu] — both
  out-of-git files from the original pass were gone: =~/.claude/themes/=
  didn't exist (so =zenburn.json= with it), and =~/.claude/settings.json=
  =theme= had reverted to ="dark"=. No record of *when* or *why*; nothing in
  this repo tracks that directory, so there is no diff to inspect. Found while
  investigating a "theme not loading" report. Also found along the way,
  unrelated: Emacs had moved 30.2 -> 31.1, which moved straight's build dir
  (=build-30.1= -> =build-31.1=) and briefly made =doom-zenburn-theme.el= look
  missing too — same file, same hexes, confirmed byte-identical, just at a new
  path. Fix: regenerated =zenburn.json= (71 overrides, =background= left
  unset) against the base ="dark"= theme's key schema, re-extracted live from
  the current CLI binary (2.1.247, up from the original pass's 2.1.241) rather
  than assumed — the key set is unchanged, still 72 keys total. Re-set
  =settings.json= =theme= to ="custom:zenburn"=. *Caveat*: the hex-per-key
  mapping is a fresh derivation from =doom-zenburn-theme.el=, not a
  byte-for-byte restore — the original pass's mapping choices were never
  recorded anywhere (out-of-git, and this ADR logged only the override
  *count*), so a diff against the pre-regression file is not possible. Not yet
  live-verified — the user needs to restart the CLI, same as the original
  pass's [2026-08-24 Sun] step below.
- Claude Code theme "Live-verified" from "Structurally validated" [2026-08-24 Sun]
  — user restarted the CLI and confirmed the zenburn theme renders. The Emacs
  half is *not* covered by this: the daemon was still down at the time, so
  =ghostel-project= on =SPC p t= and the rest of the =config.el= block remain
  read-back-only, exactly as Validation describes.
- Emacs half live-verified; whole shell half found regressed a second time,
  then rebuilt and verified [2026-08-27 Thu] — started as a user report that a
  =SPC o t= ghostel buffer wasn't themed. It was, for anything printed *after*
  the fix (=ghostel-sync-theme= redraw was confirmed correct — a live buffer
  showed exact zenburn hexes on fresh output); the buffer's already-white
  scrollback from before the daemon picked up the =config.el= fix just never
  got retroactively recolored, which is expected, not a bug. =SPC p t= itself
  was also confirmed live: =(lookup-key doom-leader-map (kbd "p t"))=
  resolves to =ghostel-project=. That closes the one outstanding Emacs-half
  item Validation had been carrying.

  Chasing the *actual* white-prompt symptom found the real fault one layer
  down: =~/.bashrc:46= still had =#force_color_prompt=yes= commented out —
  the original fix was never there to begin with, because it turned out the
  *entire* shell half of this ADR had reverted to stock Ubuntu. Not just the
  PS1 line: no =~/.inputrc=, no =~/.gitconfig= delta/merge keys, no
  =atuin=/=eza=/=bat=/=zoxide=/=fzf= wiring, none of those binaries even
  installed, and — unlike the Claude Code regression — *no backups either*:
  the =~/.bashrc.bak-20260823= through =.bak5= that Rollback names were gone
  too. Same signature as the Claude Code regression (out-of-git, no record of
  when/why, nothing in this repo tracks the directory to diff against), now
  covering six files instead of two. Rebuilt from this ADR's own Decision
  section line-by-line (backups taken first this time:
  =~/.bashrc.bak-20260827-174931=, matching =.gitconfig= backup) rather than
  reconstructed from memory, since the spec was already written down in
  enough detail to do that. Two things came out different from the original
  pass, both recorded here since neither is written into the Decision text
  above:
  - *atuin installed as 18.20.1, not 18.19.0* — the version the ADR verified
    against no longer being the latest tag. Same install method (musl
    release binary, SHA256-verified against the published checksum, into
    =~/.local/bin=), user's choice to track current over pinning for fidelity.
  - *=FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND= now calls =fdfind= directly*, not through a
    =~/.local/bin/fd= symlink. That symlink was flagged in Consequences as
    itself un-versioned state a fresh machine wouldn't have, and this
    restore found it gone too (predates this ADR, never was this ADR's to
    restore) — calling =fdfind= directly removes the dependency rather than
    re-creating the fragility. =docs/inventory.org='s =fd= row updated to
    match.
  - Found and fixed a real bug in the =PATH= dedupe while rebuilding it: the
    original one-liner (=awk '!seen[$0]++' with RS=ORS=":"= over a
    here-string) never dedupes the *last* =PATH= entry against an earlier
    occurrence of itself — mawk's single-char =RS= folds the here-string's
    trailing newline into the final record, so =".../bin"= and
    =".../bin\n"= compare unequal. Silent failure: it looked deduped for
    every other position. Replaced with a plain =while= loop over
    =${PATH%%:*}=/=${PATH#*:}=, byte-for-byte and re-verified (19 entries,
    19 unique, in a fresh =TERM=xterm-ghostty bash -i=). Ubuntu's =fzf=
    package (0.44.1) also turned out too old for =fzf --bash= (needs 0.48+),
    so that step falls back to sourcing
    =/usr/share/doc/fzf/examples/key-bindings.bash= directly, guarded by a
    version probe; Ubuntu's =fzf= package ships no separate bash completion
    file at all (only zsh/fish examples), so completion was always meant to
    come from =bash-completion=, not a second sourced file — the original
    Decision text's "and the completion file explicitly" was wrong on
    inspection, corrected in =docs/inventory.org=.
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]] made ghostel the terminal this
config actually lives in — =SPC p t= for a project shell, and every
claude-code-ide.el agent session rendered through it. What that ADR did *not*
do is configure it. Before this pass =config.el= set exactly zero ghostel
variables: one =defun=, one =map!=, one =claude-code-ide-terminal-backend=
line.

The prompt was narrower than the result. The user asked for Claude Code's own
TUI to match =doom-zenburn=; checking that surfaced three untouched layers
stacked on each other:

1. *Claude Code's colors*, which come from a built-in theme table compiled into
   the CLI binary and have no relationship to the Emacs theme.
2. *Ghostel's own configuration* — 69 =defcustom= s and 19 faces, all at
   upstream defaults, including a hand-rolled project-terminal command that
   upstream had since obsoleted.
3. *The bash environment underneath*, at Ubuntu 24.04 stock: no =EDITOR=,
   1000-line history, no =~/.inputrc=, and a =PS1= that was rendering
   uncoloured for a reason that had nothing to do with ghostel.

Each layer's colors and behavior were being blamed on the layer above it, which
is why they are one ADR rather than three.

** Decision drivers
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first, and its generalisation: ghostel ships 69 tunables and
  19 faces, so anything hand-rolled here competes with upstream rather than
  filling a gap. The pass should *delete* config where upstream caught up.
- Zenburn is this config's theme (=doom-theme 'doom-zenburn=, =config.el:52=).
  Any color introduced at the terminal layer should derive from
  =doom-zenburn-theme.el= 's palette, not from a hand-picked approximation of
  it.
- =docs/standards.org= =std-no-scope-creep=: configure the terminal layer,
  don't redesign the shell or audit the machine.
- [[id:adr-torrent-transmission][ADR-030]] and
  [[id:adr-everywhere-direnv][ADR-032]] precedent for state that cannot live in
  this repository: record it in the ADR with explicit rollback commands rather
  than pretending it is version-controlled.
- =.claude/rules/security.md=: treat external services as outside the trust
  boundary, and don't leave an outbound path enabled by default without saying
  so.
- =std-binaries=: don't claim a binary is present without checking, and record
  what the project newly depends on in [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]].

** Candidates
| Area | Candidate | Fate |
|------+-----------+------|
| Claude Code theme | Pick the closest built-in (=dark=, =dark-ansi=) | Rejected — =dark-ansi= would inherit the terminal palette and so *would* be zenburn inside ghostel, but Claude Code is also run outside Emacs, where it would inherit whatever that terminal uses |
| Claude Code theme | *Custom theme file, =~/.claude/themes/zenburn.json=, =base: dark= plus 71 overrides* | *Chosen* — the CLI has a first-class custom-theme loader; the mechanism was read out of the binary rather than guessed |
| Claude Code theme | Hand-edit the compiled theme table in the binary | Rejected — 330MB single-file bundle, replaced on every =claude update= |
| ghostel palette | Set the 16 =ghostel-color-*= faces to zenburn hexes | Rejected — *already correct*. They inherit =ansi-color-*=, which =doom-zenburn= already defines as the exact zenburn palette. Zero lines needed |
| Terminal background | Leave =ghostel-default= alone | Rejected — solaire already dims it to =#303030=, but so is every other non-file buffer, so the terminal was indistinguishable from treemacs and dirvish-side |
| Terminal background | *Explicit =:background "#2B2B2B"= (zenburn =bg-1=), overriding only that attribute* | *Chosen* — one step darker than solaire's =#303030=, and leaves the module's solaire hook still managing =:inherit= |
| Project terminal | Keep =my/project-ghostel= | Rejected — upstream =ghostel-project= does strictly more (reuses an existing terminal per project instead of spawning duplicates, prefixes the buffer with the project name, distinguishes TRAMP from local projects of the same name, passes the prefix argument through) |
| Project terminal | *Delete the defun; bind =SPC p t= to =ghostel-project=* | *Chosen* — this pass's only net deletion, and it supersedes the rationale [[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]] recorded for keeping the wrapper |
| Uncoloured prompt | Set ghostel face colors / =ghostel-bold-color= | Rejected — misdiagnosis. Bash was emitting *no* color at all; nothing at the Emacs layer could fix it |
| Uncoloured prompt | Hand-write a truecolor =PS1= with zenburn hexes | Rejected — pins the prompt to one theme, and breaks the palette-follows-terminal property everything else in this pass relies on |
| Uncoloured prompt | *Uncomment =force_color_prompt=yes=; recolor the existing =PS1= to palette slots =00;32= / =00;33=* | *Chosen* — two one-line edits to Ubuntu's own prompt block, and the colors resolve through the terminal's palette, so they are zenburn in ghostel and correct elsewhere |
| Bold text | Leave =ghostel-bold-color= =nil= | Rejected — bold keeps the normal foreground, which flattens the TUI output claude-code-ide renders |
| atuin install | =cargo install atuin= | Rejected — not viable. atuin 18.19's MSRV is 1.97.0; this box has rustc 1.75.0 |
| atuin install | =cargo install atuin --version 18.2.0= | Rejected by the user — builds on 1.75, but pins to a release roughly two years old |
| atuin install | rustup, then build current atuin from source | Rejected by the user — adds a second Rust toolchain shadowing apt's, plus a 10-20 minute compile |
| atuin install | *Prebuilt =x86_64-unknown-linux-musl= binary from the v18.19.0 release, SHA256-verified, into =~/.local/bin=* | *Chosen* (user's pick) — current version, no toolchain, static build so glibc is irrelevant |
| atuin =?= binding | Leave atuin's AI keybinding as shipped | Rejected by the user — see Consequences |

** Evidence
Everything below was read from the running system, the running Emacs daemon, or
the shipped source — not inferred:

- *Claude Code's custom-theme mechanism*, read out of the 2.1.241 bundle
  (=/home/james/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.241=) rather than from docs:
  themes load from =<user config dir>/themes/*.json=; the parser accepts
  ={name, base, overrides}=, defaults =base= to ="dark"=, and *silently drops*
  any override key not present in the base theme; the color validator accepts
  =rgb(r,g,b)=, =#RRGGBB=, =#RGB=, =ansi256(n)=, and =ansi:<name>=. The theme
  is selected as =custom:<slug>=. Files over 256KB are skipped.
- All 71 override keys were diffed against the base dark theme's own key set
  before publishing: zero invalid keys. Exactly one base key is deliberately
  left unset — =background=, whose base value is =rgb(0,204,204)= and whose use
  was not identifiable from the bundle.
- *Palette source*: every hex comes from
  =/home/james/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-30.2/doom-themes/doom-zenburn-theme.el:51-96=,
  not from a zenburn reproduction.
- *Ghostel was already zenburn.* =ghostel-color-*= all =:inherit ansi-color-*=,
  and those were read live from the daemon: =ansi-color-red= =#CC9393=,
  =-green= =#7F9F7F=, =-yellow= =#F0DFAF=, =-blue= =#8CD0D3=,
  =-magenta= =#DC8CC3=, =-cyan= =#93E0E3=, =-white= =#DCDCDC=,
  =-bright-black= =#5F5F5F=, =-bright-white= =#FFFFEF=. Exact zenburn. No
  =ghostel-color-*= override was written, because none was needed.
- =ghostel-default= was *already* dimmer than file buffers before this pass:
  read live, it inherits =solaire-default-face= (=#303030=) against =default=
  's =#3F3F3F=, with =solaire-global-mode= =t=. The change is separation from
  the *other* solaire buffers, not from file buffers.
- *Root cause of the uncoloured prompt*, at =~/.bashrc:39=:
  =case "$TERM" in xterm-color|*-256color) color_prompt=yes;; esac=. Ghostel
  sets =TERM=xterm-ghostty=, which matches neither pattern, so bash fell
  through to the *non-color* =PS1= branch at line 62. Confirmed by reading the
  block and by dumping =PS1= from =TERM=xterm-ghostty bash -i=. =tput setaf 1=
  was checked under that =TERM= before relying on =force_color_prompt=, and
  =infocmp xterm-ghostty= confirmed the terminfo entry is installed.
- =ghostel-command-finish-functions= takes =(BUFFER STATUS)= — not
  =(command code)= — and upstream's docstring states it fires *synchronously
  from the terminal parser*, which is why the hook added here defers its work
  through =run-at-time=.
- =ghostel-eval-cmds= is dispatched through OSC 52's =;e= subcommand, so it is
  gated on =ghostel-enable-osc52=. Enabling OSC 52 therefore enables *both*
  clipboard writes and whitelisted Emacs command invocation from the terminal.
  This was found by reading the =defcustom= docstrings, and it is the reason
  the whitelist addition is commented as untrusted input.
- =ghostel-keymap-exceptions= defaults to
  =("C-c" "C-x" "C-u" "C-h" "M-x" "M-:" "C-\\")= and those are the keys routed
  to *Emacs instead of* the terminal. =C-r= is not among them, so it already
  reaches the shell — correcting an earlier suggestion in this pass that an
  exception was needed for fzf/atuin, which would have done the opposite.
- =PATH= hygiene, measured before and after: 34 entries with 11 duplicates
  (=.profile= and =.bashrc= both prepend =~/.local/bin=,
  =~/.config/emacs/bin=, pyenv, snap, and four Claude plugin dirs) → 23
  entries, 0 duplicates, first-occurrence order preserved. No literal-tilde
  entry of the [[id:tshoot-path-tilde][=$PATH= tilde]] kind was present.
- *atuin could not be built here.* Read from the crates.io sparse index:
  18.19.0 declares =rust_version 1.97.0=; this box has =cargo=/=rustc=
  =1.75.0= at =/usr/bin=. The newest non-yanked atuin with MSRV ≤ 1.75 is
  18.2.0 (=1.67=).
- atuin's tarball SHA256 matched the published =.sha256=
  (=a2cd1f7bba0e45cf620683833a6414b9dd74a0c446e4d819258b6717109bc0fd=).
  Both files came from the same GitHub host, so this establishes transfer
  integrity, *not* provenance independent of GitHub — atuin publishes no
  detached signature.
- atuin's =?= binding was read out of its generated init script, not assumed:
  =_atuin_ai_question_mark= triggers =atuin ai inline= only when
  =READLINE_LINE= is empty or exactly ="?"=; its =else= branch inserts a
  literal =?= at point. So ordinary mid-line =?= was never broken — the
  exposure is specifically =?= as the first keystroke on an empty prompt.
- =bat= 's binary on Debian/Ubuntu is =batcat= (the =bat= name collides with
  =bacula-console-qt=), confirmed by =dpkg=/=command -v=. =bat --list-themes=
  confirms an =ansi= theme exists, which is what makes CLI highlighting follow
  the terminal palette instead of hardcoded hexes.
- Post-change shell state, read back from a fresh =TERM=xterm-ghostty bash -i=:
  =EDITOR='emacsclient -nw'=, =VISUAL='emacsclient -c -a emacs'=,
  =HISTSIZE=100000=, =HISTCONTROL=ignoreboth:erasedups=, and =autocd cdspell
  dirspell globstar checkjobs cmdhist histappend= all =on=.
- fzf's bindings survive the =editing-mode vi= switch: =bind -m vi-insert -X=
  lists =\C-t= → =fzf-file-widget=, and after atuin loads, =bind -m vi-insert
  -s= resolves =\C-r= to atuin's macro chain. Verified per keymap rather than
  assumed, because =bind -p= does not list =bind -x= entries and initially
  looked like a failure.
- After the =?= unbind, =bind -X= shows no =?= widget in =emacs=, =vi-insert=,
  or =vi-command=, while =\C-r= still resolves to atuin.
- atuin imported 1998 commands, 1135 unique, from the existing
  =~/.bash_history=; =atuin stats= runs inside an initialised shell.

*Not evidence.* The Emacs half of this pass was verified by =load-file= plus
reading every variable and keybinding back — =ghostel-max-scrollback=
=20971520=, =ghostel-bold-color= =bright=, =ghostel-enable-osc52= =t=,
=ghostel-default= background =#2B2B2B= and height =0.95=, the popup rule in
=display-buffer-alist=, =ghostel-project= on =SPC p t= across five evil
states, =C-c C-n= and =C-c C-e= resolving in =ghostel-mode-map=, the
=magit-status= whitelist entry, and =ghostel-sync-theme= on
=doom-load-theme-hook= — but that was *before* the user restarted Emacs, and
the server was down afterwards. Nothing here has been re-checked in the
restarted daemon, and no ghostel buffer has been looked at by a human since.
The Claude Code theme was structurally validated here (key diff, color-format
check) and only rendered later — see the LOGBOOK entry.

** Decision
*Emacs (=config.el=)*

1. Delete =my/project-ghostel=; bind =SPC p t= to upstream =ghostel-project=,
   and register it in =project-switch-commands=.
2. =after! ghostel=: =ghostel-max-scrollback= 20MB (from 5MB),
   =ghostel-bold-color= ='bright=, =ghostel-enable-osc52= =t=; add
   =("magit-status" magit-status)= to =ghostel-eval-cmds=; add a
   =ghostel-command-finish-functions= hook that reports off-screen command
   completion, deferred off the parser via =run-at-time=.
3. =custom-set-faces!= =ghostel-default= =:background "#2B2B2B" :height 0.95=.
4. =setq-hook!= =line-spacing= =0.1= on =ghostel-mode-hook=.
5. =set-popup-rule!= for =^\*ghostel= — =:size 0.35 :select t :quit nil :ttl
   nil=, since a terminal holding a live process is not a transient buffer.
6. =ghostel-sync-theme= on =doom-load-theme-hook=, so open terminals don't keep
   a stale palette across a theme switch.
7. =ghostel-mode-map=: =C-c C-p= / =C-c C-n= prompt motion, =C-c C-f= / =C-c
   C-b= hyperlink motion, =C-c C-l= clear scrollback, =C-c C-e= line mode.
   Line mode is *bound, not defaulted* — =ghostel-initial-input-mode= is left
   alone until it earns the change.

*Claude Code (out of git)*

=~/.claude/themes/zenburn.json=, =base: dark=, 71 zenburn overrides;
=~/.claude/settings.json= =theme= set to ="custom:zenburn"=.

*Shell (out of git)*

=~/.bashrc=: =EDITOR=/=VISUAL= to =emacsclient=; history to 100000/200000 with
=ignoreboth:erasedups= and =HISTTIMEFORMAT=; =shopt= =cmdhist autocd cdspell
dirspell globstar checkjobs=; a first-occurrence =PATH= dedupe;
=force_color_prompt=yes= with =PS1= recoloured to palette slots =00;32= /
=00;33=; =bat=/=eza=/=fzf=/=zoxide= wiring with =BAT_THEME=ansi= and zenburn
=FZF_DEFAULT_OPTS=; =atuin init bash --disable-up-arrow= after fzf; and
=bind '"?": self-insert'= to undo atuin's AI keybinding.

=~/.inputrc= (new): =editing-mode vi= with mode indicators, prefix history
search on Up/Down, case-insensitive completion, =colored-stats=.

=~/.gitconfig=: =delta= as =core.pager= and =interactive.diffFilter=,
=delta.syntax-theme=ansi=, =merge.conflictstyle=zdiff3=.

*Binaries*: =eza=, =bat=, =zoxide=, =fzf=, =git-delta= from apt (installed by
the user); =atuin= 18.19.0 as a SHA256-verified prebuilt musl binary in
=~/.local/bin=.

** Consequences
- *The out-of-git surface grew from two items to six.* [[id:adr-torrent-transmission][ADR-030]]'s =magnet:=
  handler and [[id:adr-everywhere-direnv][ADR-032]]'s =direnv= hook are now joined by =~/.bashrc= 's much
  larger block, a new =~/.inputrc=, three =~/.gitconfig= keys, and two Claude
  Code files. A machine restored from this repository alone gets a terminal
  that is themed correctly inside Emacs and stock everywhere else. The
  "Reproducible machine provisioning" future phase in
  [[id:roadmap-future][roadmap.org]] absorbs all of it.
- *OSC 52 is the widest new attack surface in this pass.* Enabling it lets any
  process in a ghostel buffer write the kill ring and system clipboard with no
  prompt — and because =ghostel-eval-cmds= dispatches through the same OSC 52
  =;e= subcommand, it also lets terminal output invoke whitelisted Emacs
  commands with arbitrary string arguments. That whitelist is now
  =find-file=, =find-file-other-window=, =dired=, =dired-other-window=,
  =message=, and =magit-status=. This was flagged and accepted deliberately;
  the mitigation is that the whitelist stays short and every addition is
  judged as untrusted input.
- *atuin ships an outbound AI path enabled by default*, bound to =?= on an
  empty prompt, sending the query to atuin's own service. There is no setting
  for it in =atuin default-config=, so it is disabled here by rebinding =?=
  after =atuin init= — a fix that a future atuin release could break by
  binding into a different keymap or a different key. Worth re-checking on
  upgrade.
- *Command history now lives in three places*: =~/.bash_history=, ghostel's
  20MB in-memory scrollback per buffer, and atuin's SQLite database. atuin is
  local-only (no =register=/=login=), but that is a third retention location
  for command text, which can include arguments that should not be retained.
- *Terminal-layer color now follows the palette in three tools at once.*
  =BAT_THEME=ansi=, =delta.syntax-theme=ansi=, and ghostel's own faces all
  resolve through =ansi-color-*=, so a future Emacs theme change re-colors the
  CLI tools for free — and equally, anything that breaks =ansi-color-*= breaks
  all three.
- *The readline =vi= switch is a behavior change, not a cosmetic one*, and the
  broadest-reaching item in this pass. Every interactive bash on this machine
  now starts in vi-insert. It was chosen to match Evil, but nothing about the
  terminal-theming goal required it.
- 20MB of scrollback is *per live ghostel buffer*. Several open project
  terminals is a real memory cost, not a free win.
- =ghostel-project= supersedes the wrapper-defun rationale recorded in
  [[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]]; that ADR should be read as
  amended by this one on that single point. Its terminal-backend decision is
  untouched.
- [[id:inv-binaries][inventory.org]]'s =fd= row said "not installed" and was
  stale. It is installed, but not where the row would have implied: apt's
  =fd-find= ships the binary as =/usr/bin/fdfind=. Originally
  =FZF_DEFAULT_COMMAND= depended on a user-made =~/.local/bin/fd= symlink to
  =/usr/lib/cargo/bin/fd= for the plain =fd= name — itself un-versioned
  state a fresh machine wouldn't have. That symlink didn't survive the
  2026-08-27 shell-half regression (see LOGBOOK); the rebuild calls
  =fdfind= directly instead of re-creating it.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
- *Shell half: live-verified, twice* — 2026-08-23 against the original build,
  and again 2026-08-27 against the from-scratch rebuild after the regression
  (see LOGBOOK). Both times: every setting read back from a fresh
  =TERM=xterm-ghostty bash -i=. =PATH= dedupe measured before and after (2026-08-27:
  19 entries in, 19 unique — the dedupe bug found that day was caught by this
  same check, not missed by it). fzf and atuin key bindings checked *per
  readline keymap*, including after the =?= unbind (2026-08-27: confirmed via
  =bind -p | grep '"?"'= showing =self-insert=, since =atuin init bash='s own
  source confirms it binds =?= through =bind -x=, which =bind -p= does not
  list). 2026-08-27 additionally rendered real output in a live ghostel
  buffer and read back the text properties directly — prompt and =eza=
  output both carrying exact zenburn hex foregrounds, not just Emacs-side
  face values.
- *Emacs half: live-verified 2026-08-27.* The one outstanding item —
  =SPC p t= in the restarted daemon — closed: =(lookup-key doom-leader-map
  (kbd "p t"))= resolves to =ghostel-project=. =ghostel-sync-theme= confirmed
  registered on =doom-load-theme-hook=, callable without error, and its
  effect confirmed on a live buffer (see LOGBOOK).
- *Claude Code theme: live-verified 2026-08-24.* 71 override keys diffed
  against the base theme's key set (zero invalid), every value checked against
  the CLI's own color-format validator, JSON parsed — then the user restarted
  the CLI and confirmed it renders.
- No =doom sync= and no =doom doctor=: no =init.el= or =packages.el= change.
- =atuin= tarball SHA256 verified against the published checksum before
  install; scratch download left in the job tmp dir, not in the repo.

** Rollback
- =config.el=: =git revert= the commit carrying this pass. Restoring
  =my/project-ghostel= specifically is not necessary — =ghostel-project= is
  upstream and does more.
- Claude Code theme: =rm ~/.claude/themes/zenburn.json= and set =theme= back to
  ="auto"= in =~/.claude/settings.json= (previous value; a
  =~/.claude/settings.json.bak= also predates this pass).
- Shell: the original pass's five timestamped backups
  (=~/.bashrc.bak-20260823= through =.bak5=) did not survive the 2026-08-27
  regression — see LOGBOOK. The current cleanest revert path is
  =~/.bashrc.bak-20260827-174931= and =~/.gitconfig.bak-20260827-174931=,
  taken immediately before that day's rebuild — stock Ubuntu =.bashrc=, no
  delta/merge keys. =rm ~/.inputrc= (it did not exist before either pass).
  =git config --global --unset= for =core.pager=, =interactive.diffFilter=,
  =delta.navigate=, =delta.syntax-theme=, =merge.conflictstyle=.
- atuin: =rm ~/.local/bin/atuin ~/.local/share/atuin/= (the latter destroys the
  imported history database) and delete its =~/.bashrc= block.
- apt tools: =sudo apt remove eza bat zoxide fzf git-delta=, and delete the
  aliases/exports block from =~/.bashrc=.

** Revisit when
- Emacs is restarted and =SPC p t= is actually pressed — the one outstanding
  verification, the daemon having been down for the rest of the session.
- =ghostel-line-mode= has had real use, at which point
  =ghostel-initial-input-mode= is the decision to make.
- An atuin upgrade — its =?= binding is undone by a =bind= call that assumes
  atuin's current keymap choice, and there is no config flag to rely on
  instead.
- A =claude update= changes the custom-theme schema. The loader silently drops
  unknown override keys, so the failure mode is colors quietly reverting to
  base, not an error.
- Several project terminals are open at once and memory matters — 20MB of
  scrollback each.
- The =background= key in the Claude Code theme is identified. It is the one
  base key left unset, because its base value (=rgb(0,204,204)=) gave no clue
  what renders it.
- Claude Code gains a way to reference the terminal palette (an =ansi:= base
  theme with overrides), which would let the custom theme follow
  =ansi-color-*= the way =bat= and =delta= now do, instead of restating zenburn
  hexes.
- The clipboard or a whitelisted Emacs command fires unexpectedly in a ghostel
  buffer — the OSC 52 consequence above, whose symptom would be silent.

* ADR-034: EPUB reading in Emacs via =nov.el=, configured to one setting
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-epub-nov
:STATUS:   Implemented, live-verified (user opened an EPUB in their own session 2026-08-29 and confirmed it working; individual checks not itemized back one by one)
:DATE:     2026-08-29
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "Implemented, live-verified" from "Implemented in config; not
  live-verified" [2026-08-29 Sat] — user opened a real =.epub= in their own
  interactive session and reported it verified. They confirmed the outcome
  rather than walking the Validation list item by item, so =nov-mode= firing
  and readable rendering are established; the finer points that list names
  (images specifically, =save-place= restore across a reopen, the
  =evil-collection= bindings individually) are covered only by that overall
  confirmation. Same itemization caveat as
  [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] and its siblings carry.
:END:

** Context
Reading EPUBs meant leaving Emacs for an external reader. The config gained a
=nov= declaration and a =use-package!= block; this ADR is written *after* that
change, while it was still uncommitted, to close the dangling =ADR-034=
reference the code comments already carried. The original scoping conversation
was not recorded, so the Candidates table below is an evaluation made now, not
a transcript of the choice as it was made.

Doom ships no ebook module. =nov.el= appears in Doom's tree only as an external
prerequisite named by =:lang org +noter=
(=sources/doom+/modules/lang/org/README.org:61=, alongside =:tools pdf= and
DocView) — the module never declares or installs it.

** Decision drivers
- =std-no-scope-creep=: reading EPUBs, nothing more. No library manager, no
  annotation layer, no sync.
- =AGENTS.md= Doom-first: =package!= + =use-package!=, no second package
  manager.
- =AGENTS.md= one-tool-per-responsibility: an EPUB renderer is a distinct
  responsibility with nothing currently covering it.
- Configure only what the surrounding config *doesn't* already supply — Evil
  bindings, reading position, and file browsing all have existing owners here.

** Candidates
| Candidate                             | Fate     | Reason                                                                                                     |
|---------------------------------------+----------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| =nov.el=                              | Chosen   | Only maintained Emacs EPUB renderer; renders through =shr=, so it inherits the theme and =shr= settings already in play |
| External reader (foliate, calibre)    | Rejected | Works, but leaves Emacs — the thing this config exists to avoid — and adds a GUI app to the launcher for one file type |
| =pandoc= convert to Org, read as text | Rejected | Lossy on structure and images, one-way, and turns every book into a file to manage                        |
| Do nothing                            | Rejected | The gap was the prompt for the change                                                                      |

** Evidence
Everything below was checked directly, 2026-08-29:

- =nov= 0.5.0 built at
  =~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-31.1/nov/=; its only package
  dependency, =esxml=, is built alongside it (=Package-Requires: ((esxml
  "0.3.6") (emacs "25.1"))=). =doom sync= has therefore already run for the
  =packages.el= change.
- =nov.el= carries *no* =auto-mode-alist= autoload — grepping both =nov.el= and
  =nov-autoloads.el= for =auto-mode-alist= returns nothing. The =:mode= keyword
  in the =use-package!= block is load-bearing, not decoration; without it
  =nov-mode= never fires on a =.epub=.
- =nov-text-width='s own docstring (=nov.el:92-100=): =nil= fills to the window
  width, an integer fills to that many columns, =t= "disable[s] filling
  completely". =perfect-margin= is active (=config.el:167-171=,
  =perfect-margin-visible-width= 100), so leaving =nov-text-width= at its =nil=
  default would hard-fill text a second time inside margins that already set
  the column. =t= is the setting that lets =perfect-margin= own it alone.
- Evil bindings come free: =(evil +everywhere)= is on (=init.el:66=),
  =evil-collection= ships an =evil-collection-nov.el= module, and =nov= is in
  =evil-collection-mode-list= (=evil-collection.el:325=).
- Reading position comes free: Doom enables =save-place-mode= globally on
  =doom-first-input-hook= (=~/.config/emacs/lisp/doom-emacs.el:1724=).
- Browsing a shelf of files comes free: =SPC o /= (dirvish) and =SPC o s=
  (=dirvish-side=, =config.el:359=) already do it — see
  [[id:adr-cosmetic-module-bindings][ADR-031]] for why both exist.

Not checked: no =.epub= has actually been opened. Rendering, images, evil
navigation, and position restore are all reasoned from source and module
wiring, not observed.

** Decision
=packages.el=: =(package! nov)=.

=config.el=: a =use-package! nov= block with =:mode ("\\.epub\\'" .
nov-mode)= and exactly one =:config= setting, =(setq nov-text-width t)=.

No keybinding, no hook, no =auto-mode= alias beyond the one above, and no
=nov-*= face or program settings — every other axis already has an owner named
in Evidence.

** Consequences
- =.epub= files opened from anywhere in Emacs (dired, =find-file=, an Org link)
  render in-buffer instead of prompting or opening raw XML.
- =:lang org +noter= is enabled (=init.el:172=) and named =nov.el= as one of
  its three supported backends. =org-noter= against an EPUB is now possible
  where it previously was not. Unrequested and unexercised — noted so the
  capability isn't rediscovered as a surprise.
- One more package in the tree (=nov= + =esxml=), both pure elisp, no native
  module and no external binary — unlike the =vterm=/=ghostel= class of
  dependency ([[id:adr-terminal-backend-ghostel][ADR-014]]).
- =docs/inventory.org= needs a =nov= row; it does not have one yet.
- If EPUB reading turns into a habit, the deferred axes (a library view, font
  or line-spacing tuning, annotation) become real decisions. They are
  deliberately not made here.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
Done:
- =doom sync= — implied by =nov= and =esxml= being present in
  =build-31.1/=; not re-run in this session.
- =doom doctor= clean, 2026-08-29: 77 modules, 231 packages. One warning, the
  standing false "development build of Emacs (31.1)" one — the =markdown
  +tree-sitter= warning earlier entries carry no longer appears. The package
  count is not attributed here: the 230 an earlier entry recorded predates
  other changes, so the delta doesn't cleanly isolate this one. What *is*
  established is that =esxml= arrived with =nov= rather than already being
  present — both repos were cloned and both build dirs written in the same
  second (18:33:30-31), and no other installed package declares =esxml= in its
  =Package-Requires=.

Outstanding, all requiring the user's own interactive session:
- Open a real =.epub=: it enters =nov-mode= rather than showing raw markup.
- Text renders at =perfect-margin='s column with no second, narrower fill
  inside it, and images render.
- Evil bindings work in the buffer (=j=/=k=, =n=/=p= for chapter movement) —
  proves the =evil-collection= module loaded.
- Close and reopen the file: =save-place= restores the reading position.

** Rollback
Remove =(package! nov)= from =packages.el= and the =use-package! nov= block
from =config.el=, then =doom sync=. Nothing outside =$DOOMDIR= is touched —
unlike [[id:adr-torrent-transmission][ADR-030]] and
[[id:adr-terminal-layer-pass][ADR-033]], this decision leaves no out-of-git
surface to restore. =save-place='s stored positions for =.epub= files persist
harmlessly in =doom-state-dir=.

** Revisit when
- An EPUB renders badly enough to need =nov-*= tuning — most likely
  =nov-variable-pitch=, =nov-text-width= as an integer after all, or a
  =shr=-level setting — which would mean the "configure one thing" premise
  didn't hold.
- A shelf of EPUBs accumulates and =dirvish= stops being enough, making a
  library view a real decision rather than a deferred one.
- =org-noter= gets used against an EPUB, at which point the =+noter= backend
  interaction needs its own verification and possibly its own entry.
- =nov.el= goes unmaintained (0.5.0 is the current release) and a fork or
  successor appears — it is the only maintained option today, which is the
  whole basis of the Candidates table.
- Emacs gains, or Doom adopts, a first-party ebook module, which would make
  this a module flag instead of a package declaration.

* ADR-035: Literate configuration — one =config.org= tangling Emacs and the shell layer
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-literate-config
:STATUS:   Implemented; not live-verified (needs an Emacs restart in the user's own session)
:DATE:     2026-08-29
:END:

** Context
Two problems, one shape.

The first is [[id:adr-terminal-layer-pass][ADR-033]]'s shell layer. It lives in
=$HOME= — =~/.bashrc=, =~/.inputrc=, =~/.gitconfig= — outside any repository,
and it has silently reverted to stock *twice*. The second time took its own
=~/.bashrc.bak*= backups with it, so there was nothing to diff against and no
record of when or why. Both times it was found by accident, chasing an
unrelated symptom. Nothing in this project could detect a third occurrence.

The second is that =config.el= had grown to 1328 lines whose reasoning lives in
comments, cross-referencing ADRs by number. The prose and the code are already
inseparable; they were just in the wrong file format for that.

A literate config solves both: prose and code in one =config.org=, and the
shell files become tangle targets, which puts them under version control where
a revert is a visible diff rather than a silent loss.

** Decision drivers
- The shell layer needs a regression guard that requires no discipline to
  work. Both prior regressions went unnoticed *because* noticing depended on
  someone looking.
- Doom-first ([[file:../AGENTS.md][AGENTS.md]]): use the =:config literate=
  module Doom already ships, not a hand-rolled tangling scheme.
- The migration must be provably lossless. 1610 lines of working config across
  three files, plus three live dotfiles, is not something to retype.
- =docs/decisions.org= stays the home of long-form reasoning; =config.org= gets
  section-level prose and links, not a duplicate of the ADRs.

** Candidates
| Candidate                                | Fate     | Reason                                                                                                       |
|------------------------------------------+----------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Doom =:config literate= + one =config.org= | Chosen   | Ships with Doom, tangles on =doom sync= and on save, and per-block =:tangle= targets reach outside =$DOOMDIR= — verified, see Evidence |
| =config.org= + separate =shell.org=        | Rejected | The module only tangles =+literate-config-file=; a second file needs its own =bin/org-tangle= step for no gain |
| Dotfiles repo + GNU Stow symlinks          | Rejected | Solves the shell half only, adds a second tool and a second repo, and leaves =config.el= as-is               |
| Verification script asserting ADR-033      | Rejected | Only fires when run, so it inherits the exact failure mode — depending on someone to look                    |
| Status quo, log the audit and move on      | Rejected | This is the posture that produced two silent regressions                                                     |

** Evidence
Four things were tested before any file was touched, each because the design
depended on the answer:

1. *Per-block tangle targets work.* A scratch =config.org= with four blocks
   tangled to four destinations: the default dest, =packages.el=, a path
   outside the org file's directory, and =:tangle no= (skipped). The DEST
   argument Doom passes to =org-babel-tangle-file= only sets the default; an
   explicit per-block =:tangle= wins. This is what makes the dotfiles
   reachable at all.
2. *Blank-line fidelity.* Org pads exactly one blank line between blocks
   tangled to the same file, and =:padline no= instead *strips* a block's
   trailing blank. Neither is what a verbatim copy needs. The working
   combination is: cut sections at blank lines, strip each block's trailing
   blanks, and let the default padding re-insert exactly one.
3. *Column-0 =*= survives.* =~/.bashrc:71= has a bare =*)= case pattern at
   column 0, which is also Org heading syntax. Tangled through a =sh= block it
   came back byte-identical and =bash -n= clean, so no comma-escaping is
   needed.
4. *The migration is lossless.* Generated =config.org= was tangled in a scratch
   directory against a fake =$HOME=, and all six outputs compared with =cmp=:
   =config.el=, =packages.el=, =init.el=, =~/.bashrc=, =~/.inputrc=,
   =~/.gitconfig= — *all six byte-identical* to the files they replace. Only
   after that did the two deliberate additions go in (below), and the final
   diff against the originals contains additions only, no removed or altered
   line, in any of the six.

After installation: =doom sync= clean (5.3s, 290 packages), =doom doctor=
clean at 78 modules (up from 77 — the =literate= module) and 231 packages, with
only the standing false "development build of Emacs (31.1)" warning.
=check-parens= in =emacs-lisp-mode= passes on the tangled =config.el=,
=init.el= and =packages.el=; =bash -n= passes on =~/.bashrc=; =git config
--list= parses =~/.gitconfig=.

*Not* verified: the config has not been loaded by a real Emacs. The daemon was
not restarted, so every runtime claim here rests on tangling, syntax checks and
=doom doctor=, not on a running session.

** Decision
=init.el=: enable =:config literate=.

=$DOOMDIR/config.org= becomes the single source, tangling to six targets:

| Target          | Via                                | Note                                    |
|-----------------+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------|
| =config.el=     | file-level =header-args= default   | 24 sections, one per subsystem          |
| =packages.el=   | =:tangle packages.el=              | one block, unchanged                    |
| =init.el=       | =:tangle init.el=                  | one block — =doom!= is a single form    |
| =~/.bashrc=     | =:tangle ~/.bashrc=, 4 blocks      | stock Ubuntu / toolchains / direnv / ADR-033 |
| =~/.inputrc=    | =:tangle ~/.inputrc=               |                                         |
| =~/.gitconfig=  | =:tangle ~/.gitconfig=             |                                         |

Two deliberate additions beyond a verbatim copy:

1. A =defadvice!= =:override= on =+literate-recompile-maybe-h=. Doom's
   tangle-on-save fires for *any* org file under =doom-user-dir=, and this repo
   keeps =PROJECT.org= and all of =docs/*.org= there — saving a 6000-line ADR
   file would otherwise spawn a full retangle. The override narrows it to
   =+literate-config-file= itself.
2. A three-line generated-by banner at the head of each tangled dotfile,
   naming =config.org= and this ADR, so anyone (or any agent) opening
   =~/.bashrc= directly is told their edit will be overwritten.

This is deliberately a *faithful* conversion: every in-code comment stayed in
its block rather than being promoted to Org prose. That promotion is a later,
incremental pass, done section by section where it earns its keep — doing it
during the migration would have made the byte-for-byte check impossible, which
was the whole safety argument.

** Consequences
- *The shell layer is now under version control.* A third regression shows up
  as an unstaged diff in =config.org=, and =git checkout= restores it. This is
  the point of the exercise.
- *Those dotfiles are now generated.* Editing =~/.bashrc= directly is lost on
  the next tangle — which happens on every =doom sync= and on every save of
  =config.org=. The banner says so in the file itself.
- *=init.el= edits land one sync late.* Doom loads =init.el= long before it
  tangles =config.org= during =doom sync=, so an edit to that block is written
  during one sync and takes effect on the next. Doom's own FAQ documents this;
  =bin/org-tangle= is the escape hatch if it ever matters.
- *=config.el=, =init.el= and =packages.el= stay committed*, even though they
  are now generated. Doom needs them on disk, and committing them keeps tangle
  drift visible in =git status= instead of silent.
- *Agent instructions change.* =AGENTS.md= and =.claude/rules/doom-emacs.md=
  both said configuration goes in =config.el=. Editing =config.el= now means
  writing to a file that the next tangle overwrites. Both were updated
  alongside this ADR.
- Tangling binds =org-confirm-babel-evaluate= to nil, so a src block *can*
  execute at tangle time. Nothing here does, but it is a real property of the
  file now: a block added to =config.org= is trusted code.
- One more module in =init.el= (77 → 78) and no new packages — the literate
  module installs none.

** Rejected alternatives
See the Candidates table above.

** Validation
Done, 2026-08-29:
- Byte-identical round-trip of all six targets in an isolated scratch
  directory before installation (see Evidence 4).
- Post-install diff against the originals: additions only, no removed or
  altered lines, in all six files.
- =doom sync= clean; =doom doctor= clean at 78 modules / 231 packages, one
  standing false warning.
- =check-parens= (in =emacs-lisp-mode=) on the three tangled elisp files;
  =bash -n= on =~/.bashrc=; =git config --list= on =~/.gitconfig=.
- Pre-tangle backups taken: =~/.bashrc.bak-20260829-202429= and matching
  =.inputrc=/=.gitconfig= copies, plus a set outside =$HOME= entirely.

Outstanding, needs the user's own session:
- Restart Emacs and confirm the config loads with no error — in particular the
  =defadvice!= against =+literate-recompile-maybe-h=, which has never run.
- Save =config.org= and confirm it retangles; save a =docs/*.org= file and
  confirm it does *not*.
- Open a new terminal and confirm the shell layer is unchanged in practice
  (prompt colors, =C-r= atuin, =eza= aliases, vi-mode indicators).

** Rollback
=git checkout= =init.el= to restore the commented-out =literate= line, then
=doom sync=. =config.el=/=packages.el= are already in git at their pre-migration
content, and the shell files can be restored from
=~/.bashrc.bak-20260829-202429= and its siblings. Deleting =config.org= after
that leaves no trace of the migration. Nothing about this decision is
one-directional.

** Revisit when
- A third shell-layer regression happens anyway — which would mean the guard
  failed, and the failure mode is worth understanding before adding another.
- The prose-promotion pass makes a section's block diverge from what it
  replaced, at which point that section's byte-for-byte provenance is gone and
  only the ADR trail explains it.
- =init.el='s one-sync-late behavior actually bites, making =bin/org-tangle=
  worth wiring into a real workflow rather than being noted as an escape hatch.
- Tangle time becomes noticeable on save. =config.org= is ~2100 lines now; the
  async path exists but has not been measured.
- Doom changes =:config literate='s tangling internals — the =defadvice!=
  overrides a module-internal hook function, which is exactly the kind of thing
  an upstream refactor renames.

* ADR-036: Phase 7 module-flag and =:lang= sweep — activate flags on modules already enabled
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-module-flag-sweep
:STATUS:   Implemented and committed (8e35356). Config side verified in headless daemons; two items still need the user's own graphical session — see Validation
:DATE:     2026-08-30
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-module-sweep-non-lang][ADR-029]] enabled 22 modules at Doom defaults and recorded that none of them
had any configuration. An audit on 2026-08-30 went a level deeper and asked a
different question: not "which modules are off?" but "which modules are on and
doing nothing?"

The measurement: the Emacs half of =config.org= is about 590 lines of code, and
over half of it belongs to four areas (ghostel, Vulpea, mu4e, Python/LSP). About
35 enabled modules and module flags have zero configuration, and 55 installed
packages are never named in =config.org= at all.

Most of that is not a problem to fix with elisp — Doom binds keys for avy,
embark, vundo, git-timemachine, quickrun and the =:tools lookup= stack whether
this repo configures them or not, so the gap there is discoverability and
belongs in a document, not in =config.el=. But a subset was genuinely inert, and
another subset was a flag that had simply never been turned on.

The most serious finding was tree-sitter. =treesit-available-p= returned =t=,
Doom's =:tools tree-sitter= module set =treesit-enabled-modes t= and
=treesit-font-lock-level 4=, and =+tree-sitter= was on =:lang json=, =markdown=
and =python= — but =treesit-language-available-p= returned =nil= for every
language checked, and no grammar directory existed anywhere under
=~/.config/emacs/.local=. Every =*-ts-mode= had been silently falling back for
as long as the flags had been set.

** Decision drivers
- Capability that is enabled but inert is worse than capability that is off: it
  reads as working in =init.el= and in =docs/inventory.org=, so nobody looks.
- Doom-first ([[file:../AGENTS.md][AGENTS.md]]): a flag Doom already ships beats
  configuration written here.
- One tool per responsibility per axis ([[id:std-no-duplication][standards.org]]).
  Several attractive flags fail this test, and failing it is a reason to decline
  a flag, not a reason to make an exception.
- A flag whose external binary is absent is not an activation, it is a warning
  in =doom doctor= plus a fresh piece of inert configuration.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate | Reason |
|-----------+------+--------|
| Turn on every available flag on every enabled module | Rejected | Three of them collide with a documented contract or a package already owning that axis; two more have no backing binary installed |
| Turn on nothing, document the gap only | Rejected | Leaves tree-sitter inert, which was the finding that prompted the audit |
| Selective activation, each flag justified against an existing contract | Chosen | What is recorded below |

** Decision
Enabled, in the =init.el= block of =config.org=:

| Module | Change | Why |
|--------+--------+-----|
| =:completion vertico= | =+childframe= | Minibuffer completion in a child frame; no second engine involved |
| =:checkers syntax= | =+childframe +icons= | The module previously ran with *no flags at all* |
| =:editor format= | =+lsp= | Formatting previously reached Python only, via an explicit =set-formatter!= |
| =:lang latex= | =+cdlatex +fold= | =auctex= and =auctex-cont-latexmk= were already installed |
| =:lang org= | =+present +crypt= | Extends a module already heavily used |
| =:lang data= | enabled | CSV/XML/TOML editing, previously commented out |
| =:lang graphviz= | enabled | =dot= is installed; one of the five left commented in [[id:adr-init-flag-audit][ADR-026]] |
| =:lang rest= | =+jq= | =jq= is installed |
| =:lang web= | =+tree-sitter= | Grammars installed as part of this ADR |
| =:lang yaml= | =+tree-sitter= | Same |

Tree-sitter grammars were installed into
=~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/tree-sitter/= — nineteen of them, using Doom's own
=treesit-language-source-alist=, which is ABI-gated per language and already
redirects installs to the profile data directory. No grammar recipes were added
to =config.org=; there was nothing to add.

** Evidence
- Grammar absence, before: =treesit-language-available-p= returned =nil= for
  =python json bash markdown c yaml toml=, and =~/.config/emacs/.local= contained
  no =tree-sitter= directory and no =libtree-sitter-*.so= anywhere.
- Grammar presence, after: nineteen =.so= files installed; a fresh process
  reports =t= for =python json bash markdown yaml html css javascript typescript
  latex org toml c regex=. Two grammars (=make=, =bibtex=) build and install but
  still report unavailable — the shared object exists and the language symbol
  inside it does not match. Cosmetic, and neither has a major mode in use here.
- End-to-end, in a disposable headless daemon: visiting =t.py=, =t.json= and
  =t.yaml= yields =python-ts-mode=, =json-ts-mode= and =yaml-ts-mode=. Before
  this ADR all three fell back to their non-tree-sitter modes.
- =doom doctor= after the sweep: five warnings. One is the standing false
  positive about Emacs 31.1 being a "development build". The other four are
  =:lang data= and =:lang web= reporting absent optional binaries (=xmllint=,
  =tidy=, =stylelint=, =js-beautify=), all installed the same day; see PROJECT.org
  §7.0 for the itemised list.
- The batch approach that does *not* work, recorded so it is not retried:
  =doom emacs --batch -l script.el= loads Emacs without Doom's module
  configuration, so =treesit-language-source-alist= is empty (0 recipes) and
  =treesit-extra-load-path= is =nil=. A disposable headless daemon sees all 73
  recipes and the correct install directory.

** Rejected alternatives
- =:completion corfu +tng= — TAB-and-go. Rejected *for now*, not on principle.
  =docs/ai/keybindings.org= records a live-verified TAB precedence chain
  (Copilot overlay, then a yasnippet field, then Corfu, then a pending NES edit,
  then ordinary indent) in which Corfu accepts on =RET=, deliberately. =+tng=
  rewires exactly that. It is a candidate for its own ADR with its own
  headless-daemon verification, not a line in a sweep.
- =:lang org +journal= — org-journal duplicates the Vulpea dailies at
  =config.org::Vulpea dailies=, roughly fifty lines of working code with its own
  keybindings under =SPC n v=. [[id:adr-org-roam-with-vulpea][ADR-027]] already
  settled that Vulpea wins on overlap; the same reasoning applies unchanged.
- =:tools lsp +booster= — this was in the approved plan and had to be withdrawn.
  Doom's own doctor for the module reads =(error! "+booster does nothing without
  +eglot")=, and the lsp-mode half of the integration in =modules/tools/lsp/
  +lsp.el= is commented out behind a =TODO=. This config uses lsp-mode, with
  =lsp-pyright=, =lsp-ui= and =lsp-treemacs= configured against it. The flag
  would install a binary that nothing calls. Revisit only alongside a genuine
  lsp-mode-to-eglot migration, which is a much larger decision.
- =:lang nix=, =:lang plantuml= — neither =nix= nor =plantuml= is installed, so
  enabling either produces a doctor warning and an inert major mode.
- =:lang yaml +lsp=, =:lang web +lsp= — =yaml-language-server= and
  =typescript-language-server= are not installed. The flags become correct the
  moment their language servers are installed (done 2026-08-30), and are cheap to
  add now.
- =:lang python +uv= — =uv= is not installed and =poetry= and =pyenv= are, both
  already covered by existing flags.

** Consequences
- Ten previously commented-out or unflagged module lines are now active, and
  =doom sync= installed the packages behind them: =vertico-posframe=,
  =flycheck-posframe=, =cdlatex=, =org-tree-slide=, =restclient=,
  =ob-restclient=, =web-mode=, =yaml-mode=, =graphviz-dot-mode=, =csv-mode=.
- Python, JSON, YAML, HTML, CSS, JavaScript, TypeScript, Markdown, LaTeX, Org
  and shell buffers now use tree-sitter major modes with
  =treesit-font-lock-level 4=. This is a visible change to syntax highlighting
  in daily use, not only an internal one.
- =treesit-fold=, installed and never referenced in =config.org=, now has
  something to fold. It is not bound to anything yet.
- Four new =doom doctor= warnings until the optional binaries are installed
  (done 2026-08-30; doctor is back to one standing false positive).
- =init.el= block edits take effect one =doom sync= late, so this ADR's changes
  required two syncs, as [[id:adr-literate-config][ADR-035]] predicted.

** Validation
- =doom sync= twice, then =doom doctor=: five warnings, all accounted for above.
- Grammar availability from a fresh process, and =major-mode= on real files in a
  disposable daemon. Both recorded under Evidence.
- Not yet done, and needing the user's own session: confirming that
  =+childframe= on =vertico= and =syntax= behaves in a real graphical frame, that
  =format +onsave +lsp= does not fight the explicit =black= formatter set for
  Python, and that tree-sitter highlighting is an improvement rather than a
  surprise in Org and LaTeX buffers specifically.

** Rollback
Revert the =init.el= block in =config.org= and =doom sync= twice. Grammars are
inert data: deleting =~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/tree-sitter/= returns every
=*-ts-mode= to its fallback with no other effect, and Doom's module handles the
missing-grammar case explicitly.

** Revisit when
- The language servers are in place (they now are), at which point =yaml +lsp= and
  =web +lsp= become worth adding.
- A migration from lsp-mode to eglot is genuinely on the table, which is the
  only thing that makes =+booster= meaningful.
- The =+tng= question is picked up as its own decision.
- =make= or =bibtex= tree-sitter grammars start mattering, at which point the
  symbol-name mismatch is worth chasing rather than noting.

* ADR-037: Adopt a subset of the Emacs 30.x/31.1 built-ins, and record the ones this config should not take
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-emacs-31-builtins
:STATUS:   Implemented and committed (8e35356). All seven settings read back live from a headless daemon with a clean startup log; two rendering questions still need a graphical frame — see Validation
:DATE:     2026-08-30
:END:

** Context
Emacs 31.1 was installed on 2026-08-26. This configuration predates it and was
written against the 29/30 feature set, so the 30.1, 30.2 and 31.1 releases
shipped a quantity of built-in functionality that nothing here uses. A read of
=/usr/local/share/emacs/31.1/etc/NEWS= and =NEWS.30= against =config.org= found
roughly twenty-five candidates with zero mentions.

The interesting half of this decision is not which ones to take. It is that most
of the attractive-sounding ones are second implementations of something Doom or
an installed package already owns, and taking them would violate
[[id:std-no-duplication][=std-no-duplication=]] while looking like modernization.
Those are recorded here explicitly so the question is answered once rather than
re-asked at every Emacs release.

** Decision drivers
- Every symbol must be confirmed to exist in *this* build before it is set.
  Version-gated NEWS entries and =boundp= are not the same claim.
- One implementation per axis. "It is built in now" is not by itself a reason to
  displace something that works.
- Prefer settings that need no keybinding. The TAB and leader-key surfaces here
  are already documented contracts.

** Candidates and decision
Adopted, in a new =config.org= section, "Emacs 31.1 built-ins":

| Symbol | Version | What it buys |
|--------+---------+--------------|
| =trusted-content= | 30.1 | Allowlist for evaluating embedded code without prompting; gates elisp Flymake and local variables |
| =kill-region-dwim= | 31.1 | =C-w= with no region kills the previous word instead of signalling |
| =eldoc-help-at-pt= | 31.1 | =help-echo= text (Flycheck overlays, LSP hovers) via ElDoc, no mouse needed |
| =split-window-preferred-direction= | 31.1 | Automatic splits follow the longer axis instead of always splitting vertically |
| =minibuffer-regexp-mode= | 31.1 | Highlights regexp metacharacters while typing one in the minibuffer |
| =find-function-mode= | 31.1 | Installs =C-x F= / =C-x V= / =C-x K= for find-function/variable/key |
| =hs-show-indicators=, =hs-display-lines-hidden= | 31.1 | Fold markers in the fringe and a hidden-line count |

=trusted-content= is scoped to two directories — this repository and =~/org= —
rather than =:all=. It is an allowlist of paths whose code Emacs will run
without asking, and it exists because of CVE-2024-53920, so the narrow scope is
the point. It is set here at all because [[id:adr-literate-config][ADR-035]] made
=config.org= the configuration itself, re-tangling on every save.

** Evidence
Every adopted symbol was checked with =boundp=/=fboundp= in this build before
being written, several after =require='ing the library that defines them
(=hideshow=, =project=, =xref=, =dired=). After tangling, a disposable headless
daemon returned:

#+begin_example
(:trusted ("/home/james/.config/doom/" "/home/james/org/") :kill emacs-word
 :eldoc t :split longest :minire t :findfn t :hs (t t))
#+end_example

with no errors or warnings during daemon startup.

** Rejected alternatives
Each of these was a real candidate, and each is rejected for a specific reason
rather than on taste:

- =global-completion-preview-mode= (30.1) — a third engine on the in-buffer
  completion axis, alongside Corfu and Copilot. Directly against
  [[id:adr-corfu-migration][ADR-021]] and =std-no-duplication=.
- =mode-line-collapse-minor-modes= (31.1) — pointless here. Doom sets
  =doom-modeline-minor-modes nil= in =modules/ui/modeline/config.el:20=, so minor
  modes are already hidden, and doom-modeline builds its own segments rather
  than consuming the vanilla ones this option affects.
- =vc-auto-revert-mode= (31.1) — Doom ships =doom-auto-revert-mode=
  (=lisp/doom-emacs.el:1391=), described in its own docstring as "a more
  performant alternative to =global-auto-revert-mode=", hooked to
  =doom-first-file-hook=. Taking the built-in would mean two revert systems.
- =project-mode-line=, =project-list-exclude=, =project-prune-zombie-projects=
  (30.1/31.1) — projectile is the primary project system here and doom-modeline
  already displays the project. =project.el= appears once in =config.org=, for a
  single =project-switch-commands= entry.
- =completion-eager-display=, =minibuffer-visible-completions= (31.1) — Vertico
  owns the minibuffer axis.
- =delete-selection-local-mode= (31.1) — Evil owns selection behavior.
- =package-autosuggest-mode= (31.1) — assumes =package.el=. This config is
  straight-only, and =std-doom-first= forbids a second package manager.
- =etags-regen-mode= (30.1) — lsp-mode and dumb-jump cover it.
- =global-visual-wrap-prefix-mode= (30.1), built-in =which-key=, built-in
  =editorconfig= — already provided by =:editor word-wrap=, Doom core, and
  =:tools editorconfig=. Nothing to do; recorded so the question is not re-asked.
- =hs-cycle= / =hs-toggle-all= bound to TAB — the obvious binding is the one key
  this config cannot spend. =docs/ai/keybindings.org= documents a live-verified
  TAB precedence chain. The *display* halves of the same NEWS entry are adopted
  above precisely because they need no key.
- =timeout= (31.1, throttle/debounce) — this was in the approved plan, aimed at
  the Vulpea backlinks updater, and is withdrawn on inspection.
  =my/vulpea-buffer--maybe-update= already returns immediately unless the
  backlinks window is visible, and re-renders only when the note ID actually
  changed. The one real cost is a =vulpea-db-query-by-file-path= call per window
  change while the panel is open, and no slowness has been observed or measured.
  Debouncing it now would be optimizing a problem nobody has reported.
- =system-taskbar-mode= (31.1) — deferred to the "notifications and desktop
  integration" future phase in =docs/roadmap.org=, not pulled forward.
- =native-comp-async-on-battery-power= — not bound in this build.

** Consequences
- Seven new settings, no new packages, no new keybindings, and no change to any
  documented key contract.
- =trusted-content= means Emacs will evaluate file-local variables and run elisp
  Flymake inside =~/.config/doom/= and =~/org/= without prompting. That is a
  deliberate, scoped reduction in prompting, and it is the one entry in this ADR
  with a security dimension. Anything placed in either tree is trusted code.
- A list of built-ins this config should *not* adopt now exists, with reasons,
  which is the part most likely to save work at the next Emacs release.

** Validation
Recorded under Evidence: symbol existence checks before writing, and a live
readback of all seven values from a headless daemon after tangling, with a clean
startup log. Not yet verified in the user's own session: that =hs-show-indicators=
renders acceptably in the fringe alongside =:ui vc-gutter +pretty=, and that
=find-function-mode='s =C-x= bindings do not collide with anything Evil or Doom
puts there.

** Rollback
Delete the "Emacs 31.1 built-ins" section from =config.org= and re-tangle. Every
entry is a =setq= or a global minor mode with no persistent state; nothing needs
undoing beyond the removal itself.

** Revisit when
- Emacs 32 ships, at which point the rejected list above is the place to start
  rather than re-reading NEWS from scratch.
- The completion axis changes — a move away from Corfu or Copilot would reopen
  =completion-preview-mode=.
- doom-modeline is dropped or replaced, which would reopen
  =mode-line-collapse-minor-modes= and =project-mode-line=.
- The Vulpea backlinks panel is actually observed to be slow, which is the
  condition for reconsidering =timeout=.
- =:editor fold= is reworked, which is the natural moment to reconsider whether
  =hs-cycle= deserves a key after all.

* ADR-038: Guard the credential state that cannot be tangled — startup detection plus an off-disk encrypted backup
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       adr-credential-state-guard
:STATUS:   Implemented; detection live-verified in batch, backup script not yet run end-to-end (needs the user's own terminal for two passphrase prompts)
:DATE:     2026-08-30
:END:

** Context
[[id:adr-literate-config][ADR-035]] gave the shell layer a regression guard by
making =~/.bashrc=, =~/.inputrc= and =~/.gitconfig= tangle targets of
=config.org=: state that lives only in =$HOME= silently reverts, state under
version control shows up as a diff.

On 2026-08-30, auditing the binary table in
[[file:inventory.org][inventory.org]] for Phase 7's closeout turned up an
absence: no =~/.password-store=, no GPG secret key, no =msmtp=, =oama=,
=~/.mbsyncrc= or =mu= index on this desktop. =:tools pass +auth= is enabled,
=config.org= calls =+pass-get-secret= for =api/anthropic= and =api/openai=, and
[[file:ai/providers.org][providers.org]] recorded both as "Live — verified" —
all false here. gptel's Anthropic and OpenAI backends did not work on this host
and nobody noticed, because the tool used daily, claude-code-ide, authenticates
through the Claude CLI's own login and never touches =pass=.

*The first reading of that absence was wrong, and the correction is part of this
ADR's context rather than a footnote to it.* It was recorded as state that had
*vanished* — the ADR-035 pattern recurring at larger scale. James then pointed
out that he had set the mail and credential stack up on his laptop. Checks on
this host settled it: every one of the 6775 files under =~/.mail/= is dated
2020, and =~/.bash_history= shows zero invocations of =oama=, =pass=, =msmtp= or
=gpg=. Nothing was lost here; it was never here. See
[[id:inv-homedir-state-loss][inventory.org]] for the full correction.

Two things survive that correction, and they are what this ADR rests on.

First, the exposure is real either way. This host has an empty store, a GPG key
generated on 2026-08-30 that exists nowhere else, and configuration that reads
credentials at startup. A key that is not re-issuable and a store that nothing
checks is the same risk whether it got that way by loss or by never having been
populated.

Second, the *scope* defect that produced the wrong diagnosis is itself an
argument for detection. This project's documentation described one host while
being read on another, and a broken credential path went unremarked for an
unknown period as a result. A startup check that names what is missing is
exactly the thing that would have made the true state obvious on day one,
without anyone having to hold two machines in their head.

ADR-035's answer cannot be reused here regardless. The =pass= store and the GPG
secret key must never be committed, so tangling them from =config.org= is not an
option at any price.

** Decision drivers
- The guard must not depend on anyone remembering to look. Every prior
  discovery of this class was accidental, which is the same as saying the
  project had no guard at all.
- A daily-cost guard will be disabled. Anything that fires a pinentry prompt on
  every Emacs startup would be removed within a week, leaving nothing.
- Detection and recovery are separate problems. Knowing the store is gone does
  not bring the keys back; a backup nobody is told to check does not tell you
  the store is gone.
- Secrets must not enter the repository, must not appear in command output, and
  must not pass through an agent — [[file:../AGENTS.md][AGENTS.md]] and
  =.claude/rules/security.md=.
- The recovery copy has to survive the failure that actually happened, which was
  loss of state under =$HOME= on =sdc=. A backup on the same disk fails the
  same way.

** Candidates
| Candidate | Fate | Reason |
|-----------+------+--------|
| Startup existence check + off-disk encrypted archive | Chosen | The two halves solve the two problems; neither has a recurring cost that would get it disabled |
| Startup check that decrypts each entry | Rejected | Catches a corrupt entry too, but fires a passphrase prompt at every startup — the guard would be removed, and then there is no guard |
| Backup only, no detection | Rejected | Nothing to trigger a restore. The store was missing for an unknown period precisely because nothing said so |
| Detection only, no backup | Rejected | Correctly reports a loss that is already unrecoverable. The API keys are re-issuable; the GPG key is not |
| Commit the store, encrypted, into this repo | Rejected | Puts secret material in git history permanently, where a later key compromise cannot be undone. Violates the security rules outright |
| Cron/timer-driven backup | Rejected | Needs two interactive passphrase prompts (the key's, then the archive's); no unattended job can satisfy them |
| Password manager migration (1Password, Bitwarden) | Rejected | Would re-open [[id:adr-terminal-layer-pass][ADR-033]] and the =:tools pass= integration for a problem that is backup discipline, not tooling |

** Decision
Two independent halves, both generated from =config.org=.

*Detection* — =my/pass-check-store= on =emacs-startup-hook=, in the "Credential
store guard" section. It checks that =$PASSWORD_STORE_DIR= (or
=~/.password-store=) exists and that =<entry>.gpg= exists for each of
=my/pass-expected-entries= — currently =api/anthropic=, =api/openai=,
=mail/oauth2-google-client= — and raises a delayed warning naming what is
missing. It checks for *files*, never decrypting, so it cannot prompt. A
present-but-corrupt entry therefore still slips through; that is a deliberate
ceiling, marked with a =ponytail:= comment, and the upgrade path is
decrypt-on-demand if it ever actually happens. The observed failure mode is
disappearance, not corruption.

*Recovery* — =~/.local/bin/pass-backup=, tangled from the Shell layer at mode
700. It copies the store, exports the secret key for the fingerprint in
=.gpg-id=, tars both and encrypts the archive with =gpg --symmetric
--cipher-algo AES256= into =/data/backups/pass/pass-<date>.tar.gpg=. =/data= is
=sda=; =$HOME= is =sdc=. It is run by hand after adding or rotating an entry,
and prints its own restore recipe.

The archive contains an exported secret key, so its only protection is the
symmetric passphrase. =/data= is mode 777, hence the mode-700 destination
directory — worth stating plainly rather than leaving implied.

** Evidence
- Detection, both branches, in batch Emacs against the tangled =config.el=:
  a nonexistent =PASSWORD_STORE_DIR= warns naming the directory; the real store
  warns naming all three entries, which is the true state on this host today —
  the store is initialized but empty, pending the USB transfer from the laptop
  (=PROJECT.org= §7.7).
- The host-scope correction above was itself established by measurement, not
  assertion: file dates under =~/.mail/=, command counts in =~/.bash_history=,
  and this repository's first commit date.
- =bash -n= clean on the tangled =pass-backup=; the two precondition branches
  exercised — missing store and a store with no =.gpg-id= — both exit 1 with
  the intended message.
- A full run blocks on pinentry under a non-interactive shell, which is the
  expected and intended behaviour, and is why it is not on a timer.
- =doom sync= clean (5.5s), =doom doctor= down to the one standing false
  "development build of Emacs (31.1)" warning.
- The disks are distinct: =lsblk= shows =/data= on =sda1= and =/= on =sdc5=.

** Consequences
- A missing store now announces itself at startup instead of surfacing as a
  silently broken gptel backend months later.
- =my/pass-expected-entries= is a list that has to be maintained. Adding a
  credential to =config.org= without adding it there gives a guard that passes
  while the thing it guards is missing — the same false-clean this ADR exists
  to prevent, one level up.
- Backup freshness depends on the user running the script. The guard warns that
  the store is *gone*, not that the archive is stale.
- The archive is a single-passphrase artifact on a world-writable mount. That is
  a real, accepted narrowing of the store's protection, taken because an
  unrecoverable GPG key is the worse outcome.
- =pass-backup= turns out to be the cross-machine transport as well as the
  backup: an encrypted archive is exactly what should cross a USB stick between
  the laptop and this host, since removable media is usually FAT32 and carries
  no permission bits. That was not the design intent, and it is worth noting
  precisely because it means the tool now has two callers' expectations on it.

** Rejected alternatives
Recorded in the Candidates table above, with the reason each was dropped. The
one worth restating: a decrypting startup check is strictly better at finding
problems and strictly worse at continuing to exist.

** Validation
Recorded under Evidence. Not yet verified: an actual =pass-backup= run to
completion, and a restore from its output into a scratch =$GNUPGHOME= — both
need the user's terminal for the passphrase prompts. Until a restore has been
exercised once, the recovery half is untested in the only way that counts.

** Rollback
Delete the "Credential store guard" section and the =~/.local/bin/pass-backup=
block from =config.org=, re-tangle, and =rm ~/.local/bin/pass-backup=. Neither
half writes persistent state into Emacs; existing archives under
=/data/backups/pass/= remain readable with =gpg --decrypt= regardless.

** Revisit when
- A restore is actually performed, which is the moment to record whether the
  recipe the script prints is correct and complete.
- The mail layer is restored (Phase 2), since =~/.msmtprc= and =~/.mbsyncrc=
  carry account addresses and credential references and need their own answer —
  possibly tangling, possibly this archive.
- A present-but-corrupt entry is ever observed, which is the trigger for the
  decrypt-on-demand upgrade named above.
- Any new credential is added to =config.org=, which is the moment
  =my/pass-expected-entries= must grow with it.
